# Independent analytical audit of the final multistage candidate

Project: gd-exact-profile-20260926. Auditor: max2, invocation 63.
Assignment: TASK.md, one invocation after ended baseline 62; latest
master_guidance 326ac724bafde92c. Consult OFF.

**Scope verdict: gap_found in the literal unrestricted strict collapsed-
comparison prose; no_gap_found_at_declared_scope for both core constructions
and their positive-tail growing-family claims.** The three-stage parameter
domain includes epsilon=0, where the new and collapsed values are both
zero. Strict domination and the factor ratio require epsilon>0; Section 10
gives an exact counterexample to the unrestricted strict reading. There is
also one noncritical printed algebra error, corrected explicitly in Section
5. Neither issue invalidates the positive-tail special-family payoffs,
the reference-channel separation, or the every-large-horizon witnesses.
This is an UNVERIFIED audit finding, not a verifier acceptance, a new fact,
or a solution of the original all-word problem.

Audited input: final finding b3a629e987978a22, superseding cbf8fd65600a4cea,
as frozen in input_candidate.md. All 551 lines were read. The frozen file
has 25685 bytes and SHA256
5ab2e70ab0d90c767edf3e7323ff42bd30c40b017642b37b8440835709f020a8.
The digest is evidence of text identity only. No author private memory
was accessed and no mathematical result was inferred from a digest.

The verified inputs consulted are fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290, statement and
maximal-prefix construction proof; fact_id b98bb807d514e80d, full statement
and proof; and fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf, statement and the Chebyshev/ellipse
proofs. All new reasoning below is one joint unverified audit, with no new
intermediate statement treated as an accepted premise. No fact_submit,
formal service, mathematical program, numerical evaluation, arithmetic
replay, executed trajectory/recurrence, optimizer, DP, installation,
consult, or subagent was used.

The proof labels fact1252, factb98, and factdfde below mean respectively
fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290, fact_id b98bb807d514e80d, and
fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf. These are the only accepted mathematical inputs.

## 1. Exact scope and local notation

For a nonnegative physical word h of length n, the project objective is
W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), with the original GD
updates x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Here the supremum allows every
finite dimension and legal convex L-smooth function, and the attained
examples below have the TRUE closest initial radius one and sharp L=1.
The original target concerns inf_h W_n(h) relative to the exact all-split
U_n^ZJ; a lower witness on selected words cannot settle that infimum.

The candidate's local parameters are M=M_mc>=2, B=B_mc>=2,
A=A_mc>=4B+4, and epsilon in [0,1]. The return law additionally requires
M epsilon>=2B^2. These symbols are not benchmark masses. Write
a=1-1/M and C=2B+1, and let e_1,e_2,e_3 be the Euclidean coordinate
vectors. Define

    nu=sqrt(2A-4),
    p=e_1/M,
    q=(-2e_1+nu e_2)/M,
    Q=||q||^2=2A/M^2,
    r=-q/2+sqrt((B/2-1/4)Q)e_3,
    R=||r||^2=BQ/2,       <q,r>=-Q/2.

The independent q and r coordinates will be useful; their first
coordinates are q_1=-2/M and r_1=1/M. For a finite nonnegative list
L_i of sum M-1, retain its actual number of entries, including zero
entries. The audited words are exactly

    h_three=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,1+M epsilon),
    h_return=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,CM+1,L_3,1+M epsilon).

No arbitrary word is asserted to admit either form. Each resulting
completed function may depend on the full known word, but is fixed
before the actual run and is the same function at every query.

The three analytical attack groups were: (i) actual chronology and
every remote anchor, including parameter boundaries and the old first-r
query; (ii) every reference cut, complex-lens scope, benchmark direction
and padded horizon; (iii) true-radius and collapsed-policy scope and
the inherited-hierarchy diagnostic. No decisive counterexample to the
final stated conclusions was found. This absence alone is not the
reason for the verdict: the critical claims are rederived below.

## 2. Static old function and every reached query

Let D=conv{0,p,q,r} and

    F_old(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

The maximum is attained uniquely because D is compact and the objective
is strictly concave; the maximizer is P_D(x). The projection normal
condition says, for u=x-g,

    <u,b-g><=0 for every b in D.

It also says g maximizes the support J(u)=max(0,<p,u>,<q,u>,<r,u>).
Thus x=u+g, delta=J(u)=<g,u>, and
F_old(x)=delta+||g||^2/2. Conversely, any displayed pair with g in D
and this support condition is the UNIQUE projection pair. Tied support
planes do not make the envelope gradient ambiguous.

For any two old pairs, direct cancellation in the signed original
residual of fact1252 gives

    Q_xy=F_old(x)-F_old(y)+<g_x,y-x>+||g_x-g_y||^2/2
        =<u_y,g_x-g_y><=0.

This works for every ordered pair, remote pairs, duplicate positions,
and the zero optimum. It uses the actual support condition, not a
consecutive-pair surrogate. Projection firm nonexpansiveness supplies
global smoothness one; differentiability and convexity also follow
from this attained conjugate maximum. The slopes are independent,
so D has interior, where P_D(x)=x and F_old has Hessian I. Its least
global smoothness is therefore exactly one.

Because 0 is a vertex of D, F_old>=0 and its complete minimum set is

    {x:<p,x><=0, <q,x><=0, <r,x><=0}.

Indeed this condition makes every objective term nonpositive. If any
of the three inner products is positive, a sufficiently small positive
multiple of that vertex belongs to D and makes the objective positive.
The p inequality puts all minima in x_1<=0; origin is the unique
closest one to e_1 and the true distance is one.

The following table derives the actual static chronology. Its variable
t=1+s, where s is the ordinary mass already spent in the current list;
therefore 1<=t<=M. The right endpoint t=M is also the selected following
bridge query. Nonnegativity of every entry ensures every ordinary
partial mass lies in this interval.

| Stage | Proposed proximal point u | Current support height | Exhaustive other-plane comparisons |
|---|---|---|---|
| p | (1-t/M)e_1 | (1-t/M)/M | q height is minus twice this height; r height equals this height; zero is below it |
| q | X-tq, X=-Ae_1 | (M-t)Q | p height=(-A+2t/M)/M<0; r height=-(M-t)Q/2<=0; zero is below q |
| r | X_R-tr, X_R=y-BMq | (M-t)R | q height=-BMQ+tQ/2<0; p height=(2-A+2B-t/M)/M<0; zero is below r |
| returned q | X_D-tq, X_D=z-CMr | (M-t)Q | r height=-CMR+tQ/2<0; p height=(-A+2t/M)/M<0; zero is below q |

For the first row, the p/r tie holds at EVERY p-stage query. Since p
is an active support subgradient and x=u+p, projection uniqueness
proves the actual gradient is p. It is not necessary to break that tie.
All strict comparisons in the other rows follow from B>=2 and
A>=4B+4, including t=M. At zero height the zero plane may tie with
other planes, but the displayed projection remains unique.

The endpoints and bridges follow by literal GD subtraction:

    after L_0: x=p, u=0;
    p-(AM+1)p = X=-Ae_1;
    y=X-Mq=(2-A,-nu,0),    <q,y>=<r,y>=0;
    (y+q)-(BM+1)q=X_R=y-BMq;
    z=X_R-Mr=y-BMq-Mr;
    <r,z>=0, <q,z>=M(1/2-B)Q, z_1=1-A+2B;
    (z+r)-(CM+1)r=X_D=z-CMr;
    w=X_D-Mq=z-CMr-Mq;
    <q,w>=0, <r,w>=(MQ/2)(1-CB), w_1=2-A.

For h_three the last update is from z+r and reaches z-M epsilon r.
For h_return it is from w+q and reaches w-M epsilon q. Every bridge
is an actual physical query and update. Lists were not collapsed into
oracle calls. A zero entry repeats the same jet and leaves the endpoint
identities unchanged.

The old three-law terminal has p and q supports strictly negative and
r support=-M epsilon R<=0. The old return-law terminal has p support
strictly negative, q support=-M epsilon Q<0, and r support
(MQ/2)(1-CB+epsilon)<0. Thus BOTH old terminal values are zero.
Only the unqueried terminal completion creates the stated new payoff.

## 3. Complete conjugate-anchor lists and all prefix rows

For a prefix jet x=u+g, f=delta+||g||^2/2, its conjugate lower
quadratic from fact1252 simplifies, with no dropped constant, to

    H_jet(v)=||v||^2/2+<u,v>-delta.

Within one constant-gradient stage both u and delta are affine in
the ordinary partial mass. Thus each intervening affine term is a
convex combination of the first-query and endpoint affine terms;
it cannot enlarge their maximum. BOTH endpoint jets are physical
prefix queries. This proves exact equality of the maximum over every
physical prefix jet with the endpoint maximum, rather than replacing
the interpolation problem by an endpoint relaxation.

Here are all six/eight affine terms, including the original optimum:

| Anchor u | Offset delta | Laws retaining it |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0 | both; p-stage bridge and optimum give the same affine term |
| t_P=a e_1 | a/M | both; initial query |
| y | 0 | both; q-stage ending bridge query |
| t_Q=y+(M-1)q | (M-1)Q | both; FIRST q query |
| z | 0 | both; r-stage ending bridge query |
| t_R=z+(M-1)r | (M-1)R | both; FIRST r query |
| w | 0 | return; returned-q ending query |
| t_D=w+(M-1)q | (M-1)Q | return; FIRST returned-q query |

The distinct affine-anchor counts are six and eight. In particular the
old FIRST r query is retained after returning to q; its lower quadratic
does not become redundant merely because the endpoint z is retained.

Define H_three as ||v||^2/2 plus the maximum of the first six affine
terms, H_return using all eight, and F_three=H_three^*,
F_return=H_return^*. All ordered old prefix inequalities and the
optimum jet were proved in Section 2. The maximal-prefix construction
of accepted fact1252 therefore applies exactly, preserving EVERY old
prefix position, gradient and value, including repeated queries. Its
proof gives finite convex C1 globally 1-smooth conjugates. Fix these
functions before running their words. Induction on the physical indices
then gives the same prefix updates; no online function change is used.

The new terminal gradients are not assumed equal to the old gradients.
At the terminal, x=v+conv(active anchor slopes) is the precise
subgradient optimality condition. It is checked separately below.
Since each completed H is 1-strongly convex, that condition determines
the unique terminal gradient. Incoming AND outgoing terminal rows hold
for the resulting actual function; fact1252 is not being used to
delete a queried-prefix comparison.

## 4. Three-stage terminal, including every boundary

Let L=B+a, E=t_Q-z=M L q+Mr, and

    D_3=||E||^2=2A(L^2-L+B/2),
    v_0=-M epsilon r,
    b_3=<E,v_0>-(M-1)Q=Aa(epsilon-2/M),
    theta_3=(b_3)_+/D_3,    v_3=v_0-theta_3 E.

For a in [1/2,1),
L^2-L+B/2=B^2+B(2a-1/2)+a^2-a>=B^2+B/2-1/4>=B^2.
Thus D_3>=2AB^2 and 0<=theta_3<=epsilon/(2B^2)<=1/8.
This pays the convex-combination range and every denominator sign.

All six affine comparisons can be written explicitly:

* z has value -theta_3<z,E>, with
  <z,E>=2A(1/2-B)L<0. This value is nonnegative, and is
  positive when theta_3>0.
* t_Q minus z has affine value b_3-theta_3 D_3, which is zero
  when b_3>=0 and nonpositive otherwise.
* y and 0 have value zero because y is orthogonal to q and r.
* t_R minus z has value (M-1)(<r,v_3>-R). Here
  <r,v_3>=(MQ/2)(-B epsilon+a theta_3)<0 for epsilon>0.
  At epsilon=0 this difference is instead -(M-1)R<0.
* t_P minus z has value <t_P-z,v_3>-a/M. Directly,
  <t_P-z,v_0>=-a epsilon and
  <t_P-z,E>=2L[A(B-1/2)-a]+a>0, so this difference is negative.

If epsilon<2/M, theta_3=0 and z is active, along with y and 0;
if epsilon=2/M, t_Q also ties but z alone may still be selected.
If epsilon>2/M, z and t_Q are active and theta_3 lies strictly
between zero and one. In every case,

    x_n=z+v_0=v_3+z+theta_3 E in partial H_three(v_3).

At epsilon=0 the terminal gradient and value are zero and z is a
minimum. This covers M=2, epsilon=1 as well, where epsilon=2/M;
there is no hidden positive-theta assumption at that endpoint.

The active z term gives the exact conjugate value by completing a
square:

    V_three=2F_three(x_n)
           =||v_0||^2-(b_3)_+^2/D_3
           =AB epsilon^2-(Aa(epsilon-2/M)_+)^2/D_3.

Since the loss is at most A epsilon^2/(2B^2),

    AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))<=V_three<=AB epsilon^2.

The formula is continuous across the active-set boundary; it was
derived from the full six-anchor maximum, not a terminal ansatz.

## 5. Return terminal and the retained old first-r query

The feasible return parameter set itself implies M>=2B^2>=8 and
epsilon>0. Epsilon=0 and small M outside this set are not conclusions
of the return theorem. Set

    d=C+a=2B+2-1/M,
    E_R=t_R-w=Mq+Md r,
    Dhat_R=1-d+(B/2)d^2,
    D_R=||E_R||^2=2A Dhat_R,
    v_0=-M epsilon q,
    b_R=<E_R,v_0>-(M-1)R
       =2A[(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M)],
    theta_R=b_R/D_R,    v_R=v_0-theta_R E_R.

The derivative of 1-d+Bd^2/2 is Bd-1>0 in this range and
2B+1<d<2B+2<=3B. The exact value at the lower comparison point is

    1-(2B+1)+(B/2)(2B+1)^2
        =2B^3+2B^2-3B/2.

**Printed error in the audited source:** its next-to-last term has an
extra final '-1'. That equality is false. Removing the '-1' corrects
the arithmetic and still gives Dhat_R>=2B^3, since 2B^2-3B/2>0
for B>=2. The source's downstream bounds do not rely on that extra
subtracted one. This is an explicit repair of one proof line, with no
weakening of the declared theorem.

The upper bound Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3 follows by d<=3B and 1-d<0.
Write eta=(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M). The upper eta<=B epsilon
is immediate. For the lower eta>=B epsilon/2 it suffices that
B M epsilon>=epsilon+Ba. But B M epsilon>=2B^3>=B+1>=Ba+epsilon.
Hence

    2B^3<=Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3,
    epsilon/(9B^2)<=theta_R<=epsilon/(2B^2)<=1/8,

with b_R>0. In particular the active combination is admissible,
including the boundary M epsilon=2B^2 and epsilon=1 when feasible.

The complete eight-anchor screen is as follows.

1. t_R and w tie because their affine difference is
   b_R-theta_R D_R=0. The w value is positive:
   <w,v_0>=0 and <w,E_R>=A d(1-CB)<0.
2. For the old endpoint z, direct substitution, without omitting t_R,
   gives

       <r,v_R>/(Q/2)
          =[M epsilon d(B-1/2)+(Ba/2)(Bd-1)]/Dhat_R.

   Its first numerator term is at least 2B^2 d(B-1/2).
   Since B-1/2>=3B/4 and d<=3B, this is at least B^2 d^2/2.
   Also B Dhat_R<=B^2 d^2/2. The second numerator term is
   strictly positive, so <r,v_R>>R throughout the feasible scope.
   Using the t_R/w tie, the z-minus-w value equals
   (M-1)(R-<r,v_R>)<0. Thus z is inactive even on the stated
   return parameter boundary; this is not presumed from chronology.
3. <q,v_R>=MQ[-epsilon+theta_R(B-1/(2M))]<0, because
   theta_R B<=epsilon/(2B)<epsilon. Thus t_D is below w.
   The t_Q affine value is (M-1)(<q,v_R>-Q)<0.
4. y and 0 have value zero, below the positive w value.
5. For E_P=t_P-w, the independent first-coordinate calculation is

       <E_P,v_0>=2a epsilon,
       <E_P,E_R>=a(d-2)+A d(CB-1)>=4AB^3.

   Indeed d>=2B+1 and CB-1=2B^2+B-1>=2B^2. Consequently
   <E_P,v_R><=2a epsilon-(4AB/9)epsilon<0, since
   A>=4B+4 and B>=2 imply 4AB/9>2. Subtracting delta_P
   makes the t_P-minus-w comparison still more negative.

These cover all eight terms with the original offsets. Since w and
t_R are active and 0<theta_R<1,

    x_n=w+v_0=v_R+w+theta_R E_R in partial H_return(v_R).

The exact value and loss follow from the active w term:

    V_return=2F_return(x_n)=2A epsilon^2-b_R^2/D_R,
    2A epsilon^2(1-1/(2B))<=V_return<=2A epsilon^2,
    V_return>=3A epsilon^2/2.

For the lower value bound, b_R<=2AB epsilon and D_R>=4AB^3
give loss<=A epsilon^2/B. This completes the full feasible parameter
screen, including the epsilon and cap-equality boundaries.

The source's M=1024 shortcut counterexample also checks exactly.
For B=2,A=12,epsilon=1/2,C=5, all stated guards hold. If only w,z
were used for the terminal calculation, its proposed mixture coefficient
would be

    theta_bad=(B-1/2)epsilon/(1-C+BC^2/2)=1/28,
    v_bad=-M epsilon q-theta_bad M(q+C r).

Then

    <r,v_bad>/R=(M/B)[epsilon+theta_bad(1-CB)]
              =5M/56=640/7>1.

The t_R affine term exceeds the z term by
(M-1)(<r,v_bad>-R)>0. This is a real retained-prefix violation of
the endpoint-only calculation, not a tolerance issue. The final
eight-anchor law pays it by making t_R active. It is therefore a
counterexample to the rejected shortcut, not to the final return law.

## 6. Complete new minimum sets, attainment and sharp normalization

All positive offsets in the anchor table are strictly positive, since
M>=2, a>0, Q>0 and R>0. At v=0 exactly the zero-offset anchors
are active. The subdifferential of a finite maximum of affine functions
is the convex hull of its active slopes: every such convex combination
is a supporting slope; a slope outside that hull is separated from it
by a direction and cannot satisfy the supporting inequality along that
direction. Therefore

    partial H_three(0)=conv{0,y,z},
    partial H_return(0)=conv{0,y,z,w}.

Each H(0)=0, so its conjugate F>=0. Optimality for the strictly
convex conjugate maximization says F(x)=0 precisely when its maximizer
is v=0, equivalently x in partial H(0). These are the COMPLETE
minimum sets, and the minimum value is attained and is zero.

The first coordinates of y,z,w are respectively 2-A, 1-A+2B,
2-A, all strictly negative. Thus each completed minimum hull lies
in x_1<=0 and contains origin. For any minimum x,
||e_1-x||^2=1-2x_1+||x||^2>=1, with equality only at x=0.
The unique closest minimum is origin and the TRUE radius is exactly
one. Completion shrinks the old minimum cone to these bounded hulls;
radius preservation has been proved from the new hulls, not assumed.

For 0<t<1/M sufficiently small, at v=t e_1 every nonzero
zero-offset anchor has strictly negative affine value, and each
positive-offset anchor has strictly negative affine value too. The
initial one requires t_P,1 t<delta_P, which is exactly t<1/M.
There is an open ball around such v on which the zero affine term
alone wins, so H(v)=||v||^2/2 there. Optimality gives F(x)=||x||^2/2
on that same open ball, with Hessian I. Since global smoothness one
was already supplied by fact1252, it is SHARP. There is no hidden
L or distance renormalization. The exact terminal values in Sections
4 and 5 are thus actual attained W=2R-normalized payoffs in R^3.

## 7. Exact growing parameters and physical counts

For each integer N>=2^60 the candidate chooses

    M=2N(N+1)+1,   A=M^(1/12),   B=M^(1/120),
    epsilon=M^(-1/3),   T_Cheb=(2N+1)^2=2M-1.

M>2^121 implies B>2; A=B^10>=4B+4 for B>=2. Also
M epsilon=M^(2/3)>=2M^(1/60)=2B^2 in this range. Thus these
are inside the previously screened parameter sets, with epsilon>0.
Accepted factdfde gives each Chi_N mass 2N(N+1)/3. Three copies
have mass M-1 and length 3N. The initial list uses three increasing
copies, every later list three decreasing copies. The physical lengths,
including every bridge, are therefore n_three=9N+3 and n_return=12N+4.
The guards in Section 2 cover every entry without executing the lists.

The exact value bounds give

    V_three>=AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))>=(7/8)M^(-23/40),
    V_return>=3A epsilon^2/2=(3/2)M^(-7/12).

Since B(1-1/(2B^3))>=15/8>3/2, BOTH payoffs obey the common
lower bound V>=(3/2)M^(-7/12). This uses the B factor in V_three,
not only a comparison of power exponents.

## 8. Every original cut and the quadratic/lens channels

With P_j=sum_(i<j)h_i and T_j=sum_(i>j)h_i at the ORIGINAL
physical index, accepted factb98 defines

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2sum h),
           max_j (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]}.

Write increasing Chi_N entries as csc^2(k pi/(2N+1)),
k=N,N-1,...,1. Concavity of sine makes consecutive increasing
ratios at most (k/(k-1))^2<=4. The first entry is <=4/3 for
N>=2, because N pi/(2N+1)>=2pi/5>pi/3. Thus an increasing-copy
entry h is <=9(1+preceding mass in that copy); reversing the copy
gives h<=9(1+following mass in that copy), including the small last
entry. Also sin(pi/(2N+1))>=2/(2N+1) gives max Chi_N<=T_Cheb/4<M.
Within-copy mass remains a lower bound for the corresponding full mass.

The following cut partition is complete. All denominators are at the
physical indices; no schedule compression or mass regrouping is used.

| Physical cut | Bound on its K term | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| ordinary entry in L_0 | 41/(AM) | squared prefix ratio<=81; following mass includes AM+1 |
| any later ordinary entry | 9/(A^2 M) | 1+P_j>=AM; h<=M and h<=9(1+T_j) give h^2/(1+2T_j)<=9M |
| AM+1 | A^2/(2BM) | 1+P_j=M; following mass includes BM+1 |
| BM+1 | B^2/(A^2 M) | 1+P_j>=AM; following mass>=M |
| CM+1, return word | C^2/(A^2 M)<=(25/4)B^2/(A^2 M) | same mass guards; C<=5B/2 |
| final 1+M epsilon | epsilon^2/A^2 | preceding mass>=AM; following mass is zero |
| global Huber term | 1/(2AM) | total mass>=AM |

The last ordinary cut is licensed by DECREASING suffix order. An
increasing final list would not justify this table. After substituting
the parameter powers, the slow possible terms include

    A^2/(2BM)=(1/2)M^(-101/120),
    epsilon^2/A^2=M^(-5/6).

All other displayed terms decay faster than M^(-5/6), with constants
at most 41. Since K is a maximum, BOTH complete words satisfy
K_n(h)<=41M^(-5/6).

Every convex quadratic in any finite dimension diagonalizes, and its
normalized error is a convex combination of t product_j(1-h_j t)^2
for 0<=t<=1. Zero eigenvalues contribute no objective gap; choosing a
farther minimizer only increases the denominator. Consequently the
entire quadratic channel is bounded by
Q_n(h)=max_(0<=t<=1)t product_j(1-h_j t)^2.

Let P_N denote the polynomial of one Chi_N copy from factdfde. On
[0,1], |P_N(t)|^2<=min(1,1/(T_Cheb t)). The second bound uses
|Cheb_(2N+1)(sqrt(t))|<=1. For the first, set sqrt(t)=sin(phi):
the odd Chebyshev identity reduces to
sin((2N+1)phi)/((2N+1)sin(phi)). The finite unit-complex-number
telescoping identity gives |sin(k phi)|<=k|sin(phi)|. At t=0 the
polynomial value is one.

There are nine Chebyshev copies for h_three, twelve for h_return.
With u=T_Cheb t, the actual bridge coefficients divided by T_Cheb
are <=A,B,1 (three), and <=A,B,C,1 (return). Indeed
AM+1<=A(2M-1) because A(M-1)>=1, and
1+M epsilon<=2M-1 because M>=2, epsilon<=1. The complete
quadratic expressions are therefore bounded by

    T_Cheb^(-1)u(1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+u)^2 min(1,u^(-9)),
    T_Cheb^(-1)u(1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+Cu)^2(1+u)^2
                         min(1,u^(-12)).

For u<=1 use 1+c u<=2c; for u>=1 use 1+c u<=2c u.
The latter products retain u^(-2) and u^(-3), respectively. The
objective at u=0 is zero. T_Cheb>=M and C<=5B/2 give

    Q_n(h_three)<=64 A^2 B^2/T_Cheb<=64M^(-49/60),
    Q_n(h_return)<=256 A^2 B^2 C^2/T_Cheb<=1600M^(-4/5).

This bounds all convex quadratic witnesses, not general scalar nonlinear
or higher-dimensional smooth convex functions.

For the complex comparison the EXACT named universal lens is

    Lens_n={z=alpha+i beta:
            alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2))}.

Accepted factdfde puts it inside the full cycle-legal region. If
n>=N then Lens_n is also inside Lens_N, because cot(pi/(n+2))
increases with n. The accepted ellipse proof for an N-copy gives
|z||P_N(z)|^2<=cosh^2(2)/T_Cheb on Lens_N. When
T_Cheb|z|<=1, the direct product gives instead

    |P_N(z)|<=exp(sum Chi_N |z|)<=exp(1/6)<=cosh(2),

since sum Chi_N=(T_Cheb-1)/6. Combining the two regimes, with
Kc=cosh(2), yields

    |P_N(z)|^2<=Kc^2 min(1,1/(T_Cheb|z|)).

The accepted maximal complex transcript obeys
Gap_h(z)<=(2alpha-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2<=2|z||Poly_n(z)|^2.
Repeat the preceding product estimate with u=T_Cheb|z| to obtain

    sup_(Lens_n) Gap_h_three <=128Kc^18M^(-49/60),
    sup_(Lens_n) Gap_h_return<=3200Kc^24M^(-4/5).

If the channel is augmented by the canonical Huber maximum V_lens,
its extra term is covered in the K table. These bounds assert NOTHING
about sup_(D_h) Gap_h over the larger schedule-dependent complex
region, or about all scalar functions.

## 9. Original all-split benchmark and actual padded horizons

Here C_n,B_n are benchmark sequences, distinct from candidate scalars
C,B. Use EXACTLY the accepted all-split recurrences

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Accepted factb98 gives B_n<=1+4n^rho, rho=log_2(3). This UPPER
bound is the direction needed to prove reference channels small
relative to U. Since rho<8/5 by 3^5<2^8, M>=2N^2 and actual
n<=16N show

    B_n max(K_n,Q_n,sup_(Lens_n) Gap_h)->0.

The slowest reference decay is M^(-4/5)=O(N^(-8/5)), whereas
B_n=O(N^rho). Fixed cosh(2) constants do not affect the limit.
Thus all three named channels are o(U_n^ZJ) on each selected family
at its actual horizon.

To prove V>U a LOWER bound on B_n is necessary. The all-split
recurrences directly give C_n>=Omega(C_(n-1),1)>C_(n-1), and
balanced splits give C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j for lambda=1+sqrt(2).
Selecting the split j=n-1 in B_n gives
B_n>=G(C_(n-1),1)>=2C_(n-1)-1. For d_N=floor(log_2 N),
both physical families obey n-1>=2^(d_N+3)-1, and consequently
B_n>=lambda^(d_N+3). Meanwhile M<=5N^2<20*4^(d_N). Hence
the common attained payoff satisfies

    B_n V >= [3lambda^3/(2*20^(7/12))]
                       (lambda/2^(7/6))^(d_N) >1.

This is an exact analytic estimate. Explicitly, lambda>12/5,
2^(7/6)<9/4 (equivalently 2^19<3^12), and 20^(7/12)<8
(use 5^7<2^21). Thus the prefactor is >324/125>2 and the
base is >16/15>1. The payoff exceeds U at EVERY claimed
N>=2^60 and the ratio diverges. No fitted profile constant, assumed
benchmark asymptotic, balanced-only defining recurrence, or DP was used.

For an arbitrary sufficiently large integer horizon n, use

    N=floor((n-3)/9),  k=n-(9N+3) in {0,...,8},
    or N=floor((n-4)/12), k=n-(12N+4) in {0,...,11}.

Insert k zeros at the VERY BEGINNING of the appropriate known word.
They repeat the already retained initial jet, so the exact full-prefix
maximum H is unchanged. The same static F has the same terminal value,
sharp smoothness and closest radius. The terminal remains unqueried.

For K, every new zero cut has numerator zero and every old cut's
preceding/following MASS is unchanged. Quadratic products acquire only
factors one. Lens_n shrinks. In the benchmark estimates use this
actual padded n, which still satisfies n<=16N for these large N
and has n-1 at least the unpadded value. Explicit sufficient start
horizons are 9*2^60+3 and 12*2^60+4. Thus the argument supplies
one selected attained word for each large n; it does not quantify over
every length-n word.

## 10. Precisely defined collapsed policy and its zero-tail boundary

This comparison retains the candidate's p=e_1/M, initial point e_1,
first ordinary list and selected bridge AM+1. It treats ALL remaining
steps before the final bridge as one ordinary block, whose total is
M_2-1. Directly counting the original masses gives

    M_2=(B+2)M          for h_three,
    M_2=(B+C+3)M=(3B+4)M  for h_return.

Its specified static second slope is

    q_2=(-2e_1+sqrt(2A-4)e_2)/M_2,
    ||q_2||^2=2A/M_2^2.

For the static envelope over conv{0,p,q_2}, first-stage p is
supported. After the first bridge, X=-Ae_1. At ordinary partial
mass s with t=1+s in [1,M_2],

    <q_2,X-tq_2>=(M_2-t)||q_2||^2>=0,
    <p,X-tq_2>=(-A+2t/M_2)/M<0.

Thus every physical query of THIS collapsed policy is legal, including
the old BM+1 and CM+1 steps now acting as individual ordinary entries.
Let y_2=X-M_2 q_2. The last queried jet is x=y_2+q_2,
g=q_2, f=||q_2||^2/2. For EVERY legal smooth-convex completion
of THIS entire prefix with its retained zero optimum, the descent
inequality at that jet gives

    f(y_2)<=f(y_2+q_2)-||q_2||^2/2=0.

Since the retained optimum has value zero, y_2 is forced to be a
minimum. The final physical step 1+M epsilon reaches
y_2-M epsilon q_2, and smoothness about this forced minimum gives

    2f(x_n)<=2A(M epsilon/M_2)^2.

The true radius for every such completion is also one, rather than
an assumed inherited denominator. The retained initial jet is
g_0=p and f_0=1/M-1/(2M^2). For any new minimizer x_star of value
zero, the original interpolation necessity from fact1252 gives

    Q_(0,star)=f_0+<p,x_star-e_1>+||p||^2/2
             =x_star,1/M<=0.

So all new minima have first coordinate <=0, and the retained origin
is the unique closest one to e_1. This proves the bound for every
completion of that precise prefix with TRUE radius one.

When epsilon>0, the collapsed upper is positive and division is
legitimate. The three-stage witness-to-upper ratio is at least

    [B(B+2)^2/2](1-1/(2B^3)),

and the return witness-to-upper ratio is at least

    (3/4)(3B+4)^2.

Both factors exceed one and diverge on the chosen growing words,
where epsilon=M^(-1/3)>0. In the return parameter range epsilon>0
is automatic. Thus the substantive advertised growing-family comparison
holds, with exactly the stated collapsed policy as the comparison class.

**Literal boundary gap:** source Section 7 says its three-stage value
"exceeds that upper by a factor" without explicitly restricting epsilon
to be positive. The full three-stage parameter domain permits zero.
An exact boundary example is

    M=2, B=2, A=12, epsilon=0,
    L_0=L_1=L_2=(1),
    h_three=(1,25,1,5,1,1).

All original three-stage hypotheses hold. The completed terminal is
z, its gradient is zero, and V_three=0. The collapsed M_2=8 policy
has final step one and reaches its forced minimum y_2; the maximum
terminal value among all its legal retained-prefix completions is also
zero. The printed factor specializes to 15, but strict inequality
V_three>collapsed_upper is false, and the ratio 0/0 is undefined.
The weak multiplicative inequality V_three>=15*collapsed_upper
does hold. The exact repair is therefore to state the weak inequality
on epsilon in [0,1], equality at epsilon=0, and strict ratio domination
only for epsilon>0. If source Section 7 was intended only for Section
6's growing words, say so explicitly; that intended comparison passes.
This audit does not silently reinterpret an unrestricted strict sentence
as the weaker boundary-valid inequality.

No comparison here covers every possible two-block partition, alternative
second slope, earlier return/spiral adversary, or all scalar nonlinear
functions. None of those classes is upper-bounded by the forced-minimum
argument for this one retained prefix.

## 11. Inherited-hierarchy diagnostic with its exact hypotheses

Use the same enclosing integer m>=7 throughout, and define

    beta_h=1-1/(4m),
    c_h=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/(4m)),
    a_h=beta_h/c_h,
    H_h,0=(),
    H_h,(j+1)=(H_h,j,1+a_h c_h^j,H_h,j).

Here S_h,j=sum H_h,j and the chosen first-occurrence bridge is
b_h,j=1+a_h c_h^j. These are the literal definitions used in the
candidate's diagnostic, not parameters recomputed with local j.
For m>=7, 2<c_h<3. Also
c_h-2>sqrt(2)-1=1/(1+sqrt(2))>a_h>0. Consequently
v_h=a_h/(c_h-2) belongs to (0,1).

For M_j=1+S_h,j the recursion is M_0=1,
M_(j+1)=2M_j+a_h c_h^j. Its symbolic solution is

    M_j=v_h c_h^j+(1-v_h)2^j.

Substitution verifies both the initial condition and recurrence since
v_h(c_h-2)=a_h; no recurrence was executed. The attempted first-
occurrence three-cap parameter would be

    A_j=(b_h,j-1)/M_j=a_h c_h^j/M_j<c_h-2<1.

The inequality is strict because (1-v_h)2^j>0. It also covers
j=0, for which A_0=a_h<1. Therefore that exact instantiation cannot
satisfy the sufficient A>=4B+4 hypothesis of the audited policy.
This is a failure to license a sufficient construction at these cuts.
It proves neither a W upper bound nor an impossibility theorem for
other multistage laws, other partitions, or unscreened witnesses.

## 12. Remaining gaps and the next useful interface

The positive core mathematical scope can be stated without either
write-up repair: there are two specified static three-dimensional
functions on the exact equal-mass block words above; their terminal
values, complete minimum sets, sharp smoothness and true radii are
attained as proved jointly here. On the two specified positive-tail
Chebyshev families and their LEADING-zero padding, their payoffs
exceed the exact original U at every sufficiently large actual horizon,
while K, all convex quadratics, and the EXACT universal-lens channel
are o(U). These conclusions survive the independent audit. The stronger
literal all-epsilon strict collapsed sentence does not.

The original all-word problem still has the following unpaid interfaces:

* An arbitrary nonnegative word need not admit three/four equal incoming
  masses M-1, the required bridge ratios A>=4B+4 and B>=2, or
  the fixed return ratio C=2B+1.
* The return calculation uses M epsilon>=2B^2. A different active-set
  solution is required below this threshold; none is proved by the
  formula audited here.
* A first-occurrence inherited-hierarchy cut has A<1. The current
  geometric sufficient hypotheses therefore do not reach the central
  near-critical diagnostic, even before a uniform all-word argument.
* There is no paid continuation from an arbitrary actual prefix to a
  useful final completed payoff. Early energy and noncommutation alone
  would not provide that continuation.
* Selected-word success does not give a word-independent remainder or
  a bound on inf_h W_n(h). No limiting exact-profile constant follows.
* The full schedule-dependent complex region D_h, the complete earlier
  adversary portfolio, and general nonlinear scalar functions remain
  outside the reference-channel upper comparisons.
* No all-function upper, finite full-flow/PSD seed, or negative fixed-
  relative minimax improvement is supplied by this lower construction.

Immediate source repairs are specific and preserve the special-family
result: remove the extra '-1' from the Dhat evaluation, and state the
collapsed comparison's zero-tail equality/positive-tail strict scope.
For a later verifier gate, a narrowly stated fact should contain the
complete hypotheses, six/eight full-prefix maxima, terminal formulas,
minima/radii and exact named special-family comparisons. No formal gate
is authorized or requested in this audit.

The most useful mathematical next interface is a changed multistage
construction valid at small intermediate bridge ratios, particularly
A<1, while preserving EVERY prior query and its conjugate anchor.
For an actual return at lower final excess, the concrete local interface
is the complete eight-anchor active-set problem with z and t_R both
retained. Paying that interface for a much wider physical-word class
would be progress toward a uniform route; merely extending the selected
large-bridge family would not settle the original quantifiers.

# Two actual multistage envelopes with exact maximal terminal completion

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; max4; analytical invocation68.
Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT. No result below is a new fact.
Exactly two specified constructions are screened. No mathematical program,
numeric/arithmetic replay, trajectory execution, recurrence execution,
optimizer, DP, formal submission, installation, consult or subagent is used.

The original target remains the uniform all-word/all-large-horizon comparison
for W_n(h), with W=2R and actual terminal objective f(x_n). This draft DOES
NOT establish that comparison. It gives two attained multistage policies,
a genuine earlier-face return, exact completed terminal values and complete
minimum sets, and special families at every sufficiently large horizon on
which several earlier channels miss U_n^ZJ. The arbitrary-word bridge remains
unpaid. No completion/finalization/revocation/publication/shutdown is claimed.

## 1. Inputs, notation and the fixed dictionary

Verified inputs actually read and used:

- fact_id1252b8823bb5a290, statement and maximal-prefix proof: every
  ordered smooth-convex residual Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>
  +||g_i-g_j||^2/2 is retained inside the queried prefix. The maximal
  interpolant is the conjugate of ALL its conjugate lower quadratics.
  It preserves every prefix jet, including optimum and repeated queries.
- fact_idb98bb807d514e80d, full statement/proof: K_n(h) is the maximum
  of 1/(1+2 sum h) and (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)] over ALL
  original indices, with P_j and T_j the actual preceding/following sums.
  The exact ALL-split benchmark has C_0=B_0=1,
  C_n=max_j Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)), B_n=max_j G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
  Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
  G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2, U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,
  and B_n<=1+4n^p with p=log_2(3).
- fact_iddfde2fa326c908bf, statement and Chebyshev/ellipse proofs:
  Chi_N has entries sec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2N+1)]), 1<=j<=N,
  sum 2N(N+1)/3 and polynomial
  P_N(z)=(-1)^N Cheb_(2N+1)(sqrt(z))/[(2N+1)sqrt(z)].
  Its universal legal lens obeys |z||P_N(z)|^2<=cosh^2(2)/(2N+1)^2;
  a legal polynomial transcript has Gap_h(z)<=2|z||Poly_n(z)|^2.

Shared drafts0cc3c9de3d4aa82b,5784e63fc46dbec9,6a90257cc5422a59
and e2dad1cf3b95b8d7 were read FULL. They are awareness only. Every
new geometric and terminal calculation used here is rederived below.
In particular, no unverified shared three-cell theorem is a premise.

All notation in this report is local. Its reusable dictionary is:
M_mc=M>=2; B_mc=B>=2; A_mc=A>=4B+4; epsilon is in[0,1];
e_1,e_2,e_3 are the three standard Euclidean basis vectors;
p_mc=p, q_mc=q, r_mc=r are the frozen slopes below;
Qnorm_mc=Q=||q||^2, Rnorm_mc=R=||r||^2;
C_mc=C=2B+1 is the return-bridge coefficient;
a_mc=a=1-1/M; y_mc=y, z_mc=z and w_mc=w are proximal endpoints;
t_P,t_Q,t_R,t_D are first-query proximal endpoints of the four stages;
delta_P,delta_Q,delta_R,delta_D are their nonnegative support heights.
H_three,H_return are the two explicitly listed maximum-quadratic laws;
F_three,F_return are their convex conjugates; V_three,V_return are
their normalized actual terminal payoffs. All local vectors live in R^3.

Freeze the four-plane, full-dimensional dictionary BEFORE the run:

    nu=sqrt(2A-4),
    p=e_1/M,
    q=(-2e_1+nu e_2)/M,                  Q=2A/M^2,
    r=-q/2+sqrt((B/2-1/4)Q)e_3,         R=BQ/2,
    <q,r>=-Q/2,
    J(u)=max(0,<p,u>,<q,u>,<r,u>).

Every affine offset in J is0 and every maximum weight is1. The three
nonzero slopes are independent because nu>0 and B/2-1/4>0.
Thus D=conv{0,p,q,r} is a nondegenerate tetrahedron. Define the old
static function explicitly by

    F_old(x)=max_(g in D) [<x,g>-||g||^2/2].                 (1)

Its gradient is the Euclidean projection of x onto D. Projection
optimality gives firm nonexpansiveness and hence global smoothness1.
The minimizing inequalities prove convexity and differentiability.
This is also env_1(J): with g=P_D(x), u=x-g, the normal inequality
makes g an active subgradient of J(u), and completing the square
proves that u is its unique proximal minimizer. An interior point of D
has gradient x and Hessian I, so the least smoothness is exactly1.
Its COMPLETE minimum set is {x:<p,x>,<q,x>,<r,x><=0}, value0.
The point e_1 has true closest distance1 because p imposes x_1<=0
and origin attains that distance.

For every old proximal pair x=u+g, delta=J(u)=<g,u> and
F_old(x)=delta+||g||^2/2. For ANY two such pairs, direct expansion gives

    Q_xy=<u_y,g_x-g_y><=0,                               (2)

since g_x belongs to D and g_y maximizes support at u_y. This pays
every prefix, optimum and old terminal row in both orientations.

## 2. Reached chronology common to both constructions

Let each ordinary list be ANY finite nonnegative list of sum M-1.
Use physical order and retain every individual entry; a list is never
replaced by a single oracle step. Initialize at x_0=e_1.
The first selected bridge is AM+1, the second is BM+1.

First ordinary list and first bridge query: gradient p. At ordinary
partial mass s in[0,M-1], its proximal point is

    u=(1-(s+1)/M)e_1.

It has nonnegative p-height, q-height=-2p-height, and r-height=p-height.
The latter tie is retained, rather than falsely claimed strict. The
active subgradient p makes the proximal point unique, so the actual
envelope gradient is p. At the bridge query x=p and u=0. The bridge
AM+1 sends x to X=-A e_1.

Second ordinary list and second bridge query: gradient q. Its proximal
point is X-tq, 1<=t<=M. The exhaustive other-plane comparisons are

    <q,X-tq>=MQ-tQ=(M-t)Q>=0,
    <p,X-tq>=(-A+2t/M)/M<0,
    <r,X-tq>=-<q,X-tq>/2<=0.

Thus every actual query is paid, irrespective of individual step size.
The ending proximal point is

    y=X-Mq=(2-A,-nu,0),   <q,y>=<r,y>=0.

The BM+1 bridge, queried at y+q, sends x to X_R=y-BM q.

Third ordinary list and its following bridge query: gradient r. Its
proximal point is X_R-tr for 1<=t<=M. Here

    <r,X_R-tr>=(M-t)R>=0,
    <q,X_R-tr>=-BM Q+tQ/2<0,
    (X_R-tr)_1=2-A+2B-t/M<0.

These compare with0,q,p respectively. The exact ending proximal point is

    z=y-BM q-Mr,
    <r,z>=0,  <q,z>=M(1/2-B)Q<0,
    z_1=1-A+2B<0.                                        (3)

Every reached update so far is x_next=x-hp, x-hq or x-hr, selected
by the proved static guards, with h the actual original entry.
This is a reached p-to-q-to-r change of directions, not a freely
specified switched Hessian word or a pure-generation transport law.

## 3. Screen1: p-to-q-to-r and a completed unqueried terminal

The full known word is

    h_three=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,1+M epsilon),             (4)

where all three L_i have sum M-1. The last retained query is z+r;
the terminal point reached from it is x_n=z-M epsilon r.

First fix EVERY old preterminal jet from Section2, with its actual old
value. The distinct endpoint anchors are

    t_P=a e_1,                delta_P=a/M,
    t_Q=y+(M-1)q,             delta_Q=(M-1)Q,
    t_R=z+(M-1)r,             delta_R=(M-1)R,
    0, y, z,                 each of height0.

Within EACH constant-gradient stage the proximal points and heights
trace the affine segment between its two endpoints. Therefore its
conjugate lower quadratics are EXACT convex combinations of the endpoint
quadratics, including all remote/duplicate queries. The optimum quadratic
duplicates the0 endpoint. No row is discarded. There are SIX distinct
affine anchors, rather than the conservative seven in the preliminary plan.

Define the ONE completed static function BEFORE its actual run:

    H_three(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,
         <t_P,v>-delta_P,
         <y,v>, <t_Q,v>-delta_Q,
         <z,v>, <t_R,v>-delta_R),
    F_three(x)=sup_v [<x,v>-H_three(v)].                    (5)

This is the exact full-prefix law of fact1252, not a generic Moreau
representation. That fact preserves every preceding physical update
and queried value and gives a finite convex C1 globally1-smooth function.
We now derive its actual terminal gradient, without forcing the old r.

Put L=B+1-1/M, E=t_Q-z=M L q+Mr and

    D_3=||E||^2=2A(L^2-L+B/2)>=2AB^2,
    v_0=-M epsilon r,
    b_3=<E,v_0>-delta_Q=Aa(epsilon-2/M),
    theta_3=(b_3)_+/D_3,
    v_3=v_0-theta_3 E.                                   (6)

Here 0<=theta_3<=epsilon/(2B^2)<=1/8. If theta_3>0,
the z and t_Q terms tie; <z,v_3>=-theta_3<z,E>>0 because
<z,E>=2A(1/2-B)L<0. If theta_3=0, the z,y,0 terms tie
at0 and the t_Q term is nonpositive. All remaining anchors are paid:

- <y,v_3>=0 because y is orthogonal to q and r.
- <r,v_3>=(MQ/2)(-B epsilon+a theta_3)<0 for epsilon>0,
  so t_R is below z. At epsilon0 its negative offset still applies.
- <t_P-z,v_0>=-a epsilon<=0, while
  <t_P-z,E>=2L[A(B-1/2)-a]+a>0. Thus
  <t_P-z,v_3><=0<=delta_P.

Consequently z+theta_3 E is an active convex combination of z and t_Q
(or simply z when theta_3=0), and

    x_n=v_3+z+theta_3 E in partial H_three(v_3).

Strong convexity makes v_3 the unique ACTUAL terminal gradient. The exact
normalized value is

    V_three=2F_three(x_n)
       =AB epsilon^2-(Aa(epsilon-2/M)_+)^2/D_3,
    AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))<=V_three<=AB epsilon^2.         (7)

The old function has terminal value0: its p and q supports are strictly
negative and its r support is nonpositive at z-M epsilon r.
The gain is a changed unqueried completion, with all earlier values fixed.

The COMPLETE new minimum set is conv{0,y,z}, value0. Indeed H_three(0)=0
and its active slopes there are exactly0,y,z since all three deltas are
positive. Fenchel optimality gives F_three(x)=0 iff x is in
partial H_three(0)=conv{0,y,z}. All its points have first coordinate<=0.
Origin is the unique nearest point to e_1, so the TRUE radius stays1.
For sufficiently small positive v=t e_1 all nonzero zero-height slopes
are strictly negative and positive-offset terms are below0. Thus H_three
is ||v||^2/2 on a nonempty open set; its conjugate has an open Hessian-I
region. Advertised smoothness1 is SHARP, with no normalization loss.

## 4. Screen2: an ACTUAL return p-to-q-to-r-to-q

Freeze C=2B+1 and now use the full known word

    h_return=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,CM+1,L_3,
              1+M epsilon).                              (8)

Every L_i has mass M-1. Assume also M epsilon>=2B^2. At the third
bridge query the gradient is r and the position is z+r. The CM+1
bridge sends it to X_D=z-CM r. A fourth ordinary query has proposed
proximal point X_D-tq, 1<=t<=M. Direct ORIGINAL guards give

    <q,X_D-tq>=(M-t)Q>=0,
    <r,X_D-tq>=-CM R+tQ/2<0,
    (X_D-tq)_1=-A+2t/M<0.

Thus it really returns to the earlier q face on the SAME fixed function,
and the return persists through every physical fourth-block query.
Its ending proximal point is

    w=X_D-Mq=z-CMr-Mq,
    <q,w>=0, <r,w>=(MQ/2)(1-CB)<0,
    w_1=2-A<0.                                          (9)

The last physical update reaches x_n=w-M epsilon q. Both its old q
and r supports are negative, and its first coordinate 2-A+2epsilon<0,
so F_old(x_n)=0. Early energy alone supplies no terminal witness here.

Retain EVERY p,q,r,q prefix jet. In addition to Section3's six anchors,
retain t_D=w+(M-1)q with delta_D=(M-1)Q, and w with height0.
There are exactly EIGHT distinct affine anchors after optimum duplication.
Define H_return by adding these two affine terms to H_three, and
F_return=H_return^*. This exact finite law is fixed before the run.
The accepted1252 preserves the actual chronology in Sections2 and4.

The endpoint-only guess using w and z is WRONG: the FIRST r query t_R
can become the active old anchor. The repaired exact calculation follows.
Put

    d=C+a=2B+2-1/M,
    E_R=t_R-w=Mq+Md r,
    D_R=||E_R||^2=2A Dhat_R,
    Dhat_R=1-d+(B/2)d^2,
    v_0=-M epsilon q,
    b_R=<E_R,v_0>-delta_R
       =2A[(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M)],
    theta_R=b_R/D_R,
    v_R=v_0-theta_R E_R.                             (10)

Our assumptions imply b_R>0 and

    2B^3<=Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3,
    epsilon/(9B^2)<=theta_R<=epsilon/(2B^2)<1.           (11)

For the lower Dhat bound use d>=2B+1 and the increasing quadratic
1-d+Bd^2/2: at2B+1 it is2B^3+2B^2-3B/2-1>=2B^3.
For the upper use d<=2B+2<=3B. The numerator in theta_R is between
B epsilon/2 and B epsilon because M epsilon>=2B^2 implies
B M epsilon>=epsilon+B. These justify every inequality in(11).

All eight affine terms at v_R are now checked:

- The w and t_R terms tie by definition. The w term is positive since
  <w,v_0>=0 and <w,E_R>=A d(1-CB)<0.
- The old endpoint z is BELOW w. In fact

      <r,v_R>/(Q/2)
        =[M epsilon d(B-1/2)+(Ba/2)(Bd-1)]/Dhat_R>=B.

  The last inequality follows from M epsilon>=2B^2,
  Dhat_R<=Bd^2/2, d<=3B and B-1/2>=3B/4:
  2B^2 d(B-1/2)>=B^2 d^2/2. Thus <r,v_R>>=R.
  As t_R-z=(M-1)r and delta_R=(M-1)R,
  <z-w,v_R>=(M-1)(R-<r,v_R>)<=0.
- <q,v_R>=MQ[-epsilon+theta_R(B-1/(2M))]<0.
  Hence t_D is below w, and t_Q has negative affine value.
- The y and0 terms have value0, since y is orthogonal to q,r.
- For the initial p anchor, set E_P=t_P-w. Then
  <E_P,v_0>=2a epsilon and
  <E_P,E_R>=a(d-2)+Ad(CB-1)>=4AB^3.
  Therefore(11) gives
  <E_P,v_R><=2a epsilon-(4AB/9)epsilon<0<=delta_P,
  using A>=4B+4 and B>=2.

No remaining remote anchor is assumed inactive without a check.
The convex combination w+theta_R E_R of the TWO active slopes
w,t_R gives x_n=v_R+w+theta_R E_R in partial H_return(v_R).
Thus v_R is the ACTUAL new terminal gradient, and

    V_return=2F_return(x_n)
       =2A epsilon^2-b_R^2/D_R,
    2A epsilon^2(1-1/(2B))<=V_return<=2A epsilon^2.        (12)

In particular V_return>=3A epsilon^2/2. The upper follows also from
the forced proximal minimizer w and smoothness. The retained first r
anchor causes a paid, vanishing relative loss; it is not discarded.

The COMPLETE new minimum set is conv{0,y,z,w}, value0, by the exact
active slopes at v=0. Every nonzero listed vertex has negative first
coordinate. Thus origin is the unique closest initial minimizer,
TRUE radius1. The same small-positive-e_1 argument as in Section3
proves SHARP global smoothness1. Every actual directed prefix, terminal
and optimum residual, both orientations, holds because this is one actual
convex1-smooth function. The formal Gram comes from actual R^3 vectors.

## 5. A concrete failed deletion of the old r-start anchor

This is a symbolic counterexample to a shortcut, not a third construction.
Take M=1024,B=2,A=12,epsilon=1/2, C=5. All preceding cap guards and
M epsilon>=2B^2 hold. If one retains only the minimum anchors w,z at
the terminal, the proposed gradient is

    v_bad=-M epsilon q-theta_bad(z-w),
    theta_bad=(B-1/2)epsilon/[1-C+BC^2/2]=1/28.

It has <r,v_bad>/R=5M/56=640/7>1. The omitted t_R affine term
exceeds the z term by (M-1)(<r,v_bad>-R)>0.
Thus the endpoint-only answer is not the maximal-prefix function and
fails an ACTUAL retained prefix comparison. Section4's eight-anchor
calculation repairs this by changing the terminal gradient and value.
No deleted incoming/outgoing or optimum rows are inferred from accepted1252.

## 6. Specified growing words beating earlier channels on the SAME words

For every integer N>=2^60 set

    M=2N(N+1)+1,  A=M^(1/12), B=M^(1/120), epsilon=M^(-1/3).

Then B>=2, A=B^10>=4B+4 and M epsilon>=2B^2.
Let L_up be THREE consecutive increasing copies of Chi_N. Let L_down
be THREE consecutive decreasing copies of Chi_N. Both have mass M-1.
Use L_0=L_up and all subsequent L_i=L_down in(4),(8).
Their actual lengths are n_three=9N+3 and n_return=12N+4.
All caps are reached by the order-independent guards already proved.
No mathematical recurrence or trajectory has been executed.

For either law, its payoff is at least(3/2)M^(-7/12), and in fact
V_three>=(7/8)M^(-23/40). Keep EXACT formulas(7),(12) for the values.

Here is the full K_n screen, including every ordinary and bridge index.
In increasing Chi_N, h<=9(1+preceding mass within that copy).
In decreasing Chi_N, h<=9(1+following mass within that copy).
To justify9, consecutive increasing Chebyshev entries have ratio<=4:
write them as csc^2(k pi/(2N+1)), use consecutive angle ratio<=2
and concavity of sin on[0,pi/2]. The smallest entry is<=4/3.
Also max Chi_N=csc^2(pi/(2N+1))<M, by sin t>=2t/pi.
Repeated copies preserve these within-copy inequalities.

- Every ordinary cut in L_0 has its squared prefix ratio<=81 and
  tail mass>=AM, so its term is<=41/(AM).
- Every later ordinary cut has preceding mass>=AM. Write T for its
  full following mass. The decreasing order gives h<=9(1+T), while
  h<=M. Hence h^2/(1+2T)<=9M and its term is<=9/(A^2 M).
- The AM+1 bridge term is<=A^2/(2BM).
- The BM+1 bridge term is<=B^2/(A^2 M).
- In the returning word, the CM+1 bridge term is<=C^2/(A^2 M)
  <=(25/4)B^2/(A^2 M).
- The final bridge term is<=epsilon^2/A^2.
- The global Huber term is<=1/(2AM).

Substitution of the exact powers of M gives for BOTH complete words

    K_n(h)<=41 M^(-5/6).                                (13)

The reverse order in later lists is load-bearing. An increasing FINAL
list would invalidate this small-K argument near its last large entry;
that failed order-free shortcut is not used.

All convex quadratic witnesses in any dimension reduce by orthogonal
diagonalization to Q_n(h)=max_(0<=t<=1)t product(1-h_j t)^2.
Write T=(2N+1)^2=2M-1. The accepted Chebyshev identity gives
|P_N(t)|^2<=min(1,1/(Tt)). The first bound follows from
|sin((2N+1)theta)|<=(2N+1)|sin theta| by telescoping unit complex
numbers; the second from |Cheb_(2N+1)(sqrt(t))|<=1.
Setting u=Tt, the complete three-cap quadratic expression is bounded by

    T^(-1)u(1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+u)^2 min(1,u^(-9)),

and the returning expression has an additional (1+Cu)^2 and u^(-12)
in place of u^(-9). Split u<=1 and u>=1 and bound each positive
coefficient factor by twice its coefficient times max(1,u). This gives

    Q_n(h_three)<=64 A^2 B^2/T<=64 M^(-49/60),
    Q_n(h_return)<=256 A^2 B^2 C^2/T<=1600 M^(-4/5).      (14)

The actual bridge/T ratios are <=A,B,C,1 respectively; no physical
step was changed in this comparison. The order of Chebyshev copies
does not alter their polynomial.

The universal-lens channel can be bounded on the same words. Since
n>=N, Lens_n is contained in Lens_N. For Kc=cosh(2), the accepted
ellipse estimate and, when T|z|<=1, the direct product bound
|P_N(z)|<=exp(s(Chi_N)|z|)<=exp(1/6)<=Kc, give
|P_N(z)|^2<=Kc^2 min(1,1/(T|z|)). The same calculation as(14),
now using Gap_h(z)<=2|z||Poly_n(z)|^2, yields

    sup_Lens_n Gap_h_three <=128 Kc^18 M^(-49/60),
    sup_Lens_n Gap_h_return<=3200 Kc^24 M^(-4/5).         (15)

Since p=log_2(3)<8/5 (3^5<2^8), M>=2N^2 and n<=16N, accepted
B_n<=1+4n^p and(13)-(15) prove

    max(K_n,Q_n,sup_Lens_n Gap_h)=o(U_n^ZJ)              (16)

for each of these TWO specified full word families. This asserts nothing
about the full schedule-dependent complex legal region.

For the attained witness lower comparison, use the EXACT all-split
recurrences, not an assumed profile constant. They imply C_n>=C_(n-1),
C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j for lambda=1+sqrt2 by the balanced split,
and B_n>=2C_(n-1)-1. If d_N=floor(log_2 N), then n-1>=2^(d_N+3)-1,
so B_n>=lambda^(d_N+3). Also M<=5N^2<20*4^(d_N). Therefore

    B_n V >=(3lambda^3/[2*20^(7/12)])
                     (lambda/2^(7/6))^(d_N) >1,        (17)

for both families at every N>=2^60. The exact elementary inequalities
lambda>12/5, 2^(7/6)<9/4, 20^(7/12)<8 make the prefactor>2 and
the base>16/15. Thus the ratio in(17) diverges.

To cover EVERY sufficiently large integer horizon for each family,
take N=floor((n-3)/9) or floor((n-4)/12) respectively and insert the
remaining0through8 or0through11 zero steps at the VERY BEGINNING.
They repeat the initialized jet, retain all original rows and leave
the unqueried terminal free. Use exactly the same static H,F law.
Its true radius and terminal value are unchanged. The bounds use the
actual padded n and the actual all-split B_n. Thus both are attained
all-large-horizon SPECIAL-FAMILY lower witnesses exceeding U_n^ZJ.

## 7. What these policies add to the earlier two-block example

These paths have three or four physically queried gradient stages;
Screen2 actually returns to a retained earlier face. All normal components,
stage heights and intercepts are fixed, with a radius-one initial point.
There is no pure-generation mass dilution, imposed matrix word, uniform
positive lower Hessian window, or one-cap/one-cap single-cut continuation.

A precise same-word comparison with the NATURAL collapsed two-block
policy from shared e2dad is available, without adopting that draft as a
premise. Keep p=e_1/M and the bridge AM+1. Regard all remaining steps
BEFORE the final bridge as one ordinary block of mass M_2-1. For(4),
M_2=(B+2)M; for(8), M_2=(B+C+3)M=(3B+4)M. The frozen second slope
q_2=(-2e_1+sqrt(2A-4)e_2)/M_2 has norm squared2A/M_2^2.
The same old-cap guards prove every query legal. Its last proximal point
is an actual zero-height minimum for EVERY legal prefix completion:
the last query has f=||q_2||^2/2, and a unit step gives f(y_2)<=0.
Consequently every completion of THIS complete prefix has normalized
terminal value<=2A(M epsilon/M_2)^2, by smoothness about y_2.
The initial p jet forces true closest radius1 for every such completion.

Our three-stage value exceeds that upper by a factor at least
B(B+2)^2(1-1/(2B^3))/2. Our returning value exceeds the corresponding
upper by at least(3/4)(3B+4)^2. Both diverge. This comparison covers
the specifically defined collapsed policy, not EVERY possible two-block
partition, arbitrary second slope, scalar function, or spiral witness.
The broader established witness portfolio is not upper-bounded here.

## 8. Precise unpaid bridge and a diagnostic scoped failure

The sufficient arbitrary-block contracts proved here are literal:
equal incoming masses M-1, first bridge excess AM, second excess BM,
A>=4B+4, B>=2; the returning policy requires excess CM with C=2B+1
and M epsilon>=2B^2 at the last bridge. No claim is made that an arbitrary
word admits this partition, these ratios or a paid continuation afterward.

For the assigned inherited-(m,tau=1/4) hierarchy, define its separate
parameters beta_h=1-1/(4m), c_h=(1+sqrt2)(1+1/(4m)), a_h=beta_h/c_h,
H_h,0=(), H_h,(j+1)=(H_h,j,1+a_h*c_h^j,H_h,j),
S_h,j=sum H_h,j and b_h,j=1+a_h*c_h^j, using the SAME enclosing m>=7.
Its naive first-occurrence instantiation has first prefix H_h,j,
M=1+S_h,j and A=(b_h,j-1)/M. Here c_h<3 and
v_h=a_h/(c_h-2) lies in(0,1), since c_h-2>1/(1+sqrt2)>a_h.
The symbolic sum M=v_h*c_h^j+(1-v_h)*2^j therefore implies
A<c_h-2<1 for j>=1. For j=0, A=a_h<1. Either case contradicts
A>=4B+4. Therefore this SPECIFIC saturated three-cap policy cannot
be licensed at those first-occurrence hierarchy cuts. This is a failure
of its sufficient geometry, not an upper on W or on unscreened policies.

The main theorem still needs a different all-word decomposition or a
multistage law valid at much smaller intermediate bridge ratios, while
retaining an original terminal objective and a word-independent
constant-profile margin. That missing uniform statement is a theorem-level
gap. The special-family gains in(17) do not close it.

## 9. Retrieval, audit and allocation discipline

Skills used: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving and
construct-counterexamples. Fact search was performed before new proving.
Shared scalar multiscale, fresh-child tree/generation, projection-polytope,
and free-terminal scope failures were recalled. Relevant shared identifiers
include312a773572f0099c,35f68d3b35dfe32c,0458dd4dceadebde,
ad323c2b7c7c75aa,0e15279742ae0415,70e9da73936cb7b3.
The present A>=4B+4 growing-bridge families do not infer an extension of
their near-critical scoped conclusions. The returning law includes the
old first-r anchor explicitly rather than recycling a fresh-child tree rule.

Two Matlas queries were issued, then primary-source web background search.
The useful conceptual hit1910.01845,Theorem5 was not imported as a premise.
Downloaded exact1502.05666v6 HTML and extracted its text. Read Lemma2's
conjugacy proof and Theorem4's interpolation proof plus Remark2's explicit
maximum-quadratic construction. Authors: Adrien B. Taylor, Julien M.
Hendrickx, Francois Glineur; title: Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation
and Exact Worst-case Performance of First-order Methods (2016v6;2017journal).
Their smooth convex specialization is mu=0,L=1 in a finite Euclidean space.
It motivates the conjugate-anchor calculation; no external theorem is a
new black-box premise, because accepted1252 and the complete new algebra
pay interpolation, terminal completion and radius directly.
Exact source: https://arxiv.org/html/1502.05666v6.

Checks included every old and new terminal anchor, both original directed
orientations and optimum, exact minima/radii, sharp global norm, first-stage
p/r tie, return chronology, decreasing terminal-block ordering, zero-step
duplicates, physical horizon count, W=2R normalization and actual all-split
benchmark. No arithmetic or trajectory was executed by a program.

Preliminary plan3476d05f5f0346e5 conservatively said seven/nine anchors;
after accounting for the duplicated optimum the exact distinct counts are
six/eight. The endpoint-only terminal shortcut is concretely invalidated
in Section5, and the final formulas retain its missing anchor.

This is one allocated invocation after ended baseline67, ended ceiling68
and attempted ceiling69. All old grants remain CLOSED. No counters or
runtime service state were modified. Exactly TWO new specified laws were
screened; no third law, renewal or formal gate is requested or inferred.
Every new mathematical result is UNVERIFIED. The original target is OPEN.

# Exact zero-tail boundary of the collapsed comparison

Status: UNVERIFIED. This is a counterexample to a literal unrestricted
STRICT comparison/ratio sentence in final b3a629e987978a22 Section 7.
It does not refute the weak multiplicative inequality or the positive-tail
growing-family comparison. It does not address the original minimax goal.

Set M=2,B=2,A=12,epsilon=0 and L_0=L_1=L_2=(1). These satisfy
M>=2,B>=2,A>=4B+4 and epsilon in[0,1]. The three-stage physical word
is h=(1,25,1,5,1,1). No return-law hypothesis is being asserted.

Define p=e_1/2,q=(-2e_1+sqrt(20)e_2)/2,
r=-q/2+sqrt((3/4)||q||^2)e_3, so ||q||^2=||r||^2=6.
Let F_old(x)=max_(g in conv{0,p,q,r})(<x,g>-||g||^2/2).
It is convex 1-smooth, with gradient the unique projection. For a
proposed pair x=u+g, projection is certified exactly when g maximizes
max(0,<p,u>,<q,u>,<r,u>). The p-stage proximal points are e_1/2
and0, with p/r ties. The q-stage points are t_Q=-12e_1-q and
y=-12e_1-2q, whose q supports are6 and0; their p supports are
negative and r supports are -3 and0. The r-stage points are
t_R=y-4q-r and z=y-4q-2r, whose r supports are6 and0; their q
supports are -21 and -18 and their first coordinates are -13/2 and
-7. Thus every actual physical query has respectively gradients
p,p,q,q,r,r. Direct GD subtraction reaches z after the last unit step.

For ANY two of these pairs the signed residual is
Q_xy=<u_y,g_x-g_y><=0 by support maximality, including both
orientations and the zero optimum. Accepted fact_id1252b8823bb5a290
therefore preserves the entire actual prefix in the fixed completed law

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,<e_1/2,v>-1/4,
                           <y,v>,<t_Q,v>-6,
                           <z,v>,<t_R,v>-6),
    F=H^*.

Since H(0)=0 and H(v)>=||v||^2/2+<z,v>, its conjugate satisfies
F(z)=0, attained at v=0. The completed terminal gradient is zero.
All its minimum points have first coordinate<=0: the active slopes
at v=0 are exactly0,y,z. Thus the true closest initial radius is one.
In particular the normalized completed payoff V_three is exactly zero.

For the precisely specified collapsed policy M_2=(B+2)M=8,
q_2=(-2e_1+sqrt(20)e_2)/8. After the first bridge the remaining
ordinary entries1,5,1 have total7=M_2-1. Projection onto
conv{0,p,q_2} certifies the q_2 cap at every such query because
<q_2,-12e_1-tq_2>=(8-t)||q_2||^2>=0 and the p support is
negative for1<=t<=8. The last query has x=y_2+q_2,
g=q_2,f=||q_2||^2/2, where y_2=-12e_1-8q_2.
Every legal completion of this prefix WITH its retained zero optimum
has f(y_2)<=f(y_2+q_2)-||q_2||^2/2=0. Hence the final physical
unit step reaches a minimum and its terminal payoff is also exactly
zero for every such completion. The old collapsed function attains it.

The claimed factor specializes to
B(B+2)^2(1-1/(2B^3))/2=15. The weak inequality0>=15*0 holds.
Strict domination0>0 fails, and a ratio of zero to zero is undefined.
Consequently a strict/ratio comparison on the entire three-stage
parameter domain needs epsilon>0. That condition already holds for
the explicitly selected growing words epsilon=M^(-1/3), whose
comparison survives the independent audit. The exact repairs are to
state the weak bound at epsilon=0, and the strict ratio bound only
for epsilon>0, or explicitly restrict the comparison to those growing
words. The viable construction is not a dead branch.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002334 -->
# Exact six/eight-anchor multistage completions and two special word families

All norms and inner products are Euclidean. For an integer n>=0, a finite
nonnegative physical schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), and L>0, let W_n(h)
be the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)

over every finite-dimensional convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz
gradient, every minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, using the actual updates
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Write R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2. The witnesses
below use smoothness1 and the true closest initial distance to the complete
minimum set. Multiplication of their objective by any L>0 gives the same
points with steps h_i/L and the same normalized payoff.

## Definitions of the two completed functions

Fix real parameters M=M_mc>=2, B=B_mc>=2, A=A_mc>=4B+4, and
epsilon in [0,1]. These are local construction parameters, distinct from
the benchmark sequences C_n and B_n below. Put a=a_mc=1-1/M,
C=C_mc=2B+1, and let e_1,e_2,e_3 be the standard basis of R^3. Define

    nu=sqrt(2A-4),
    p=p_mc=e_1/M,
    q=q_mc=(-2e_1+nu e_2)/M,
    Q=Qnorm_mc=||q||^2=2A/M^2,
    r=r_mc=-q/2+sqrt((B/2-1/4)Q)e_3,
    R=Rnorm_mc=||r||^2=BQ/2,       <q,r>=-Q/2,
    D_mc=conv{0,p,q,r},
    F_old(x)=max_(g in D_mc){<x,g>-||g||^2/2},
    y=y_mc=-Ae_1-Mq,
    z=z_mc=y-BMq-Mr,
    w=w_mc=z-CMr-Mq,
    t_P=a e_1,              delta_P=a/M,
    t_Q=y+(M-1)q,           delta_Q=(M-1)Q,
    t_R=z+(M-1)r,           delta_R=(M-1)R,
    t_D=w+(M-1)q,           delta_D=(M-1)Q.

Here conv denotes convex hull. For each ordinary list L_i, choose any
finite nonnegative list of total mass M-1 and retain every entry, including
zeros. With concatenation in the displayed physical order, define

    h_three=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,1+M epsilon),
    h_return=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,CM+1,L_3,1+M epsilon).

Their physical lengths are sum_(i=0)^2 length(L_i)+3 and
sum_(i=0)^3 length(L_i)+4. Assertions about h_return additionally assume
M epsilon>=2B^2. Define, for v,x in R^3,

    H_three(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,<t_P,v>-delta_P,
               <y,v>,<t_Q,v>-delta_Q,<z,v>,<t_R,v>-delta_R},
    H_return(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,<t_P,v>-delta_P,
               <y,v>,<t_Q,v>-delta_Q,<z,v>,<t_R,v>-delta_R,
               <w,v>,<t_D,v>-delta_D},
    F_three(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H_three(v)},
    F_return(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H_return(v)}.

These are one fixed globally defined function per full physical run,
specified before that run. To define its complete old prefix, run F_old
from x_0=e_1 on the appropriate schedule and retain every triple
(x_i,grad F_old(x_i),F_old(x_i)) with 0<=i<n, together with the
zero optimum triple. The terminal index n is unqueried by the updates.
For any of the resulting actual functions, its signed ordered residual is
Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 on
{star,0,...,n}, where f_i=F(x_i), g_i=grad F(x_i), and
x_star=g_star=f_star=0.

## The specifically defined collapsed comparison

For the SAME h_three or h_return, retain the first list L_0 and bridge
AM+1, and treat all subsequent entries before the last 1+M epsilon as
one ordinary list, retaining their physical indices. Its mass is M_2-1,
where

    M_2=M_col_three=(B+2)M,
    or M_2=M_col_return=(B+C+3)M=(3B+4)M.

Set q_col=(-2e_1+nu e_2)/M_2 and define the static old collapsed function

    F_col_old(x)=max_(g in conv{0,p,q_col}){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

Run F_col_old from e_1 on that same full physical word and retain all
its old queried triples at indices 0<=i<n.
The collapsed completion class consists exactly of all everywhere defined
convex differentiable 1-smooth functions on R^3 interpolating its ENTIRE
old queried prefix, including values and gradients, and the optimum triple
(0,0,0). It is nonempty. For any such completion F_col, the same actual word
from e_1 has normalized true-distance payoff V_col(F_col). Define the positive
or zero upper comparison number

    U_col_three=2A epsilon^2/(B+2)^2,
    U_col_return=2A epsilon^2/(3B+4)^2,
    rho_col_three=[B(B+2)^2/2](1-1/(2B^3)),
    rho_col_return=(3/4)(3B+4)^2.

## Exact named reference channels and original benchmark

For any h of length n, put s(h)=sum_(i<n)h_i, P_j=sum_(i<j)h_i,
T_j=sum_(i>j)h_i, and (u)_+=max(u,0). The accepted single-cut channel is

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2s(h)),
          max_(0<=j<n)(h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]},

with the inner maximum omitted at n=0. Define the entire convex-quadratic
channel by

    Q_n(h)=max_(0<=t<=1) t product_(i<n)(1-h_i t)^2.

This is the supremum of the normalized payoff over all finite-dimensional
convex quadratics with gradient Lipschitz constant at most L, with arbitrary
initial points and chosen minimizers, in the original normalization.

For n>=1 and a complex variable zeta=alpha+i beta, put
Poly_0(zeta)=1, Poly_i(zeta)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t zeta),
xi_i=Poly_i(zeta), gamma_i=zeta Poly_i(zeta), and xi_star=gamma_star=0.
Identify the complex plane with the real plane using <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). On the index set
I_n={star,0,...,n}, the weight of the directed edge j->i is

    q_ij=<gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2.

Let ell_n be the maximum sum of these weights on directed simple paths
n->star. Define Gap_h(zeta)=-2ell_n for zeta in the exact universal lens

    Lens_n={alpha+i beta:
       alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2))},
    L_lens_n(h)=sup_(zeta in Lens_n) Gap_h(zeta),
    V_lens(h)=max{L_lens_n(h),1/(1+2s(h))}.

The meaning and legality of this transcript are those of accepted fact_id
dfde2fa326c908bf; the reference is restricted to Lens_n. No upper
comparison with the full schedule-dependent complex legal region is made.

Use the original all-split sequences, exactly as in accepted fact_id
b98bb807d514e80d. The joins have real arguments x,y>=0, and the
recurrences apply for every integer n>=1:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,      lambda=1+sqrt(2),      Kc=cosh(2).

Every maximum here ranges over ALL integer splits, with C_n=S_circle(n)+1
and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 in that predecessor's notation.

For each integer N>=2^60, set

    M=2N(N+1)+1, A=M^(1/12), B=M^(1/120), epsilon=M^(-1/3),
    T_Cheb=(2N+1)^2=2M-1.

Let Chi_N be the list of entries
sec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2N+1)]), 1<=j<=N. Let L_up be three consecutive
increasing copies of Chi_N and L_down three consecutive decreasing copies.
Use L_0=L_up and L_i=L_down for every subsequent i. Thus the two base
word lengths are 9N+3 and 12N+4. For every integer n>=9*2^60+3 define
the padded three-stage word by N=floor((n-3)/9), followed by inserting
n-(9N+3) leading zero steps into that base word. For every integer
n>=12*2^60+4 define the padded return word by N=floor((n-4)/12) and
insert n-(12N+4) leading zero steps. These are the two SPECIAL families
in the conclusions below, with their respective N and parameters.

## Conclusions

(i) F_three and, on its additional parameter domain, F_return are finite
everywhere, convex, continuously differentiable, and have least global
gradient Lipschitz constant EXACTLY1. Each is the pointwise maximal legal
completion of its complete specified old prefix. The exact maximum of
all physical prefix conjugate quadratics is H_three or H_return, with
six or eight distinct affine anchors, respectively; the first r-query
anchor t_R is retained in BOTH laws. Every old prefix point, value and
gradient is preserved. The actual queried gradients are p,q,r for the
three-stage law and p,q,r,q for the return law, one constant gradient on
each ordinary list and its following bridge query. Every signed ordered
interpolation residual, including every remote, optimum and terminal pair,
in both orientations, is nonpositive for the resulting actual function.
The complete minimum sets and minimum values are

    argmin F_three=conv{0,y,z},   min F_three=0,
    argmin F_return=conv{0,y,z,w},   min F_return=0.

Origin is the unique closest minimizer to e_1, at distance1 in both cases.

(ii) For the three-stage law set

    L_stage=B+a, E_3=M(B+a)q+Mr,
    D_3=||E_3||^2=2A[(B+a)^2-(B+a)+B/2],
    b_3=Aa(epsilon-2/M), theta_3=(b_3)_+/D_3.

The actual terminal point, gradient and normalized payoff are exactly

    x_n=z-M epsilon r,
    grad F_three(x_n)=-M epsilon r-theta_3 E_3,
    V_three=2F_three(x_n)
       =AB epsilon^2-[Aa(epsilon-2/M)_+]^2/D_3,
    AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))<=V_three<=AB epsilon^2.

This includes epsilon=0, epsilon=2/M when in range, M=2, and epsilon=1.
For the return law set

    d_R=C+a, E_R=Mq+M d_R r,
    Dhat_R=1-d_R+(B/2)d_R^2, D_R=||E_R||^2=2A Dhat_R,
    b_R=2A[(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M)], theta_R=b_R/D_R.

The actual terminal quantities are exactly

    x_n=w-M epsilon q,
    grad F_return(x_n)=-M epsilon q-theta_R E_R,
    V_return=2F_return(x_n)=2A epsilon^2-b_R^2/D_R,
    2A epsilon^2(1-1/(2B))<=V_return<=2A epsilon^2.

They include the feasible boundary M epsilon=2B^2. The denominator
bounds are 2B^3<=Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3; the lower-point identity is exactly
1-(2B+1)+(B/2)(2B+1)^2=2B^3+2B^2-3B/2.
In particular W_n(h_three)>=V_three and W_n(h_return)>=V_return.

(iii) Every member of the narrowly defined collapsed completion class
has true closest initial radius1 and 0<=V_col(F_col)<=U_col for its word.
On the entire respective parameter domains the WEAK comparisons are

    V_three>=rho_col_three U_col_three>=rho_col_three V_col(F_col),
    V_return>=rho_col_return U_col_return>=rho_col_return V_col(F_col).

At epsilon=0 in the three-stage domain, V_three=U_col_three=V_col(F_col)=0:
the displayed weak comparison has equality and no payoff ratio is asserted.
When epsilon>0 the comparison upper U_col is positive and the ratios
V_three/U_col_three>=rho_col_three>1 and
V_return/U_col_return>=rho_col_return>1 are defined and give strict
domination of every such collapsed completion. Epsilon>0 is automatic
in the return parameter domain. Both factors diverge on their specified
growing families. The comparison covers this exact retained prefix and
second slope, with no claim about other partitions, slopes, or witnesses.

(iv) For each of the two base growing words and each of its padded words,
the following bounds hold with each law evaluated on its corresponding
word and ACTUAL physical horizon n; V denotes that law's attained payoff:

    V_three>=(7/8)M^(-23/40),
    V_return>=(3/2)M^(-7/12),
    V>=(3/2)M^(-7/12)  for either law,
    K_n(h)<=41M^(-5/6),
    Q_n(h_three)<=64M^(-49/60),
    Q_n(h_return)<=1600M^(-4/5),
    L_lens_n(h_three)<=128Kc^18 M^(-49/60),
    L_lens_n(h_return)<=3200Kc^24 M^(-4/5).

Writing d_N=floor(log_2 N), the actual original benchmark obeys

    B_n V >= [3lambda^3/(2*20^(7/12))]
                       (lambda/2^(7/6))^(d_N)>1.

Consequently each SPECIAL family supplies an attained static witness at
EVERY integer horizon above its stated threshold with V>U_n^ZJ, and
V/U_n^ZJ tends to infinity. Meanwhile

    max{K_n(h),Q_n(h),L_lens_n(h)}=o(U_n^ZJ)

along each family, also with V_lens in place of L_lens_n. These results
neither upper-bound unrestricted W_n on these words nor quantify over
arbitrary words. They give no comparison with all nonlinear scalar
functions, all earlier adversaries, or the full complex legal region.
The original arbitrary-word asymptotic exact-profile theorem remains OPEN.
