<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002335 -->
# Independent audit of the frozen benchmark package

Auditor: max4, invocation 77. Status: UNVERIFIED independent mathematical review.
Verdict: no_gap_found_at_declared_scope.
This verdict is not verifier acceptance and is not a resolution of the original minimax problem.

Frozen source: DRAFT_UNVERIFIED.md in this directory.
SHA256: 34ea5110540b43c7598efb96dc5692a5ce93ab5594199bf9af0052f4be65cf4a.
The source was read in full and has not been edited.

## Scope and dependency audit

I checked all three assertions together: both all-n/all-split power envelopes;
the same-enclosing-m hierarchy ratio for every integer m>=1536; and the
conditional 799/800 implication. The full-function upper 17/(16D_m) is NOT
proved and is NOT an input fact. The statement's last assertion is an implication
with precisely that hypothesis.

The permitted accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d was read in full. It identifies the
original mass recurrences and W normalization. None of its coarse estimates is
needed. In particular, neither 04219554e41d9750's saved endpoints nor the earlier
unverified envelope 48010339bbfbce4f is a proof premise. Fact search produced
no duplicate of this complete package. Broad initial memory queries were
truncated; the full controlling guidance f6b7cc9ddb9c8d02 and needed inputs were
then obtained in bounded reads. An rg search for local skill files returned no
matches (exit 1); it was not a mathematical failure.

A Matlas query about the primitive power inequality returned two unrelated
theorems (1609.04774 Theorem 2.5 and 1703.09411 Lemma 3.4). Neither is used.
A web terminology search distinguished convexity in log coordinates from
ordinary log-convexity; no source theorem enters the proof. The full calculation
is provided below and in PROOF.md. No external_refs entry is needed.

## Independent attack: normalization and final implication first

For every finite nonnegative word h, W_n(h) is the supremum of twice the
objective gap divided by L times the squared distance to the chosen minimizer.
Removing that numerator factor defines R_n(h), so W_n(h)=2R_n(h).
W_n_star is the infimum over all such words, and therefore
W_n_star<=W_n(H_m) at n=2^m-1. Multiplication is by B_n>0, since U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.
Thus an upper estimate for B_n, combined with a lower estimate for D_m, gives
the required UPPER ratio B_n/D_m. The direction is correct.

Under the stated unproved hypothesis, the resulting ratio is strictly below
(17/16)(47/50)=799/800 for every integer m>=1536. Since 2^m-1 is an unbounded
sequence of actual integer horizons, the liminf over all horizons is at most
799/800. This requires no existence of a global limit and no supremum exchange.
There is no unconditional conclusion about W_n(H_m).

## Independent attack: same-m mass and endpoint m=1536

Every bridge 1+a*c^j is positive: epsilon=1/(4m) lies in (0,1/6144],
beta=1-epsilon>0, c=lambda(1+epsilon)>lambda>2, and a=beta/c>0.
Concatenation gives length(H_j)=2^j-1, S_0=0, and
S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a*c^j. Hence A_j=1+S_j satisfies
A_(j+1)=2A_j+a*c^j, A_0=1.

Let v=a/(c-2). Substituting (1-v)2^j+v*c^j into the recurrence gives
2(1-v)2^j+(2v+a)c^j, which is the proposed next term because
2v+a=cv. The initial value is 1. Since lambda(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda,
c(c-2)>1; consequently 0<v<1. This proves A_m>=v*c^m without changing m at
any child.

The denominator expansion is
c(c-2)=1+2(lambda+1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2.
The bounds lambda<5/2 and lambda^2=2lambda+1<6 give
v>(1-epsilon)/(1+7epsilon+6epsilon^2).
The comparison to 1-8epsilon follows by multiplying the positive denominator:
(1-8epsilon)(1+7epsilon+6epsilon^2)
=1-epsilon-50epsilon^2-48epsilon^3<1-epsilon.
All factors used when multiplying lower bounds are positive for m>=1536.

Keeping only the first three nonnegative binomial terms gives
(1+1/(4m))^m>=41/32-1/(32m). Also 2^m>m for every integer m>=1
(by induction), hence lambda^(-m)<1/m. Expansion of the resulting product is
2(1-2/m)(41/32-1/(32m))-1/m
=41/16-99/(16m)+1/(8m^2).
Discarding the last positive term gives a strict lower bound. At m=1536,
41/16-99/24576=(62976-99)/24576=20959/8192.
The cross products are 20959*47=985073 and 8192*120=983040.
Their difference is 2033>0. The endpoint is included; no eventual unspecified
threshold was substituted. Together with B_(2^m-1)<=(12/5)lambda^m this proves
the strict ratio 47/50 with the correct direction.

## Independent attack: primitive envelope

First lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2)>14 because (7/5)^2<2.
Thus lambda^15>14^5=537824>524288=2^19, and rho>19/15>1.

For t>0 set E(t)=(1+t)^rho-1>0 and Q(z)=log E(exp z).
Writing t=exp z gives
Q'(z)=rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-1)/E(t).
Differentiating once more and collecting the numerator gives
Q''(z)=rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)
 *[(1+rho*t)E(t)-rho*t*(1+t)^rho]/E(t)^2
=rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)
 *[(1+t)^rho-1-rho*t]/E(t)^2.
The bracket has value zero at t=0 and strictly positive derivative for t>0,
so Q is convex. Its midpoint inequality at z and -z yields
E(t)E(1/t)>=(2^rho-1)^2=2.

For positive x,y and T=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho, multiplication gives
(T-x)(T-y)>=2xy. Equivalently T^2-(x+y)T-xy>=0.
Its negative root is strictly negative and T>0, so T>=Omega(x,y).
If x=0 or y=0, the asserted inequality is equality directly; at x=y=0 both
sides vanish. No use of log 0 or division by 0 is needed.
Omega is coordinatewise nondecreasing on the closed nonnegative quadrant
because both its linear part and the polynomial under its square root are.
Induction uses k+1 and n-k, both positive for every allowed integer split,
whose sum is n+1. The n=0 case is equality.

## Independent attack: sharper scalar inequality and global maximum

It suffices to use p=19/30 in (5/6)x^(2p)+(1-x)^p<=1 because the bases are
in [0,1] and p>0. Endpoint powers are well-defined including base zero.
Let Psi(x)=(5/6)x^(19/15)+(1-x)^(19/30).
Its derivative on (0,1) is
(19/18)x^(4/15)-(19/30)(1-x)^(-11/30).
A critical point therefore satisfies
x^(4/15)(1-x)^(11/30)=3/5,
equivalently x^8(1-x)^11=(3/5)^30.

The logarithmic derivative of x^8(1-x)^11 is
(8-19x)/(x(1-x)); it is negative on (8/19,1).
For x>=2/3 the left side is at most 2^8/3^19. The strict comparison to the
critical value follows from the exact integer identities
2^8*5^30=238418579101562500000000
<239299329230617529590083=3^49.
These are integer products, not a floating point approximation. The powers
can be checked by successive integer multiplication, for example from
5^10=9765625, 3^10=59049 and 3^9=19683. No program was run for them.
Thus every critical point lies strictly below 2/3.

Substituting the derivative relation gives the exact critical value
g(x)=(1-x/2)/(1-x)^(11/30).
Its logarithmic derivative is
-1/(2-x)+11/[30(1-x)]
=(19x-8)/[30(1-x)(2-x)].
It decreases then increases on [0,2/3], so its maximum there is at an endpoint.
g(0)=1 and g(2/3)^30=2^30/3^19<1, since
2^30=1073741824<1162261467=3^19.
Psi is continuous on [0,1], differentiable in its interior, with endpoint
values 1 and 5/6. Even though the derivative is singular at x=1, an interior
maximum must be critical and endpoints are checked separately. This exhausts
the possible maximizers without assuming how many critical points exist.

## Independent attack: dominant induction

For x,y>=0,
sqrt(G(x,y))=(sqrt(y)+sqrt(y+8x))/2.
Squaring proves this identity. The expression is the larger nonnegative root
of z^2-sqrt(y)z-2x=0, and is positive unless both x and y vanish.
For a positive trial z, nonnegativity of this polynomial puts z above that
root; the other root is nonpositive. G is coordinatewise nondecreasing.

Set kappa=12/5. At every split, A=k+1>=1, F=n-k>=1, N=A+F=n+1.
The trial z=sqrt(kappa)N^(rho/2) has nonnegative root polynomial exactly when
(2/kappa)(A/N)^rho+(F/N)^(rho/2)<=1.
This is the scalar inequality with p=rho/2>19/30. Strong induction on B_n,
using the already established C bound, controls each split and hence its
full maximum. B_0=1<=12/5 is included.

## Editorial changes authorized for the new package

The new statement expands W_n_star and R_n and the full objective class.
The proof uses E_be,Q_be,Psi_be,g_be,A_be_j,v_be,kappa_be for local auxiliaries
to avoid clashes with unrelated project glossary entries. H_j, S_j, a and c
are explicitly local hierarchy notation. Positive-root endpoint language is
made precise: a root at (0,0) is nonnegative, not strictly positive.
The derivative calculations, signs, finite-horizon n=0 case, positivity of
every multiplying factor, and subsequence liminf argument are expanded.
These are editorial expansions, not changed constants, quantifiers, hypotheses,
or conclusions. No weakened statement is proposed.

No mathematical program, numerical/arithmetic replay, trajectory or recurrence
execution, optimizer, solver, benchmark DP, installation, paid consult, or
subagent was used. Administrative reading, hashing, JSON serialization and
receipt handling are separate from the mathematical reasoning.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002336 -->
# A tighter all-split benchmark envelope and a conditional certificate threshold

**UNVERIFIED direct Codex candidate. The original minimax problem is OPEN.**

This document is a self-contained scalar benchmark argument, produced by main
under the operator's explicit direct-reasoning authorization. It is not a
verifier-accepted fact. No mathematical program, numerical experiment, or
formal call was used in preparing it. The earlier shared draft
48010339bbfbce4f suggested the power-envelope proof framework; none of its
unverified conclusions is used as a premise. All needed inequalities are
derived below.

## 1. Exact original definitions and candidate statements

Put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),  n>=1.

EVERY maximum is over ALL integer splits. These are exactly the original
benchmark capacities, with U_n^ZJ=1/B_n and W=2R. No balanced-only
recurrence or decimal asymptotic constant replaces them.

The candidate claims are:

1. For every integer n>=0,

       C_n <= (n+1)^rho,
       B_n <= (12/5)(n+1)^rho.                       (1.1)

2. Fix any integer m>=1536 and use the SAME enclosing parameters at every
   child:

       epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
       c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
       H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

   Let n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j and D_m=1+2S_m. Then

       B_(n_m)/D_m < 47/50.                         (1.2)

3. Consequently, IF a separate rigorous full-class argument establishes

       W_(n_m)(H_m) <= 17/(16D_m)
                       for every m>=1536,          (1.3)

   then

       B_(n_m) W_(n_m)(H_m) < 799/800,
       liminf_(n->infinity) W_n^*/U_n^ZJ <=799/800<1. (1.4)

Assertion (1.3) is an UNPROVED HYPOTHESIS here. This document supplies NO
full-class GD certificate, no uniform original lower witness and no
resolution of the original problem. Its purpose is to give a rigorously
oriented benchmark threshold for a future certificate.

## 2. An exact lower bound on the exponent

The identity lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2) and the strict bound sqrt(2)>7/5 give
lambda^3>14. Therefore

    lambda^15 > 14^5 = 537824 > 524288 = 2^19.

Since lambda=2^rho and logarithm is increasing,

    rho>19/15>1,   rho/2>19/30.                     (2.1)

These are exact integer and radical comparisons.

## 3. Primitive capacity power inequality

For t>0 define E(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 and q(z)=log E(exp(z)), where log is the
natural logarithm. With t=exp(z), direct differentiation gives

    q''(z)=rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)
       *[(1+t)^rho-1-rho*t]/[(1+t)^rho-1]^2 >=0.     (3.1)

The bracket vanishes at t=0 and its derivative for t>0 is
rho[(1+t)^(rho-1)-1]>0, because rho>1. Thus q is convex on the whole
real line. Applying convexity at z and -z yields

    E(t)E(1/t) >= E(1)^2
                 =(2^rho-1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2.      (3.2)

For x,y>0 put A=x^(1/rho), F=y^(1/rho), and T=(A+F)^rho. Then

    T-x=x E(F/A),   T-y=y E(A/F).

By (3.2), (T-x)(T-y)>=2xy, equivalently

    T^2-(x+y)T-xy>=0.

T is positive, so it lies at least as high as the positive root of this
quadratic. That root is Omega(x,y). By continuity the same inequality
holds if x or y is zero. We have proved

    Omega(x,y) <= (x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho
                                          for all x,y>=0. (3.3)

Omega is nondecreasing in both nonnegative arguments, directly from its
defining formula. The n=0 primitive claim in (1.1) is equality. If the
claim holds below n, each original split k+(n-k-1)=n-1 gives

    Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1))
       <= Omega((k+1)^rho,(n-k)^rho)
       <= (n+1)^rho.

Taking the original maximum proves C_n<=(n+1)^rho for ALL n. No integer
split has been removed.

## 4. A sharper scalar power inequality

We prove

    (5/6)x^(2p)+(1-x)^p <=1
              for every x in[0,1] and p>=19/30.      (4.1)

For fixed x in[0,1], increasing p only decreases both summands. It
suffices to study the continuous function

    psi(x)=(5/6)x^(19/15)+(1-x)^(19/30).

Its endpoint values are psi(0)=1 and psi(1)=5/6. Every interior critical
point satisfies

    x^(4/15)(1-x)^(11/30)=3/5,
    x^8(1-x)^11=(3/5)^30.                           (4.2)

The function x^8(1-x)^11 is strictly decreasing for x>8/19: its
logarithmic derivative there is (8-19x)/(x(1-x)). For x>=2/3, therefore,

    x^8(1-x)^11 <= 2^8/3^19 < (3/5)^30.             (4.3)

The last strict inequality is exactly the integer comparison

    2^8*5^30 = 238418579101562500000000
           < 239299329230617529590083 = 3^49.

Thus EVERY interior critical point of psi lies below2/3.

At such a point the derivative equation in (4.2) gives

    psi(x)=(1-x/2)/(1-x)^(11/30)=:g(x).

Direct differentiation gives

    g'(x)/g(x)=(19x-8)/[30(1-x)(2-x)].               (4.4)

Consequently g decreases up to8/19 and then increases. Its maximum on
[0,2/3] is attained at an endpoint. At the endpoints,

    g(0)=1,
    g(2/3)^30=2^30/3^19<1,

where 2^30=1073741824<1162261467=3^19. Hence psi<=1 at every interior
critical point, and also at its two endpoints. The maximum of this
continuous function on[0,1] is attained; if interior it is a critical
point. This proves (4.1), including both boundary values.

## 5. Dominant capacity bound at every integer split

Set kappa=12/5. The formula for G gives

    sqrt(G(x,y))=(sqrt(y)+sqrt(y+8x))/2.

This is the positive root of z^2-sqrt(y)z-2x=0. G is nondecreasing in
both nonnegative arguments. The n=0 claim B_0<=kappa is immediate.
For any split at n>=1 put A=k+1, F=n-k and N=A+F=n+1. The induction
hypothesis and Section3 imply

    G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)) <= G(A^rho,kappa F^rho).

Let z=sqrt(kappa)*N^(rho/2). To place z at least at the positive root,
it is sufficient and necessary that

    z^2-sqrt(kappa F^rho)z-2A^rho >=0.

Dividing by kappa N^rho makes this inequality

    (2/kappa)(A/N)^rho+(F/N)^(rho/2)<=1.

It is (4.1) with x=A/N, p=rho/2>19/30 and 2/kappa=5/6. Thus EVERY
original split has value at most kappa(n+1)^rho. Taking the original
maximum proves the second statement of (1.1) for every integer n>=0.
This proof uses no numerical recurrence evaluation or saved endpoint.

## 6. The same-enclosing-m hierarchy and its exact total mass

Use exactly the parameters in Section1, with m>=1536. The physical
lengths are n_j=2^j-1. Put A_j=1+S_j. Summing the literal concatenation
gives A_0=1 and

    A_(j+1)=2A_j+a*c^j.

With

    v=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)],

the exact solution, checked by substitution and the initial value, is

    A_j=(1-v)2^j+v*c^j.                              (6.1)

Since c>lambda>2 and lambda(lambda-2)=1, we have0<v<1. In particular
A_m>=v*c^m, and D_m=2A_m-1.

For the required elementary lower bound on v, write

    c(c-2)=1+2(lambda+1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2.

The inequalities lambda<5/2 and lambda^2=2lambda+1<6 imply

    v > (1-epsilon)/(1+7epsilon+6epsilon^2)>1-8epsilon.

For the second comparison, epsilon<1/8 and

    (1-8epsilon)(1+7epsilon+6epsilon^2)
       =1-epsilon-50epsilon^2-48epsilon^3<1-epsilon.

Thus v>1-2/m. The binomial expansion at integer m>=2 gives

    (1+1/(4m))^m
       >=1+1/4+(m-1)/(32m)=41/32-1/(32m).           (6.2)

Also lambda^m>2^m>m, so lambda^(-m)<1/m. Combining these inequalities,

    D_m/lambda^m
       >2(1-2/m)(41/32-1/(32m))-1/m
        =41/16-99/(16m)+1/(8m^2)
       >41/16-99/(16m)
       >=41/16-99/(16*1536)
        =20959/8192 >120/47.                         (6.3)

The last rational comparison is 20959*47=985073>983040=8192*120.
Every bound uses the SAME enclosing m; no child parameter is reset.

At the ACTUAL horizon n_m=2^m-1, (1.1) gives

    B_(n_m) <= (12/5)(2^m)^rho=(12/5)lambda^m.

Division by the positive lower bound (6.3) proves

    B_(n_m)/D_m < (12/5)/(120/47)=47/50.

This establishes (1.2) as a scalar benchmark claim, independently of
any claim about the unrestricted GD worst-case performance on H_m.

## 7. Correctly oriented conditional implication for the original target

Assume, and ONLY for this paragraph, that a separate complete full-class
certificate proves (1.3). Then for every m>=1536,

    W_(n_m)^*/U_(n_m)^ZJ
      =B_(n_m) W_(n_m)^*
      <=B_(n_m) W_(n_m)(H_m)
      <=(17/16) B_(n_m)/D_m
      <(17/16)(47/50)=799/800.

The actual horizons n_m tend to infinity. Thus this hypothesis would
give the original negative resolution with fixed delta=1/800. No
supremum/limit interchange, balanced benchmark replacement, radius
change, or missing factor2 is used.

The HYPOTHESIS (1.3) remains absent. The unconditional results here are
only the exact benchmark envelope (1.1) and its scalar hierarchy
comparison (1.2). They do not establish a full-function upper bound,
the uniform all-word lower bound, or either resolution of the original
minimax problem. Independent audit and verifier acceptance are required
before treating these new benchmark inequalities as project facts.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002338 -->
The accepted predecessor b98bb807d514e80d is used only to identify the original benchmark coordinates and normalization: C_n=S_circle(n)+1, B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, the displayed Omega and G recurrences, and the factor-2 definition of W_n. All inequalities needed here are proved in this proof. No saved numerical enclosure, evaluated recurrence, other accepted estimate, or unverified finding is used.

1. Exact exponent and positivity.

The identity lambda^2=2lambda+1 gives lambda(lambda-2)=1 and lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2). Since 2>49/25, sqrt(2)>7/5, so lambda^3>14. Therefore
lambda^15>14^5=537824>524288=2^19.
Taking the increasing base-2 logarithm gives rho>19/15>1. In particular rho/2>19/30. Also sqrt(2)<3/2, whence lambda<5/2 and lambda^2=2lambda+1<6.
The finite recurrences define positive finite capacities: C_0=B_0=1, and Omega and G map positive finite pairs to positive finite numbers. Each maximum is over a nonempty finite set when n>=1.

2. Primitive power inequality, including zero arguments.

For t>0 let E_be(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. It is positive. For z in the real line set Q_be(z)=log E_be(exp z), where log is natural. With t=exp z,
Q_be'(z)=rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-1)/E_be(t).
Since dt/dz=t, differentiation and collection of factors gives
Q_be''(z)
=rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)*[(1+rho*t)E_be(t)-rho*t*(1+t)^rho]/E_be(t)^2
=rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)*[(1+t)^rho-1-rho*t]/E_be(t)^2.
The function of t in the last square bracket is zero at t=0 and has derivative rho*((1+t)^(rho-1)-1)>0 for t>0. Consequently Q_be''(z)>0 for every real z. Convexity applied at z and -z yields
Q_be(z)+Q_be(-z)>=2Q_be(0).
Exponentiating and using 2^rho=lambda gives
E_be(t)*E_be(1/t)>=E_be(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2 for every t>0.

Take x,y>0, let u=x^(1/rho), w=y^(1/rho), and T_be=(u+w)^rho. Then
T_be-x=x E_be(w/u), T_be-y=y E_be(u/w).
Multiplying proves (T_be-x)(T_be-y)>=2xy, or
T_be^2-(x+y)T_be-xy>=0.
The roots of the latter polynomial are
[(x+y) +/- sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy)]/2.
For x,y>0 the smaller root is negative, the larger is Omega(x,y), and T_be>0. Thus T_be>=Omega(x,y). If one of x,y is zero, Omega(x,0)=x and Omega(0,y)=y, so the same inequality is equality; at (0,0) both sides are zero. This proves
Omega(x,y)<=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho for all x,y>=0.

The polynomial x^2+6xy+y^2 is nondecreasing in each nonnegative variable, as is x+y. Monotonicity of the square root shows that Omega is nondecreasing in each argument on the whole nonnegative quadrant. For the primitive induction, C_0=1=(0+1)^rho. Suppose the bound is proved at all indices below n>=1. For every integer k with 0<=k<n, both k and n-k-1 are below n, so
Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1))
<=Omega((k+1)^rho,(n-k)^rho)
<=(k+1+n-k)^rho=(n+1)^rho.
Taking the maximum over every allowed integer k proves C_n<=(n+1)^rho.

3. A scalar inequality that will control every dominant split.

For every real p>=19/30 and x in [0,1], we claim
(5/6)x^(2p)+(1-x)^p<=1.
The exponents are positive, so zero bases present no ambiguity. Both summands are nonincreasing functions of p for each fixed x in [0,1]. It suffices to establish the claim at p=19/30. Define the continuous function
Psi_be(x)=(5/6)x^(19/15)+(1-x)^(19/30), 0<=x<=1.
It has endpoint values Psi_be(0)=1 and Psi_be(1)=5/6. On the open interval,
Psi_be'(x)=(19/18)x^(4/15)-(19/30)(1-x)^(-11/30).
Thus each interior critical point satisfies
x^(4/15)*(1-x)^(11/30)=3/5,
equivalently x^8*(1-x)^11=(3/5)^30.
For x in (0,1), logarithmic differentiation of x^8*(1-x)^11 gives
8/x-11/(1-x)=(8-19x)/(x(1-x)).
It is strictly negative for x>8/19. Since 2/3>8/19, every x in [2/3,1) satisfies
x^8*(1-x)^11<=2^8/3^19<(3/5)^30.
For the strict inequality, multiply positive denominators and use the exact integer comparison
2^8*5^30=238418579101562500000000
<239299329230617529590083=3^49.
It follows that every interior critical point of Psi_be is in (0,2/3).

At an interior critical point, the derivative equation gives
(5/6)x^(19/15)=(x/2)*(1-x)^(-11/30).
Hence its value is
Psi_be(x)=(1-x/2)*(1-x)^(-11/30)=g_be(x).
The right side defines a positive continuously differentiable function on [0,2/3]. Its logarithmic derivative is
g_be'(x)/g_be(x)=-1/(2-x)+11/[30(1-x)]
=(19x-8)/[30(1-x)(2-x)].
The denominator is positive on [0,2/3]. Therefore g_be decreases on [0,8/19] and increases on [8/19,2/3]. Its maximum on [0,2/3] is at an endpoint. At the endpoints,
g_be(0)=1,
g_be(2/3)^30=2^30/3^19<1,
where 2^30=1073741824<1162261467=3^19.
Because g_be(2/3)>0, this proves g_be(2/3)<1. In particular every interior critical value of Psi_be is at most 1.
The continuous function Psi_be attains a maximum on [0,1]. If a maximizing point is an endpoint the value is at most 1 by the endpoint calculation; if it is interior its derivative vanishes and the critical-value argument applies. This proves Psi_be<=1 and therefore the claimed scalar inequality for all p>=19/30 and x in [0,1]. No differentiability at x=1 is required.

4. Dominant power inequality and induction over all splits.

Put kappa_be=12/5. For x,y>=0 define
r_be=(sqrt(y)+sqrt(y+8x))/2.
Direct squaring yields
r_be^2=y/2+2x+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2=G(x,y).
Thus sqrt(G(x,y))=r_be, the larger nonnegative root of
z^2-sqrt(y)z-2x=0.
The other root is nonpositive. For a positive trial z, if this polynomial is nonnegative, then z is at least its larger root. This includes x=0 or y=0; when both vanish the larger root is zero.
The formula for G is coordinatewise nondecreasing on x,y>=0, because its linear terms and its square-root radicand have that property.

We prove B_n<=kappa_be(n+1)^rho by strong induction. At n=0 this is 1<=12/5. Suppose it holds below n>=1. For each original integer split k with 0<=k<n set u=k+1, w=n-k, and N_be=u+w=n+1. Then u,w>=1 and the primitive bound already proved gives
G(C_k,B_(n-k-1))<=G(u^rho,kappa_be*w^rho).
Take the positive trial z=sqrt(kappa_be)*N_be^(rho/2). The root polynomial for this last G value is nonnegative at z if and only if
kappa_be*N_be^rho-kappa_be*w^(rho/2)*N_be^(rho/2)-2u^rho>=0,
or, after division by the positive quantity kappa_be*N_be^rho,
(2/kappa_be)*(u/N_be)^rho+(w/N_be)^(rho/2)<=1.
Apply part 3 with x=u/N_be in (0,1) and p=rho/2>19/30. Because 2/kappa_be=5/6 and w/N_be=1-x, that part gives exactly this inequality. Consequently
G(u^rho,kappa_be*w^rho)<=z^2=kappa_be(n+1)^rho.
The same bound holds for EVERY split k, so its maximum B_n satisfies it. This completes the dominant induction, including n=0.

5. Exact mass of the same-enclosing-m hierarchy.

Fix an arbitrary integer m>=1536. The parameters epsilon,beta,c,a are those in the statement and do not change with j. They satisfy
0<epsilon=1/(4m)<=1/6144<1/8,
0<beta<1, c>lambda>2, a>0.
Every bridge 1+a*c^j is strictly positive. Length starts at zero and satisfies length(H_(j+1))=2length(H_j)+1, so induction gives length(H_j)=2^j-1 for all 0<=j<=m.
Summing the literal concatenation gives S_0=0 and
S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a*c^j.
Define A_be,j=1+S_j for 0<=j<=m and v_be=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)].
Then A_be,0=1 and A_be,j+1=2A_be,j+a*c^j.
For all these indices the exact solution is
A_be,j=(1-v_be)2^j+v_be*c^j.
Indeed the initial value is 1, and substituting the right side into the recurrence gives
(1-v_be)2^(j+1)+(2v_be+a)c^j
=(1-v_be)2^(j+1)+v_be*c^(j+1),
using v_be(c-2)=a.

For t>2, t(t-2) is strictly increasing since its derivative is 2t-2>0. Thus c>lambda>2 and lambda(lambda-2)=1 imply c(c-2)>1. Since 0<beta<1, one has 0<v_be<1, so A_be,m>=v_be*c^m.

Expanding c=lambda(1+epsilon) gives
c(c-2)=1+2(lambda+1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2
<1+7epsilon+6epsilon^2,
where the strict upper bound uses lambda<5/2 and lambda^2<6.
The numerator beta=1-epsilon is positive, so
v_be>(1-epsilon)/(1+7epsilon+6epsilon^2).
Furthermore
(1-8epsilon)(1+7epsilon+6epsilon^2)
=1-epsilon-50epsilon^2-48epsilon^3<1-epsilon.
Division by the positive denominator shows v_be>1-8epsilon=1-2/m>0.

The binomial expansion at integer m>=2 has nonnegative terms. Its first three terms give
(1+1/(4m))^m
>=1+1/4+[m(m-1)/2]/(16m^2)
=41/32-1/(32m)>0.
Also 2^m>m for integers m>=1: at m=1 this is 2>1; if 2^m>m then 2^(m+1)>2m>=m+1. Since lambda>2, lambda^m>m and lambda^(-m)<1/m.

Now D_m=1+2S_m=2A_be,m-1 is positive. Using the exact A_be,m formula, then the positive lower bounds just proved, yields
D_m/lambda^m
>=2v_be*(1+1/(4m))^m-lambda^(-m)
>2(1-2/m)*(41/32-1/(32m))-1/m
=41/16-99/(16m)+1/(8m^2)
>41/16-99/(16m)
>=41/16-99/(16*1536)
=20959/8192
>120/47.
The last strict comparison follows from
20959*47=985073>983040=8192*120.
Every division and multiplication in this chain is by a positive quantity, and m=1536 is included.

At the actual horizon n_m=2^m-1, part 4 gives
B_(n_m)<=(12/5)(2^m)^rho=(12/5)lambda^m.
Combining the upper bound on B with the strict lower bound on D gives
B_(n_m)/D_m<(12/5)/(120/47)=47/50.
The strictness is retained because D_m/lambda^m>120/47 and B_(n_m)>0. This proves the unconditional hierarchy comparison with the SAME enclosing parameters at every child.

6. Conditional conclusion and normalization.

The definitions of W_n and R_n have identical suprema and differ only by multiplication of each nonnegative payoff by 2. Multiplication by this positive constant commutes with the supremum, so W_n=2R_n. We use W throughout, with no rescaling of the supplied hypothetical bound.

For this part ONLY, assume the full-class upper bound
W_(n_m)(H_m)<=17/(16D_m)
for every integer m>=1536. H_m is an admissible predetermined word of length n_m, so the infimum defining W_(n_m)_star satisfies
W_(n_m)_star<=W_(n_m)(H_m).
Because B_(n_m)>0 and U_(n_m)^ZJ=1/B_(n_m), part 5 gives
W_(n_m)_star/U_(n_m)^ZJ
=B_(n_m)*W_(n_m)_star
<=B_(n_m)*W_(n_m)(H_m)
<=(17/16)*B_(n_m)/D_m
<(17/16)*(47/50)=799/800.
The same displayed chain also proves B_(n_m)*W_(n_m)(H_m)<799/800.

For every integer cutoff N there is an m>=1536 with n_m>=N, since n_m=2^m-1 tends to infinity. Thus the infimum of W_n_star/U_n^ZJ over all n>=N is at most 799/800. Taking the increasing limit of these tail infima proves
liminf_(n->infinity) W_n_star/U_n^ZJ<=799/800<1.
This conclusion is conditional on the full-class upper hypothesis, which has not been established in this proof. Only the benchmark envelopes and the hierarchy benchmark comparison are unconditional.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002339 -->
A tighter exact all-split benchmark envelope and a conditional GD certificate threshold.

Fix L>0. For every integer n>=0 and predetermined nonnegative finite word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), run gradient descent
x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t), 0<=t<n.
Let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite Euclidean dimension, every convex differentiable function f with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star. Let R_n(h) be the same supremum without the numerator factor 2, so W_n(h)=2R_n(h). Put W_n_star=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h), including the empty word at n=0.

Define lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda). For nonnegative real x,y put
Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2,
G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.
Define C_0=B_0=1 and, for EVERY integer n>=1,
C_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
B_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)).
These are the original primitive and dominant benchmark capacities, with EVERY integer split retained. Put U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

For every integer n>=0,
C_n<=(n+1)^rho and B_n<=(12/5)(n+1)^rho.

For each integer m>=1536, set epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, c=lambda(1+epsilon), and a=beta/c. Define the finite words
H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.
The same enclosing m, epsilon, beta, c, and a are used at every child. Here the hierarchy symbols H_j,a,c are local to this statement. Put S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1, and D_m=1+2S_m. Then H_m is a predetermined strictly positive word of length n_m and
B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50
for every integer m>=1536.

Finally, the following is a CONDITIONAL implication, with its entire hypothesis left unproved: IF
W_(n_m)(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) for EVERY integer m>=1536,
as a bound on the full function-class supremum W defined here, THEN for every such m,
B_(n_m) W_(n_m)(H_m)<799/800,
W_(n_m)_star/U_(n_m)^ZJ<799/800,
and
liminf_(n->infinity) W_n_star/U_n^ZJ<=799/800<1.

No unconditional full-function upper bound on W_(n_m)(H_m) is asserted. The unconditional conclusions are the two all-n benchmark envelopes and the hierarchy benchmark ratio. The original unrestricted minimax problem remains unresolved by this result alone.

# R12 fixed-profile certificate handoff

**UNVERIFIED. Grant 1ad8f4912d077d53 complete; stop this invocation. Original mathematical target OPEN.**

## Result

No requested full-PSD certificate exists for either fixed parent Profile A or B on every sufficiently large inherited m. An exact one-dimensional Huber trajectory of the COMPLETE physical H_m gives D_A<0 and D_B<0 for every sufficiently large ODD enclosing m. For every admissible zero-row-sum matrix C with the exact prescribed columns and nonnegative off-diagonal entries, the SAME actual gradient vector consequently satisfies

    g^T R_p(C)g = D_p - sum C_ji Q_ij <= D_p <0,
    p in {A,B}.

This excludes all such C, including arbitrary nonseparable C and non-diagonal full remainders. It is stronger than an interval-mode obstruction, but it is a statement about these fixed dominance functionals only. No original-target proof/disproof or full-class W(H_m) bound was obtained.

## Exact construction and audit chain

For one enclosing m>=1536, retain epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, and the SAME parameters in every child H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j). Define s_j=sum H_j, k_j=a c^j,

    X_1=beta,
    X_j=s_(j-1)+1+k_(j-1)-X_(j-1),  j>=2.

Use the single fixed function f(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=1 and |x|-1/2 otherwise, original minimizer x_star=0, and original start x_0=X_m. The proof establishes k_j>=X_j>0 at EVERY inherited depth. Each complete left child and bridge is saturated with its current sign and reflects to the negative critical child start. The ACTUAL final point is

    x_n=g_n=(-1)^m a beta.

For odd m it is -t, t=a beta>0. All physical gradients except the last are +/-1; the last is +beta. Every Q_star,i=0 identically. Thus, if Z_p=sum_(i=0)^n u_i^p g_i,

    D_p=2X_m Z_p-Z_p^2-Bcert*t^2.

The strict signs follow from fully enclosing-m-uniform scalar asymptotics. With r=sqrt(2)-1 and v=r^2/(1+2sqrt(2)), the proof derives

    X_m/S -> r,
    (sum h_i^2)/S^2 -> v,
    (sum h_i x_(i+1))/S^2 -> v.

For Profile A the exact summation-by-parts identity is

    sum_(i<n)rho_i^A h_i g_i
       =X_m-x_n/2-(sum h_i x_(i+1))/(2S).

Consequently

    Z_A/S -> -9r/68-(2-r)v/4 <0.

For Profile B, physical indices satisfy

    0<=rho_i^A-rho_i^B
       =T_i(S-1-T_i)/[2S(S+1+T_i)]<=r^2/2.

This sign is asserted only for i<n; the terminal rhos have the reverse order. The physical negative-gradient mass divided by S tends to (1-r)/2. Transferring the A bound gives

    limsup_(odd m) Z_B/S
       <=-9r/68-(2-r)v/4+r^2(1-r)/4
       <-19r/272<0.

Both D_p are therefore strictly negative on an unbounded sequence of literal complete words with the original radius and actual last iterate. The explicit moment formulas and uniform error bounds are in PROOF_ATTEMPT.md, Sections 3-6.

## Boundary of the conclusion

The witness's normalized objective is t^2/X_m^2=Theta(S^(-2)), whereas the desired upper bound is of order S^(-1). Thus it does NOT violate the proposed objective upper bound. It invalidates the prescribed gradient-weighted square and the associated dominance residuals. The accepted 931b9653ce0f7201 implication to 799/800 separation still lacks its full-class upper premise.

The original target remains lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ=1 or a rigorous full-class asymptotic separation, with W=2R, exact all-integer U_n^ZJ, every original selected minimizer/radius and actual last iterate. No target, truth store, Goal status, service, worker, configuration or other packet was changed. No fact was submitted, accepted, revoked, or finalized by this call.

The parent relayed the independent primal search's matching trajectory while this call was in progress. This packet independently derives the legal recurrence, the uniform moment asymptotics, both exact signs and the implication for EVERY admissible C. That analytic agreement is not formal verification; all new mathematics remains UNVERIFIED.

The parent subsequently proposed a modified theta=1/8 Profile C only if a second approach remained unused. Both authorized approaches were already exhausted by A and B. This call did not open, analyze or claim a result for that new profile and did not extend its grant.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002341 -->
# R12 fixed-profile matrix certificate: original-trajectory obstruction

**UNVERIFIED analytical draft. Grant 1ad8f4912d077d53. Original target OPEN.**

The requested PSD certificate does not exist for either fixed parent profile on all sufficiently large odd enclosing depths. This draft gives an actual one-dimensional smooth-convex trajectory for the literal complete word H_m on which the specified dominance functional is strictly negative. Consequently, for every matrix C satisfying the requested row, column and off-diagonal conditions, the same actual gradient vector is a strictly negative direction of the FULL remainder R. This is a rejection of these two sufficient dominance profiles, not a counterexample to the proposed full-class objective bound or a resolution of the original minimax problem.

No mathematical program, generated mathematical array, solver, formal submission, extra model, or extra agent was used. There were two analytical approaches: (1) Profile A, including the necessary original-space test of the full matrix construction; (2) transfer of that actual obstruction to the fixed Profile B. No replacement profile is proposed.

## 1. Locked sources and scope

Read completely: OPERATOR.md; the original PROBLEM.md; R11_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md; MATHEMATICAL_ELABORATION_20261009_R11_FINAL.md; R11_BROWNIAN_BRIDGE_DOMINANCE_LEAD_20261009.md; R12_FIXED_DOMINANCE_PROFILES_20261009.md; and the complete R11_VARIABLE_INTERVAL_CERTIFICATE_LEAD_20261009.md, including Section 5. The latter's old positive-cone/physical-diagonal obstruction is not used to infer infeasibility here.

Checked SHA-256 values:

- TASK.md: e05ccb4f67bafd913490af950228e50deefc8f02a8c4949d3d4c040ab1080f57.
- R12_FIXED_DOMINANCE_PROFILES_20261009.md: 277b5d3e3473a358acf98ae9f10c64b063b462195da307c73c1ca0dd0d7439f4.
- R11_BROWNIAN_BRIDGE_DOMINANCE_LEAD_20261009.md: 0fcebe8f462bc029b6a9cf45a1d83abf10854b3f4de386b4bc37d5a01e8d95f7.

The original target is lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ = 1 or a certified full-class asymptotic separation. Here W_n(h) = sup 2(f_n-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over every finite-dimensional convex L-smooth function with an attained minimum, every original selected minimizer, and every distinct initial point. W = 2R. The exact benchmark is U_n^ZJ = 1/[2S_bullet(n)+1], with the all-integer maximization defining S_bullet in PROBLEM.md. No decimal asymptotic constant replaces that sequence.

For each enclosing integer m >= 1536, fix ONCE

    c0 = 1 + sqrt(2), epsilon = 1/(4m), beta = 1-epsilon,
    c = c0(1+epsilon), a = beta/c,
    H_0 = empty, H_(j+1) = (H_j, 1+a c^j, H_j).

Every child uses these same enclosing-m parameters. Set n=2^m-1, h=H_m, S=sum h, T_i=sum_(t<i) h_t, and

    Bcert = 1 + kappa S,       kappa = 32/17.

This local kappa is the doubled coefficient 32/17; it is not the 16/17 symbol used in some earlier projection notes. Bcert is not the exact benchmark denominator.

For Profile A and Profile B respectively,

    rho_i^A = 1-T_i/(2S),
    rho_i^B = (S+1)/(S+1+T_i).

For each profile p, u_i^p=rho_i^p h_i for i<n and u_n^p=Bcert-sum_(i<n)u_i^p. The actual boundary rho_0=1 is retained. The algebraic terminal ell_n=u_n/rho_n is never a physical update.

## 2. The exact matrix necessity being tested

Use the actual smooth-convex rows

    Q_ij = 2f_i-2f_j-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2,
    Q_star,i = -2(f_i-f_star)+2<g_i,x_i-x_star>-||g_i||^2.

All these rows are nonnegative on normalized convex 1-smooth data. Let

    Z_p = sum_(i=0)^n u_i^p g_i.

The prescribed doubled residual is exactly

    D_p = ||x_0-x_star||^2 - sum_i u_i^p Q_star,i
          -2Bcert(f_n-f_star)-||x_0-x_star-Z_p||^2.       (2.1)

For any zero-row-sum C with nonnegative off-diagonal entries and column sums b_i=u_i (i<n), b_n=-sum_(i<n)u_i, the source identity gives

    D_p = sum_(i,j) C_ji Q_ij + g^T R_p(C) g,            (2.2)

where Bmat has its actual physical lower entries h_j and diagonal 1, and

    R_p(C) = K_p+diag(u_i(1-ell_i)+b_i)
             -(C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T).

Therefore one legal trajectory with D_p<0 gives, for EVERY such C,

    g^T R_p(C) g = D_p-sum_(i,j)C_ji Q_ij <= D_p < 0.  (2.3)

This tests the entire non-diagonal matrix. It does not impose individual diagonal signs, use a free-gradient relaxation, or infer anything from positivity of K alone.

The coefficient constraints on C themselves are nonempty: for example, C_ni=u_i for i<n, C_nn=-sum_(i<n)u_i, and all other entries zero satisfy them. Equation (2.3) will exclude PSD for this explicit C and every other admissible C, including signed interval expansions whose final off-diagonal entries are nonnegative.

## 3. One fixed function and an exact complete-hierarchy trajectory

Choose the symmetric Huber function

    f(x) = x^2/2              if |x|<=1,
           |x|-1/2           if |x|>=1.

The function is convex and differentiable, and its derivative g(x)=clip(x,[-1,1]) is 1-Lipschitz. Its unique global minimizer is the ORIGINAL point x_star=0. At every real query x,

    -2f(x)+2x g(x)-g(x)^2 = 0.                         (3.1)

Thus every Q_star,i vanishes on this fixed function. No function, wall, or minimizer is changed during the trajectory.

Write s_j=sum H_j and k_j=a c^j. Define

    X_1=beta,
    X_j=s_(j-1)+1+k_(j-1)-X_(j-1),    j>=2.           (3.2)

We next prove that these starts are legal for every level of the literal inherited word and that

    T_(H_j)(X_j)=(-1)^j t,       t=a beta=beta^2/c.     (3.3)

### 3.1 Exact room for the saturated left child

Put

    alpha = beta/[c(c-2)] = a/(c-2).

Then 0<alpha<1, and direct summation of the physical word gives

    s_j+1 = alpha c^j + (1-alpha)2^j.                  (3.4)

For j>=1 define

    d_j = k_(j+1)-(s_j+1)
        = (beta-alpha)c^j-(1-alpha)2^j.

The first term is strictly positive:

    d_1=beta c-2-a
       =epsilon[2/(c0(1+epsilon))-c0 epsilon] > 0.     (3.5)

Indeed epsilon<=1/6144, c0<5/2 and 1+epsilon<2 make the first term in the bracket greater than 2/5 and the second less than 5/(2*6144). Moreover,

    d_(j+1)=c d_j+(c-2)(1-alpha)2^j > d_j >0.         (3.6)

Let e_j=k_j-X_j. Equation (3.2) gives e_1=0 and

    X_(j+1)=s_j+1+e_j,
    e_(j+1)=d_j-e_j.                                  (3.7)

Inductively 0<=e_j<=d_(j-1) for j>=2: if this holds, then 0<=d_j-e_j<=d_j by (3.6). In particular k_j>=X_j>0 for every j.

### 3.2 Physical forward execution

The base word H_1 contains the one actual step 1+a. Since X_1=beta<1,

    T_(H_1)(X_1)=beta-(1+a)beta=-a beta=-t.

For j>=2, the start is X_j=s_(j-1)+1+e_(j-1). During the entire first H_(j-1), the iterate remains at least 1 and its gradient is exactly 1. The left child ends at 1+e_(j-1). The following bridge has length 1+k_(j-1), also uses gradient 1, and lands exactly at

    1+e_(j-1)-(1+k_(j-1))=-X_(j-1).

The map x -> x-h clip(x,[-1,1]) is odd for every physical step h. Hence the last child is the negative of the already constructed complete H_(j-1) trajectory. This proves (3.3) by induction.

For odd enclosing m, take x_0=X_m. The ORIGINAL squared radius is X_m^2>0, and the ACTUAL terminal point and gradient are

    x_n=g_n=-t,        f_n=t^2/2.                      (3.8)

All physical gradients except the last physical query have absolute value 1. The last physical gradient is (+beta) for odd m, and its step length is h_(n-1)=1+a>1. This description follows by descending the rightmost recursive child until the base H_1; it includes all 2^m-1 physical updates.

## 4. Uniform enclosing-m asymptotics of the actual trajectory

All limits below let the enclosing m tend to infinity while retaining its parameters throughout all children. They are not limits with fixed internal depth followed by an unproved interchange.

Set

    r = sqrt(2)-1 = 1/c0,
    v = r^2/(1+2sqrt(2)) >0.

We prove

    X_m/S -> r,
    V_m/S^2 -> v,        V_j=sum_(h in H_j)h^2,
    M_m/S^2 -> v,        M_j=sum_(i in H_j) h_i x_(i+1), (4.1)

where the M_j path starts at X_j and uses the same enclosing parameters.

### 4.1 The starting point and the physical second moment

Let

    d(c)=(c-1)/(c+1),
    P=alpha c d(c), Q=2(1-alpha)/3, R0=beta-P-Q.

Solving (3.2), with (3.4), gives the exact finite-depth expression

    X_j=P c^(j-1)+Q 2^(j-1)+R0(-1)^(j-1).            (4.2)

For example, the coefficient of c^(j-1) follows from
P(1+1/c)=alpha+beta/c, using beta=alpha c(c-2). This also checks the base X_1=beta.

The physical second moment is exactly

    V_j=(2^j-1)
        +2alpha(c^j-2^j)
        +a^2(c^(2j)-2^j)/(c^2-2).                    (4.3)

The identity can also be checked from V_j=2V_(j-1)+(1+k_(j-1))^2 and V_0=0.

Write

    v(c)=(c-2)^2/(c^2-2).

Since c belongs to the fixed compact interval [c0,c0(1+1/6144)] and alpha is uniformly positive, (3.4), (4.2) and (4.3) imply, with constants uniform in the enclosing m and all 1<=j<=m,

    s_j=alpha c^j+O(2^j),
    X_j=d(c)alpha c^j+O(2^j),
    V_j=v(c)alpha^2 c^(2j)+O(c^j+2^j).              (4.4)

At j=m, the relative errors tend to zero because 2/c<=2/c0<1. As c->c0 and beta->1, d(c)->r and v(c)->v. This proves the first two limits in (4.1), and t->r.

### 4.2 Exact first moment of the physical iterates

For j>=2 abbreviate s=s_(j-1), k=k_(j-1). The left child has x_(i+1)=X_j-T_i-h_i, so its contribution to M_j is

    s X_j-(s^2+V_(j-1))/2.

The bridge contributes -(1+k)X_(j-1). The final child is the negative of the previous critical trajectory. Therefore

    M_j+M_(j-1)
      =s X_j-(s^2+V_(j-1))/2-(1+k)X_(j-1).           (4.5)

The exact base is M_1=-(1+a)a beta. Negative small-depth values are allowed; no positivity of the moment has been assumed.

Substitute (4.4) into (4.5) and use the exact equality
k_(j-1)=(c-2)alpha c^(j-1). The leading term is

    F(c)alpha^2 c^(2j-2),
    F(c)=2d(c)-(1+v(c))/2,

and the error is O((2c)^j), uniformly over the same compact parameter set. Define

    w(c)=F(c)/(c^2+1).

Then E_j=M_j-w(c)alpha^2 c^(2j) satisfies

    E_j+E_(j-1)=O((2c)^j).

Summing this scalar recurrence with absolute values gives E_j=O((2c)^j), with a uniform constant: the geometric ratio 2c is uniformly greater than 1, and the base is uniformly bounded. Thus

    M_m/S^2=w(c)+O((2/c)^m) -> w(c0).                (4.6)

Finally w(c0)=v. One exact check is

    v=(8sqrt(2)-11)/7,
    4r-1=v(2c0^2+3),

which is equivalent to 2r-(1+v)/2=v(c0^2+1). This proves the last limit in (4.1). Every error estimate above is uniform in the actual enclosing-m parameters.

## 5. Approach 1: Profile A fails on that original trajectory

Denote its physical weight and weighted physical gradient by

    U_A=sum_(i<n) rho_i^A h_i,
    G_A=sum_(i<n) rho_i^A h_i g_i.

The fixed-profile identity gives

    U_A=3S/4+V_m/(4S),
    u_n^A=1+kappa S-U_A.                             (5.1)

The actual GD recursion h_i g_i=x_i-x_(i+1) and a finite summation by parts give

    G_A=X_m-x_n/2-M_m/(2S).                          (5.2)

To check the endpoint coefficient, rho_i-rho_(i-1)=-h_(i-1)/(2S) and rho_n=1/2. Using M_m including its final term h_(n-1)x_n yields precisely (5.2), without adding a terminal step.

On the odd-m trajectory x_n=g_n=-t, put

    Z_A=G_A-t u_n^A.

Equations (4.1), (5.1) and (5.2) give the exact limiting coefficient

    Z_A/S -> r-v/2-r(kappa-3/4-v/4)
             = -9r/68-(2-r)v/4 <0.                  (5.3)

No decimal sign test is needed: r>0, v>0 and r<1. Consequently Z_A<0 for every sufficiently large odd enclosing m.

By (3.1), (3.8) and (2.1),

    D_A=X_m^2-Bcert t^2-(X_m-Z_A)^2
       =2X_m Z_A-Z_A^2-Bcert t^2 <0.                (5.4)

All terms use the original radius X_m^2 and the actual terminal iterate. The strict failure is at the COMPLETE depth m, not at an internal fixed level. In fact its leading negative term is of order S^2, since X_m/S->r and Z_A/S has a strictly negative limit.

By (2.3), there is no admissible C with R_A(C) PSD for every sufficiently large enclosing m. For each of the infinitely many odd m in (5.4), the actual original gradient vector is a negative direction of R_A(C) for every admissible C.

## 6. Approach 2: the fixed Profile B also fails

Use the SAME physical function, word, original start/minimizer and odd-m trajectory. For physical indices i<n set

    delta_i=rho_i^A-rho_i^B
           =T_i(S-1-T_i)/[2S(S+1+T_i)].              (6.1)

Since the last physical step is 1+a>1, every such T_i satisfies
0<=T_i<=S-(1+a)<S-1. Hence delta_i>=0. This statement is restricted to physical indices: rho_n^B>rho_n^A, and no false terminal inequality is used.

For z=T_i/S in [0,1],

    delta_i <= 1-z/2-1/(1+z)
              =z(1-z)/[2(1+z)] <= r^2/2.           (6.2)

The last maximum occurs at z=r, since the derivative has the sign of 1-2z-z^2. This is an exact uniform bound.

Let Delta=sum_(i<n)delta_i h_i. Then U_B=U_A-Delta and u_n^B=u_n^A+Delta. Therefore

    Z_B-Z_A=-sum_(i<n)delta_i h_i(g_i+t)
            <= (r^2/2) N_minus,                     (6.3)

where N_minus=sum_(g_i<0)h_i(-g_i). For (6.3), terms with g_i>=0 are nonpositive, and a term with g_i<0 is at most delta_i h_i(-g_i). No equality between the profiles' terminal rhos is presumed.

On the odd-m trajectory all physical gradients have magnitude 1 except the last, which is +beta. Thus

    sum_(i<n)h_i |g_i|=S-(1+a)(1-beta),
    sum_(i<n)h_i g_i=X_m+t,

and consequently

    N_minus=[S-(1+a)(1-beta)-X_m-t]/2,
    N_minus/S -> (1-r)/2.                            (6.4)

Combining (5.3), (6.3) and (6.4),

    limsup_(odd m) Z_B/S
       <= -9r/68-(2-r)v/4+r^2(1-r)/4
       < -9r/68+r/16
        = -19r/272 <0.                              (6.5)

Here r(1-r)<1/4 because r is in (0,1) and is not 1/2; the additional negative v-term is simply retained or discarded in the valid direction.

It follows that Z_B<0 for every sufficiently large odd enclosing m. The same original-radius identity gives

    D_B=2X_m Z_B-Z_B^2-Bcert t^2<0.                  (6.6)

Thus (2.3) also rules out every admissible full-PSD C for Profile B at those complete depths.

## 7. What this settles and what remains open

The new statement is a complete analytic obstruction to the TWO SPECIFIED fixed dominance functionals and therefore to their full matrix certificate targets. It covers arbitrary admissible C, not merely the old positive interval dictionary, diagonal H, a separable factorization, or independent diagonal slack. It supplies a real one-dimensional function and every physical query through an exact recursive execution. No freely assigned gradient table is used.

It does not establish W(H_m)>1/Bcert. Indeed this witness has terminal objective t^2/2 while its original squared radius X_m^2 is of order S^2, so its normalized objective is of order S^(-2), much smaller than the proposed S^(-1) upper bound. The failing part is the particular prescribed gradient-weighted square in D_u. The argument therefore supplies no negative answer to the original minimax conjecture and no refutation of the possible full-class upper bound for H_m.

The conditional implication involving accepted fact 931b9653ce0f7201 remains conditional: a genuine full-class bound W(H_m)<=1/Bcert would imply the stated 799/800 subsequence separation, but neither profile proves that premise. No signed fact, TARGET, Goal status, original minimizer, benchmark, physical word, shared truth store, or other packet was changed.

The parent relayed the independent primal search's same critical trajectory during this call. The recurrence, legal saturated path, uniform moment limits, both profile signs and their consequence (2.3) were checked analytically here. This is not an independent model-backed formal verification and remains UNVERIFIED under the Danus contract.

## 8. Resource and stop record

- Native research invocations: 1, this fresh grant only; requested model/effort gpt-6-astra/ultra.
- Substantive analytical approaches: 2, Profile A and the permitted Profile B transfer.
- Mathematical programs, generated mathematical arrays, solvers, formal submissions: 0 each.
- Extra agents/models, service/worker changes, old grants reused, shared administration: 0 each.
- File operations: source reads, the two permitted Markdown outputs, and SHA-256/readback checks only.
- No per-call token receipt is exposed here; none is invented.

Stop after writing PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and HANDOFF.md and reporting their final hashes. The original target remains OPEN; these fixed profiles should not receive further coefficient tuning as if feasible.

Handle rho_0=1 as the actual covariance boundary. The second allowed approach may use Profile B, rho_i=(S+1)/(S+1+T_i), if A fails or a materially different constructive route is needed. is PSD for all sufficiently large inheritedm. HereK_ij=ell_iell_j(min(rho_i,rho_j)-rho_i rho_j),ell_i=h_i fori<n,ell_n=u_n/rho_n is only an algebraic weight, and Bmat is the ACTUAL physical trajectory matrix. The exact D_u expansion is in the source note. Positivity ofK alone says nothing about the sign ofR. Do not replace fullPSD with nonnegative individual diagonal slack.

One concrete construction option is a positive interval-mode combination with the NEW b_i and signed diagonal remainder, followed by a proof of K+diag(mu)>=0 using its finite integral/partial-sum representation. Another is an original-row C with a non-diagonalH and an explicit sum-of-squares factorization. In either case provide actual coefficients and uniform sign estimates, not another optimization problem. The methods are suggestions, not premises. A new negative free-vector direction is only a sufficient-family obstruction unless it can be realized by a function.

The coefficient target is Bcert=1+(32/17)S. A true D_u>=0 yields the full original-class W(H_m)<=1/Bcert<=17/[16(1+2S)]. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 then gives a strict799/800 subsequence separation from the EXACT benchmark. No original-target premise is currently proved. An independent primal search is testing the already fixed parent D_u profiles; it is not auditing your unfinished C. If a genuine whole-hierarchy profile counterexample arrives, use it to stop that exact profile and spend any remaining approach only on the other permitted explicit law. Do not expand the call or create an agent.

# Selected C107 theorem package

## Statement

Exact fixed-b=3 benchmark thresholds and conditional full-certificate amplification for five- and six-step seeds.

For a nonnegative integer n and a finite nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), let W_n(h) be the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)

over all finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, all convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and at least one minimizer x_star, and all x_0!=x_star, under x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Here L>0 is arbitrary and fixed; the empty schedule is allowed. This is the W=2R normalization, where R is the same supremum without the factor2.

For nonnegative real a,d define

    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,
    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.

Retain the ORIGINAL ALL-split benchmark recurrences

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),  integer n>=1,
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,
    lambda=1+sqrt(2).

Every split index k is an integer. For every positive integer b let Z_b be the positive finite limit lim_(j->infinity)lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1), established in predecessor04219554e41d9750. Define

    Gamma5_c107=7977607/806000,
    Gamma6_c107=387153999/32240000,

and let Gamma_r_c107 denote Gamma5_c107 for r=5 and Gamma6_c107 for r=6. With the denominator ray FIXED at b=3, the strict comparisons are

    Z_18/Z_3 < Gamma5_c107 < 99/10 < 292666/29275,
    Z_21/Z_3 < Gamma6_c107 < 1201/100 < 90/7.

The following assertions are conditional. Fix r in {5,6}. Suppose h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)) is an ACTUAL nonnegative seed word with s=sum_(i=0)^(r-1)h_i and capacity A=1+s>Gamma_r_c107. Suppose there is a full real (r+1)-by-(r+1) array Lambda with Lambda_ii=0 and Lambda_ij>=0 for EVERY ordered distinct i,j in {0,...,r}. Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1; let the real matrix T have T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i. Set

    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).

Require

    L_flow^T e=omega,
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2
        positive semidefinite on the entire R^(r+1).

Here diag of a column is the diagonal matrix with that column, and a superscript T on a matrix or column means transpose. Thus r=5 requires all30 ordered multipliers and all6 formal gradients, and r=6 requires all42 multipliers and all7 formal gradients. A zero physical step permits neither deletion nor equality of formal gradient coordinates. No rank, support, symmetry, or zero-remainder restriction is imposed.

For each nonnegative integer n choose ANY fully expanded maximizing tree of the original B_n recurrence, expanding every C child by the original C recurrence. Replace each of its n primitive empty leaves by the actual word h, retain every internal node, and leave its one terminal dominant empty leaf EMPTY with mass1. Recompute each physical bridge as follows. A primitive join, with actual child capacities A_left=1+sum(left) and A_right=1+sum(right), uses the unique positive d_pp solving d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left*A_right and concatenates (left,1+d_pp,right). A dominant join, with primitive left capacity A_left and actual dominant right mass D_right=1+2sum(right), uses zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2 and concatenates (left,1+A_left/zeta,right). Write Hsub_n for this actual physical word and Dsub_n=1+2sum Hsub_n for its mass.

For every n>=0 the accepted full substitution identities give

    length(Hsub_n)=(r+1)n,
    W_((r+1)n)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n,
    Dsub_n>=A B_n(1-ell_n)^2,
    ell_n=(1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_n).

The equality is an all-function objective upper certificate with a matching one-dimensional Huber lower witness. For every nonnegative integer j put

    n_j=3*2^j-1,
    N_j=(r+1)n_j=3(r+1)2^j-(r+1),
    q107_j=(2/3)^j,
    epsilon_c107=1-Gamma_r_c107/A in (0,1).

At these TRUE physical horizons,

    lim_(j->infinity) B_(N_j)W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
        =Z_(3(r+1))/(A Z_3)<1.

For every j with q107_j<1/3 one has the finite bound

    B_(N_j)W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
        < (Gamma_r_c107/A)/(1-3q107_j).

In particular, for EVERY integer

    j>=ceil(log(6/epsilon_c107)/log(3/2)),

where log is the natural logarithm,

    W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) < (1-epsilon_c107/2) U_(N_j)^ZJ.

Each of the following separate sufficient hypotheses gives the uniform strict bound W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) < (1-1/16000)U_(N_j)^ZJ for EVERY integer j>=27:

    r=5 and A>=99/10, with N_j=18*2^j-6;
    r=6 and A>=1201/100, with N_j=21*2^j-7.

This theorem supplies sufficient upper enclosures for this fixed ray and is conditional on an actual full seed. It supplies no such seed, proves no seed nonexistence, and asserts no global phase optimum, no ordering between the TRUE b=1 and b=3 ratios, and no unrestricted all-n/all-nonnegative-word exact-profile theorem. The last two comparisons to292666/29275 and90/7 are numerical comparisons of displayed constants, not an invocation or acceptance of another seed-threshold draft.

## Proof

# Exact fixed-integer-ray thresholds for five- and six-step seeds

The sole mathematical predecessor is fact04219554e41d9750, specifically
its proof Sections2--3 and7--13. Every reference below to the accepted
fact or an accepted section means that predecessor. Its general-length
full-seed substitution and saved endpoint are used in their proved scope.
All new scalar comparisons and constant checks are proved jointly below.
No unverified finding or analytical audit is a mathematical premise.

## 1. Statement and original benchmark

For a nonnegative integer n and a nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h)
is the supremum of

  2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)

over every finite Euclidean dimension, every convex differentiable f
with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Here L>0; this is W=2R.

For a,d>=0 put

  Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,
  G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.

Retain the ORIGINAL ALL-split recurrences

  C_0=B_0=1,
  C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
  B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
  U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,   lambda=1+sqrt(2),   rho=log_2(lambda).

The accepted fact defines the positive finite limits

  Z_b=lim_(j->infinity) lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1),  integer b>=1.

Freeze b=3 for BOTH lengths before making any limiting comparison. Define

  Gamma5_c107=7977607/806000,
  Gamma6_c107=387153999/32240000.

For r in {5,6}, Gamma_r_c107 means the corresponding displayed constant.
Every dyadic depth j below is a nonnegative integer.

The new strict sufficient bounds are

  Z_18/Z_3 < Gamma5_c107 < 99/10 < 292666/29275,             (1)
  Z_21/Z_3 < Gamma6_c107 < 1201/100 < 90/7.                (2)

Consequently, CONDITIONAL on an actual full-certified primitive seed
of length r in {5,6} and capacity A=1+sum h>Gamma_r_c107, the original
substitution from the accepted fact, with n_j=3*2^j-1, has actual horizon

  N_j=(r+1)(3*2^j-1).

Let epsilon_c107=1-Gamma_r_c107/A, so 0<epsilon_c107<1. For EVERY integer

  j >= ceil(log(6/epsilon_c107)/log(3/2)),                  (3)

the substituted physical word satisfies

  W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) < (1-epsilon_c107/2) U_(N_j)^ZJ.    (4)

In particular each of the following separate sufficient conditions gives
the uniform strict margin 1/16000 for EVERY j>=27:

  r=5, A>=99/10,      N_j=18*2^j-6;
  r=6, A>=1201/100,   N_j=21*2^j-7.                      (5)

The full seed certificate meant here is stated in Section6. The constants
are upper enclosures, not a lower barrier or a global optimum over rays.
In particular (1)--(2) compare sufficient bounds; they do NOT establish
that the true ratios at b=3 are below the true ratios at b=1.

## 2. All-split comparison with primitive capacities

We prove jointly that

  G(a,lambda d)<=lambda Omega(a,d)   for all a,d>=0.       (6)

When a=d=0 both sides are zero. Otherwise put w=Omega(a,d)>0 and t=a/w.
The equation w^2-(a+d)w-ad=0 gives

  0<=t<=1,    d/w=(1-t)/(1+t).

The larger root of P(v)=v^2-(4a+lambda d)v+4a^2 is G(a,lambda d).
Since lambda^2=2lambda+1, substitution and expansion give

  P(lambda w)/w^2
    =lambda^2-4lambda t-lambda^2*(1-t)/(1+t)+4t^2
    =4t*(t-1/sqrt(2))^2/(1+t) >=0.

Also w>=a+d, so lambda w>=lambda(a+d)>=2a+lambda d/2,
the vertex of P. A quadratic of positive leading coefficient is
nonnegative to the right of its vertex only at or beyond its larger
root. This proves (6), including either zero argument.

Both kernels are increasing in each nonnegative argument directly from
their displayed formulas. Since B_0=1<=lambda C_0, induction over every
ORIGINAL split k+l=n-1 gives

  G(C_k,B_l)<=G(C_k,lambda C_l)
             <=lambda Omega(C_k,C_l)<=lambda C_n.

Taking the maximum over all splits proves B_n<=lambda C_n. The accepted
symmetric identity C_(b*2^j-1)=lambda^j C_(b-1) therefore implies

  Z_b<=lambda C_(b-1),  for every fixed integer b>=1.      (7)

No optimizing split for B_n has been selected or discarded.

## 3. Exact discrete primitive identities and rational enclosures

The accepted fact's Section2 gives the balanced identities for U_N=C_(N-1),
with U_0=0:

  U_(2a)=lambda U_a,
  U_(2a+1)=Omega(U_a,U_(a+1)).

They are statements about the symmetric ALL-split recurrence, not a
balanced assumption about B. Set the following new local quantities:

  c107=C_2=Omega(1,lambda),
  p107=C_4=Omega(lambda,c107),
  u107=C_8=Omega(lambda^2,p107),
  v107=C_10=Omega(p107,lambda*c107),
  w107=C_20=Omega(lambda*p107,v107).

The identities also give C_5=lambda*c107, C_9=lambda*p107, and
C_17=lambda*u107. Thus (7) yields

  Z_18<=lambda^2*u107,     Z_21<=lambda*w107.              (8)

Here are complete rational upper checks. First

  12/5<lambda<169/70,   lambda^2=2lambda+1<204/35,

because (7/5)^2<2<(99/70)^2. For positive a,d define

  P_(a,d)(x)=x^2-(a+d)x-ad.

Its positive root is Omega(a,d). Each proposed x in the table below
exceeds a+d; hence P_(a,d)(x)>0 proves x>Omega(a,d).

quantity    a                 d                  x          P_(a,d)(x)
c107        1                 169/70             502/125    2405/1093750
p107        169/70            502/125            1923/250   4084/437500
u107        204/35            1923/250           407/25     3943/43750
v107        1923/250          42419/4375         419/20     854258/17500000
w107        324987/17500      419/20             23841/500  804318/8750000

The exact substitutions used in the last two rows are

  lambda*c107 < (169/70)(502/125)=42419/4375,
  lambda*p107 < (169/70)(1923/250)=324987/17500.

For an explicit audit of the larger rows, the u107 numerator on
denominator43750 is

  11595430-9630027-1961460=3943.

The v107 numerator on denominator17500000 is

  7680793750-6374791700-1305147792=854258.

The w107 numerator on denominator8750000 is

  19893764835-16488721692-3404238825=804318.

All entries are strictly positive. Monotonicity and the preceding exact
identities prove, in particular,

  u107<407/25,     w107<23841/500.                         (9)

We will also need c107>4. The polynomial defining c107 has value
16-4(1+lambda)-lambda=12-5lambda<0 at4, so its positive root exceeds4.

## 4. The accepted finite endpoint gives the denominator

Use ONLY the saved endpoint in accepted04219554e41d9750 Sections8--9:

  D80=1208925819614629174706176,
  L3071=77954524439662110401113475160,
  B_3071>=L3071/D80,
  L3071-64000D80=583271984325843219918211160.

No part of the defining DP or certificate check is rerun. Subtracting the
displayed integer480D80 gives the purely textual comparison

  L3071-64480D80=2987590910821216059246680>0.               (10)

The accepted lower ray enclosure at b=3,j=10 has finite index3071,
so

  Z_3>=lambda^(-10) B_3071
     >=lambda^(-10) L3071/D80
      >64480/lambda^10.                                  (11)

The elementary power identities

  lambda^5=41+29sqrt(2),
  lambda^6=99+70sqrt(2),
  lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2),
  lambda^12=19601+13860sqrt(2)

follow by multiplication using lambda^2=2lambda+1. Since the conjugates
of the even powers are their positive reciprocals,

  lambda^10+lambda^(-10)=6726,
  lambda^12+lambda^(-12)=39202.

It follows that

  lambda^10<6726,
  lambda^12<39202,
  lambda^11<6726*(169/70)=1136694/70<16239.               (12)

Equations (8)--(12) now give

  Z_18/Z_3 < lambda^12*u107/64480
            < 39202*(407/25)/64480
             =7977607/806000=Gamma5_c107,

  Z_21/Z_3 < lambda^11*w107/64480
            < 16239*(23841/500)/64480
             =387153999/32240000=Gamma6_c107.             (13)

The strict comparisons to the convenient capacities are

  99/10-Gamma5_c107=1793/806000>0,
  1201/100-Gamma6_c107=48401/32240000>0.

The improvement over the previously sufficient b=1 bounds needs no
assumption that those unverified proofs are accepted:

  292666/29275-99/10=28435/292750>0,
  90/7-1201/100=593/700>0.

This proves (1)--(2) as one joint argument from the accepted input.

## 5. Quantitative approach to the fixed denominator ray

The accepted Section9 gives, with n_j=3*2^j-1,

  0<=Z_3-lambda^(-j)B_(n_j)<=E(3,j),

  E(3,j)=8sqrt(2)*3^(rho-1)*2^(-j)
          +(3/25)*4^(rho/2)*lambda^(-j/2)
          +(9/10000)*lambda^(-j).

Define q107_j=(2/3)^j. The accepted bound1<rho<4/3 implies
3^(rho-1)<3/2, since3<(3/2)^3. Also sqrt(2)<3/2,
4^(rho/2)=lambda<5/2, and sqrt(lambda)>3/2. Consequently

  E(3,j)
    <18*2^(-j)+(3/10)*lambda^(-j/2)+(1/1000)*lambda^(-j)
    <=(18+3/10+1/1000)q107_j<19q107_j.                   (14)

Equation (11) and lambda^10<6726 give

  Z_3>64480/6726>19/2,

where the last comparison is128960>127794. Hence

  B_(n_j)>lambda^j Z_3(1-2q107_j).                       (15)

The accepted lower comparison B_n>=C_n and the exact symmetric identity
give the second useful estimate

  B_(n_j)>=C_(n_j)=lambda^j*c107>4lambda^j.              (16)

Neither estimate evaluates a new B value.

## 6. Actual seed requirement and the physical output

Fix r in {5,6}. The hypothesis is an ACTUAL nonnegative word
h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)), s=sum h_i and A=1+s>Gamma_r_c107, together with
nonnegative real Lambda_ij for EVERY ordered distinct i,j in{0,...,r}
and Lambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1 and set

  T_ii=1,   T_it=h_t for t<i,   T_it=0 for t>i,
  L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
  omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
  D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).

Require the FULL original-coordinate conditions

  L_flow^T e=omega,
  M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0 on R^(r+1).

This means30 multipliers and all6 formal gradients for r=5, or42
multipliers and all7 formal gradients for r=6. A zero physical step
does not permit deletion or equality of its formal gradient coordinate.
No rank, support, symmetry or remainder-zero condition is imposed.

Accepted04219554e41d9750 Sections10--13 applies for these general lengths.
For each n choose any fully expanded maximizing tree of the ORIGINAL
B_n recurrence. Replace its n primitive empty leaves by this actual h;
leave its single terminal dominant empty leaf empty. Retain all internal
nodes and recompute the physical bridges specified in that accepted fact:

* A primitive join with capacities A_left,A_right uses bridge1+d_pp,
  where d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left*A_right.
* A dominant join with primitive capacity A_left and right mass D_right
  uses bridge1+A_left/zeta, where
  zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2.

Use the accepted names Hsub_n for the resulting word and Dsub_n for
its mass1+2sum Hsub_n. Its conclusions, including payment for the
untouched dominant leaf and the Huber lower witness, are

  length(Hsub_n)=(r+1)n,
  W_((r+1)n)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n,
  Dsub_n>=A B_n(1-ell_n)^2,
  ell_n=(1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_n).

These are cited accepted results, not new composition claims.

For n=n_j, (16) gives 0<=ell_(n_j)<q107_j/2. Also

  N_j=(r+1)n_j=3(r+1)2^j-(r+1).

By monotonicity of B and its accepted lower ray enclosure at
b=3(r+1),

  B_(N_j)<=B_(3(r+1)2^j-1)<=lambda^j Z_(3(r+1)).          (17)

Thus the finite output is compared at its TRUE horizon; no offset is
silently removed. For q107_j<1/3, (15)--(17) and (13) give

  B_(N_j) W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
     < (Gamma_r_c107/A)
        /[(1-2q107_j)(1-q107_j/2)^2]
     <=(Gamma_r_c107/A)/(1-3q107_j),                     (18)

because

  (1-2q)(1-q/2)^2=1-3q+(9/4)q^2-(1/2)q^3>=1-3q

for0<=q<1/3. The already accepted shifted-ray statement also gives the
exact conditional limiting ratio

  B_(N_j) W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) -> Z_(3(r+1))/(A Z_3),

with the same finite offset. The finite bound (18) is stronger than an
unspecified eventuality.

If q107_j<=epsilon_c107/6, (18) implies

  B_(N_j) W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
    <(1-epsilon_c107)/(1-epsilon_c107/2)
    <1-epsilon_c107/2.

The final strict inequality follows by subtracting: the square
(1-epsilon_c107/2)^2 exceeds1-epsilon_c107 byepsilon_c107^2/4.
Condition (3) ensures exactly q107_j<=epsilon_c107/6, proving (4).

For the convenient capacities in (5), the respective lower margins are

  1-Gamma5_c107/(99/10)=1793/7979400>1/8000,
  1-Gamma6_c107/(1201/100)=48401/387202400>1/8000.

For the second strict comparison,
48401*8000-387202400=5600>0; the first follows from
1793*8000=14344000>7979400. Increasing A only increases epsilon_c107.
Finally

  (3/2)^27=((3/2)^10)^2*(3/2)^7
           >57^2*17=55233>48000,

using59049>57*1024 and2187>17*128. Thus for EVERY j>=27,
q107_j<1/48000<epsilon_c107/6. Equation (4) gives the claimed strict
factor1-1/16000 at the respective actual horizons.

## 7. Scope

No seed existence, all-word lower bound, global ray optimum, or ordering
of the true b=1 and b=3 ratios is inferred. Dyadic rescaling alone is
not a new phase: the definition gives Z_(2b)=lambda Z_b, hence preserves
Z_((r+1)b)/Z_b. Nothing here asserts density or continuity of phases.

The conditional consequence requires an actual seed satisfying Section6.
No pointwise domination claim about another seed family is a premise.
This theorem does not supply such a seed, prove its nonexistence, or
settle the original all-n/all-nonnegative-word exact-profile problem.
