max2 round53 COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED actual-distance result, scoped to zero/one checkpoint: on every H_(m,tau) with0<tau<=1/4,m>=1536, EVERY eligible single checkpoint of BOTH static spiral realizations, over ALL independent kappa>0,0<epsilon<=1/2, has true closest-distance payoff <441/500 of Huber. Thus the actual zero-or-one-checkpoint optimum equals Huber, retaining finite benchmark deficits<237/250 at tau1/4 and<79/80 uniformly on[1/5,1/4]. Full minimizer sets/rows are rederived, the one-source-stage projection is solved exactly, and two actual tests delimit the multiple-stage interface. No all-count bound, normalized escape, fact acceptance, or original-target solution.

# Actual spiral distances and a complete one-checkpoint comparison

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL CANDIDATE, UNVERIFIED.
Worker max2, assigned round53, under TASK.md and
master_guidance2915a98c60a7ec96; plan fc3c0a7f0e845447.
No fact_submit, mathematical program, numerical evaluation, solver,
executed recurrence, installation, consult, or sub-agent was used.
All new proofs below belong to this single joint candidate. They are
not independently verified premises and are not accepted facts.

The sole accepted mathematical input is fact_id04219554e41d9750,
restricted to its original benchmark ray enclosure and its saved exact
table. No benchmark was recomputed. The explicit geometric source is
unverified finding c8fcf4ced5a34046, read at
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/c8fcf4ced5a34046.md,
SHA2564d1e99db853eba3751a3f4761fc2c400c68eacdc9c7912b17c0b0c82d6975d2e.
It is provenance, not a correctness premise: all geometry used here is
proved jointly. The earlier scalar obstruction717b541e05f4484f was read
in its exact reduced/specified-radius scope, and is not used as a lemma.

## 1. The precise scope and conclusions

Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be a finite nonnegative physical word. For a
convex differentiable function f with 1-Lipschitz gradient and attained
minimum, the actual trajectory is x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t). Its
closest-distance payoff is

    E(f,h)=2(f(x_n)-min f)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2.          (1)

The symbol W_n(h) denotes the supremum of this payoff over all such
functions, initial points with positive distance, and finite Euclidean
dimensions. None of the upper bounds below is an upper bound on W_n.

This manuscript analyzes the SAME two explicitly defined static spiral
realizations as the source, at the fixed advertised smoothness L=1.
We derive their full minimizer sets for every checkpoint count, their
exact or finite-projection nearest-distance structure, and their actual
terminal payoffs. The comparison theorem is deliberately restricted to
ZERO OR ONE selected checkpoint. It includes every eligible position,
every independent parameter pair kappa>0, 0<epsilon<=1/2, and every
actual ordered block at that position. There is no assertion about the
optimized family with two or more checkpoints.

For lambda=1+sqrt(2), fix 0<tau<=1/4 and an integer m>=1536. Define

    beta=1-tau/m, c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    H_(m,tau)=H_m, n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j.             (2)

Every occurrence of a child H_j uses this SAME enclosing pair (m,tau).
Let H_base(h)=1/(1+2 sum h). The joint comparison conclusion is:

    E(f,h)<(441/500) H_base(h)                         (3)

for EVERY one-checkpoint realization of EITHER kind below on
h=H_(m,tau), using that realization's own eligibility threshold.
The two zero-checkpoint realizations have payoffs H_base(h) and
1/[2(1+sum h)], respectively. Consequently the supremum over these
actual zero-or-one-checkpoint functions, normalized by their TRUE
closest-minimizer distances, is exactly

    L_actual,<=1(H_(m,tau))=H_base(H_(m,tau)).           (4)

In particular the normalization improvement does not repair the
small-tau benchmark deficit in this precisely delimited subfamily:

    B_(n_m) L_actual,<=1(H_(m,1/4))<237/250,
    B_(n_m) L_actual,<=1(H_(m,tau))<79/80
                              if 1/5<=tau<=1/4.       (5)

For fixed 0<tau<=1/4 the exact limit is Z_1/(2 exp(tau)).
B_n and Z_1 are the ORIGINAL accepted benchmark quantities defined in
Section9. These conclusions do not reuse the old reduced-channel
estimate to upper-bound actual payoffs.

Two actual boundary tests delimit the argument. With two source-format
checkpoints, copying the one-checkpoint radius can overstate its square
by a factor of five. Also two ordinary blocks with the same sum can
have different backward P,Q outputs. Neither test is an escape on the
small-tau family or a counterexample to the full construction.

## 2. Arc, backward chronology, and actual displacement

Fix independent 0<epsilon<=1/2 and kappa>0. Write

    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    C=c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa theta_sp),
    R(theta)=C exp(-kappa theta),
    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)),
    n(theta)=e(theta)+kappa t(theta), 0<=theta<=theta_sp.

C here is an arc constant, distinct from the benchmark C_n.
The endpoints are v(0)=(C,0) and v(theta_sp)=(epsilon,-1).
Let K be the convex hull of 0 and this compact arc. For
delta=phi-theta,

    <n(theta),v(phi)>/R(theta)
         =exp(-kappa delta)(cos delta+kappa sin delta).

Its derivative is -(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa delta)sin delta, so its
maximum on the relevant interval (-pi/2,pi/2) is1 at delta=0.
Its value at the extra point0 is0. Thus n(theta) supports K at
v(theta). The vector (0,1) also supports K at v(0).
Furthermore

    p'=-R(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,
    q'=R(cos theta-kappa sin theta),
    p>=epsilon>0, q>=0, p>=epsilon q.                 (6)

For an actual chronological ordinary block alpha_1,...,alpha_r,
alpha_j>=0, construct backward states as follows. Set
r_r=v_r=(epsilon,-1). Given r_j=(u_j,-y_j) and its projected arc
point v_j=v(theta_j), let alpha=alpha_j. If

    y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)C],                        (7)

put v_(j-1)=v(0), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(0). Otherwise take the unique
theta in (0,theta_j] satisfying

    <r_j,e(theta)-kappa^(-1)t(theta)>/R(theta)=1-alpha, (8)

and put v_(j-1)=v(theta), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(theta).
These are exact implicit definitions, never executed root searches.

Here is a full projection and existence proof. Inductively

    u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon,           (9)

and, before a cap,

    r_j=R(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e(theta_j)+kappa t_j t(theta_j)],
    t_j>=0.                                           (10)

They hold at the endpoint with t_r=0. In (7), set
mu=u_j+(alpha-1)C. Then mu>=y_j/kappa>0 and
r_(j-1)-v(0)=mu n(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j), a nonnegative combination
of supporting normals. This proves projection at the cap. The new
inequality y_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-C) makes every earlier step capped.

If (7) fails, the point is uncapped. The left side of (8) equals1
at theta_j, is less than1-alpha at0, and has strictly positive
derivative (kappa+kappa^(-1))(u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R(theta).
Thus the stated root exists and is unique, including alpha=0 at the
current endpoint. The polar angle of r_j is at least theta_j by(10),
so <r_j,t(theta)> >=0 at this earlier angle. Equation(8) makes
r_j+(alpha-1)v(theta) a nonnegative multiple of n(theta), proving
projection and(10) at the preceding state. Adding alpha(p,-q), using
(6), preserves(9). No chronological step or zero is removed.

For the actual outputs define

    P=sum_j alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),
    Q=sum_j alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)), s=sum_j alpha_j.

Then

    r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), P>=epsilon Q, 0<=P<=Cs.      (11)

We also need the uniform Q bound used to define the source's scales.
Set d_sp=1-kappa^2 and

    T_sp=(exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d_sp, 0<kappa<1,
    T_sp=theta_sp/kappa,                         kappa>=1,
    H_sp=C[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]-1,
    A_sp=epsilon+(1+H_sp)/epsilon.                    (12)

For consecutive uncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new with difference
Delta, resolving the exact backward update along t(theta_old) gives

    t_old=B_D+A_D t_new,
    A_D=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B_D=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.

Both are nonnegative. Differentiation gives
A_D'-(d_sp/kappa)A_D=-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.
Thus A_D<=exp(d_sp Delta/kappa). If kappa<1, B_D<=Delta/kappa and
1+d_sp B_D<=exp(d_sp Delta/kappa); telescope to get t_j<=T_sp.
If kappa>=1, use A_D<=1 and B_D<=Delta/kappa to get the same bound
with its second definition. Consequently
y_j=R[(1+t_j)sin theta_j+kappa t_j cos theta_j]
<=C[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]. A cap leaves y unchanged, so

    0<=Q<=H_sp.                                      (13)

The exact maximum of q is

    qmax=1, kappa<=epsilon;
    qmax=sqrt((1+epsilon^2)/(1+kappa^2))
         exp(kappa[theta_sp-arctan(1/kappa)]), kappa>epsilon.

This follows from the sign of q' in(6). Define

    N_sp=max(0,kappa qmax-epsilon),
    B_sp=1+max(epsilon^(-2),N_sp/epsilon).              (14)

At every projected state y_j>=q_j, by(10), or by q_j=0 at a cap.
Also u_j-p_j>=-N_sp: a positive horizontal normal gives a
nonnegative displacement; with a negative normal, theta>arctan(1/kappa)
and p_j<=kappa qmax, whereas u_j>=epsilon. At a cap u_j>=C.

The new lower bound, in the direction needed for an upper bound on
normalized error, is particularly simple. Since R(theta)>=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)
and cos theta+sin theta>=1 on [0,pi/2],

    p(theta)+q(theta)>=1,
    P+Q>=s,
    (epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2
        >=(1+epsilon+P+Q)^2/2 >=(1+epsilon+s)^2/2.     (15)

The first quadratic inequality is the square of the elementary
Euclidean comparison (u+y)^2<=2(u^2+y^2). It uses actual P,Q,
not the upper bound P<=Cs. It imposes no sum-only dependence on them.

## 3. Local planes, their exact zero wedge, and smooth envelopes

Take a local selected step b>=1+epsilon^(-2), and any sigma>0 with

    sigma(P+epsilon b)<=1,
    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.                 (16)

Set

    z_circ=(1-sigma(epsilon+P), sigma(1+Q))=(c_1,c_2),
    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b), Y=sigma Q,
    V={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_r},
    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V)<v,z-z_circ>.             (17)

The finite set retains the endpoint and cap slopes and every actual
backward projected slope. Each (p,-q) in it equals
[(p-epsilon q)/C](C,0)+q(epsilon,-1), with nonnegative coefficients.
Therefore its exact zero set is

    Z_phi={z: z_1<=c_1,
                 epsilon(z_1-c_1)-(z_2-c_2)<=0}.      (18)

Both endpoint constraints are necessary and the displayed conic
representation proves sufficiency for every other plane.

The function phi is nonnegative. From(16) and p>=epsilon q one has
<v,-z_circ><=0 for every v. It follows that phi=0 on z_1<=0,z_2>=0.
Also <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0, so
phi(U,y)=0 for y>=Y. These statements keep ALL local planes.

For any nonnegative finite maximum J of affine functions having a zero,
define env_1 J(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2]. The proximal minimizer is
unique by coercivity and strict convexity. If z is this point and
g=x-z, then g belongs to the convex subdifferential of J at z.
Conversely this subgradient condition proves proximal optimality by
expanding the squared norm. For another input and its pair z',g',
subgradient monotonicity gives ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>. A trial point
z gives the upper increment bound <g,w>+||w||^2/2; using z' and the
subgradient inequality at z gives the lower bound
<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2. These prove differentiability, convexity,
gradient g, and 1-Lipschitz continuity. Nonnegativity and attainment
show EXACTLY

    argmin(env_1 J)={J=0}, min(env_1 J)=0.              (19)

At x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j, let z_j=x_j-sigma v_j. The projection
inequalities proved in Section2 hold against every vector of V, hence
sigma v_j is an active subgradient of phi at z_j. Thus these are the
actual gradients of env_1 phi. The path starts at (1,0), follows
each actual ordinary alpha_j, then reaches (U+sigma epsilon b,Y)
with gradient sigma(epsilon,-1). Its selected step b reaches
(U,Y+sigma b). The first physical coordinate decreases and the second
increases, with x_(j),1>=U, 0<=x_(j),2<=Y during the ordinary block.

At the local proximal points, the more precise bounds are

    0<=z_(j),2=sigma(1+Q-y_j+q_j)<=sigma(1+Q),
    z_(j),1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]>=0
                                               if b>=B_sp. (20)

The second bound pays N_sp through(14); the first uses y_j>=q_j,
including the overshooting arc. No error is dropped at a checkpoint.

## 4. First realization: full minimum set, rows, and exact radius

For any k>=1 selected checkpoints, write the actual word as
(block_0,b_0,block_1,...,b_(k-1),block_k), with block sums s_i.
Run Section2 separately on each ACTUAL full-step block_i, i<k.
Require b_i>=B_sp, and use

    M_i=P_i+max(epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon),
    sigma_i=1/M_i,
    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),
    ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).                      (21)

Use (17) to define phi_i, c_(i,1),c_(i,2),U_i,Y_i and put

    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,
    delta=D_k/(1+2s_k), Phi_k(z)=delta max(z_k-ell_k,0),
    J(z)=max_(0<=i<=k)Phi_i(z), f=env_1 J, x_0=e_0.    (22)

All D_i are positive. This is one function fixed before its run;
all old and future pieces remain in the maximum. Conditions(16) hold.
J is nonnegative, and J(0)=0 by its pre-activation zero wedges.
Equation(19) makes f convex and 1-smooth with minimum0.

At the start of block i the frozen coordinates are ell_j+D_j U_j
for j<i, the current coordinate is ell_i+D_i, and all later ones
are0. At each candidate local proximal point the current piece has
the local subgradient. The preceding piece is zero: its first input
is U_(i-1), and its second is at least ell_i/D_(i-1)>=Y_(i-1),
by(20). Earlier pieces stay in their zero wedges. The next piece
has first input at most0 because z_(i+1)<=ell_(i+1), by(20),
and second input0. Every further piece and the hinge are also zero.
Thus the current subgradient is a subgradient of J, even at a tie.
The local proximal point is the actual global proximal point.

After b_i the new coordinate equals D_i sigma_i(Q_i+b_i)
=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1), proving this state induction. In the tail, if u
is its already executed mass, the moving coordinate equals
ell_k+D_k-u delta. Its candidate proximal coordinate is at least
ell_k because D_k-(u+1)delta>=0 for 0<=u<=s_k. All old pieces are
zero there. Therefore the actual gradient is delta e_k throughout
the tail, including at the terminal point, and

    2f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k).                            (23)

For ALL directed pairs of samples a,b, including the initial point,
all queried points, terminal point, and ANY minimizer, put
g_a=grad f(x_a), z_a=x_a-g_a. Then f_a=J(z_a)+||g_a||^2/2 and

    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2
        =J(z_a)-J(z_b)+<g_a,z_b-z_a><=0.               (24)

This proves every original smooth-convex interpolation row in its
specified orientation, without deleting optimum or terminal rows.
The actual Gram matrix is that of vectors in R^(k+1), hence is
positive semidefinite. Zero steps preserve identical actual jets.

The full minimizer set, directly from (18),(19),(22), is

    C_env={x: x_i<=zeta_i,
                 epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta_i-D_i c_(i,2), i<k;
                 x_k<=ell_k},
    zeta_i=ell_i+D_i c_(i,1).                          (25)

Define x_*=(c_(0,1),c_(0,2),0,...,0). The first component is at
its center. Its second coordinate equals ell_1, so the next component
has local input(0,0), and all remaining components are in their
pre-activation zero wedges; the hinge is zero. Thus x_* is feasible.
The vector e_0-x_* has the active-normal representation

    e_0-x_*=sigma_0[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/C](C,0,0,...)
                  +sigma_0(1+Q_0)(epsilon,-1,0,...).

Both coefficients are nonnegative. For every x in C_env this implies
<e_0-x_*,x-x_*><=0. Expanding ||e_0-x||^2 proves that x_* is the
unique projection and gives

    rho_env^2=dist(e_0,C_env)^2
      =sigma_0^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2],           (26)
    E_env=(b_0-1)^2/([(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k))
                     *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i]^2. (27)

In particular at k=1,

    E_env<=[2(b-1)^2]/[(1+s)^2(1+2t)],                (28)

where s,t are its actual prefix and suffix sums. This uses (15) as
a LOWER radius bound; it never uses P<=Cs to upper-bound payoff.

## 5. Second realization: full minimum set and nearest-distance structure

Use the same selection, but now require b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)).
Apply Section2 to the actual ordinary blocks with EVERY physical step
divided by2; P_i,Q_i in this section mean those HALF-step outputs.
In (17) use the local selected step beta_i=b_i/2 and scale

    sigma'_i=1/[Cs_i/2+epsilon b_i/2+A_sp].             (29)

Both conditions(16) follow from(11),(13). Define primed local objects
phi'_i,c'_(i,1),c'_(i,2),U'_i,Y'_i and f_i=env_1 phi'_i. Put

    G_i=sigma'_i b_i/2,
    D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,
    D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i, ell'_(i+1)=D'_i sigma'_i Q_i,
    delta'=D'_k/(1+s_k).

Let Hub_d(z) be0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for 0<=z<=d, and
dz-d^2/2 for z>=d. The one static source-format function is

    F(x)=(1/2)sum_(i<k)(D'_i)^2
           f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)
               +(1/2)Hub_(delta')(x_k-ell'_k), x_0=e_0. (30)

It is nonnegative with F(0)=0. Every local term is convex and
1-smooth on its two coordinates. Their quadratic upper bounds,
after multiplication by1/2, count each coordinate at most twice,
including the hinge. Hence F(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2.
For a differentiable convex F this implies

    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>
                     >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.    (31)

Indeed apply that upper bound to V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x and
w=-grad V(x); y minimizes V. Adding(31) and its exchanged version
proves 1-Lipschitz continuity of grad F. Taking x=x_b,y=x_a in(31)
is exactly Q_ab<=0 in(24)'s orientation for every original pair.
The optimum and terminal rows are included.

At the start of stage i, the frozen coordinates are
ell'_j+D'_j U'_j for j<i, current coordinate ell'_i+D'_i, and later
coordinates0. During its local PHYSICAL path, x_i>=ell'_i and
0<=x_(i+1)<=ell'_(i+1). Thus all old terms are in post-checkpoint
zero wedges and all future terms are in pre-activation zero wedges.
They have gradient0, since they are differentiable and nonnegative.
Only the current term acts; its coefficient1/2 turns each physical
step into the stipulated local half step. The selected step gives
next coordinate ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1). This proves the actual trajectory,
without using a horizontal proximal-coordinate bound. In the tail
the physical gradient is delta'/2; the final coordinate above ell'_k
is D'_k-delta' s_k/2>=delta'. Substitution yields

    2F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)].                       (32)

All original pieces, translations, scales, and the 1/2 coefficient
are retained. The specified origin is a minimizer at radius1, but
we now divide(32) by the ACTUAL closest distance.

Because each term is nonnegative and each envelope has zero set(18),
the complete minimum set is the nonempty closed polyhedron

    C_source={x: x_i<=zeta'_i,
                 epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta'_i-D'_i c'_(i,2), i<k;
                 x_k<=ell'_k},
    zeta'_i=ell'_i+D'_i c'_(i,1).                      (33)

It contains0. For k>=1, e_0 violates its first horizontal constraint,
so its squared distance rho_source^2 is positive and at most1.
Since C_source is a subset of the first local zero wedge, projection
onto that wedge by the normal argument of Section4 gives the genuine
lower bound

    rho_source^2 >= (sigma'_0)^2
                   [(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2].       (34)

In general this is a lower bound, NOT the exact distance. Unlike the
first realization, c'_(0,2)=sigma'_0(1+Q_0) is larger than
ell'_1=sigma'_0 Q_0. Later constraints must be checked.

For completeness an exact finite projection formula, with all those
constraints retained, is as follows. Let A_rad consist of rows
e_i^T and (epsilon e_i-e_(i+1))^T for i<k, followed by e_k^T,
and let t_rad consist of the right sides in(33). For each independent
row subset S, put A_S=(A_rad)_S, r_S=A_S e_0-(t_rad)_S,
K_S=A_S A_S^T and lambda_S=K_S^(-1)r_S. Include the empty subset
with value0. Then

    rho_source^2=max_(S independent,lambda_S>=0)
                          r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S.          (35)

This is an expression, not an executed solver. For any feasible x and
lambda>=0 on S, completing a square gives
||e_0-x||^2>=2lambda^T r_S-||A_S^T lambda||^2. At lambda_S this is
the displayed entry, proving every such lower bound. At the actual
projection x_*, the normal e_0-x_* lies in the nonnegative cone of
its active constraint rows. To justify this without importing a
duality theorem, any direction having nonpositive inner products
with all active rows is feasible for a sufficiently small move;
projection optimality forces its inner product with e_0-x_* to be
nonpositive. A finitely generated cone is closed: remove linear
dependences from any nonnegative representation until independent
generators remain; it is a finite union of closed injective images
of orthants. If the normal were outside that cone, subtracting its
projection on the cone would contradict the preceding feasible-
direction inequality. Choose independent active rows representing
the normal. Activity gives r_S=K_S lambda, and their value in(35)
equals the squared distance. This proves equality.

## 6. Exact simplification with one source-format checkpoint

Suppose k=1, write s,t for the prefix and suffix sums, and omit
primes on the local sigma for this paragraph. P,Q are still the
HALF-step outputs of the whole ordered prefix. Formula(33) becomes

    x_0<=1-sigma(epsilon+P),
    epsilon x_0-x_1<=epsilon-sigma(epsilon^2+epsilon P+1+Q),
    x_1<=sigma Q.                                    (36)

Define E_0=P+epsilon+1/epsilon, a local abbreviation distinct from
the payoff E(f,h). The point

    x_*=(1-sigma E_0,sigma Q)                         (37)

satisfies all three constraints: the second and third are equalities,
and the first has slack sigma/epsilon. Its normal from e_0 is

    e_0-x_*=sigma(E_0,-Q)
       =sigma(E_0/epsilon)(epsilon,-1)
          +sigma(E_0/epsilon-Q)(0,1).                 (38)

The coefficients are positive because
E_0/epsilon-Q=(P/epsilon-Q)+1+epsilon^(-2)>0 by(11).
Thus(38), using the active second and third constraints, proves
projection by squared-distance expansion. We obtain the closed formula

    rho_source^2=sigma^2[(P+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2+Q^2],  (39)
    E_source=b^2/[8(1+t)((P+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2+Q^2)]. (40)

Both the first scale sigma and its activation allowance cancel
correctly, but the actual P,Q remain. This is an exact payoff of the
original function(30), not a new scale choice or a reset minimum.

By(15) for half steps, P+Q>=s/2. Since epsilon+1/epsilon>=5/2
on 0<epsilon<=1/2, the denominator in(39) obeys

    (P+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2+Q^2
       >=(P+Q+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2/2 >=(s+5)^2/8.

Consequently the actual one-checkpoint source payoff satisfies

    E_source<=b^2/[(1+t)(s+5)^2].                     (41)

This is a LOWER bound on its actual squared radius and an UPPER
bound on its actual payoff, with both directions justified.

## 7. All one-checkpoint selections on the small-tau hierarchy

For(2), put

    r_h=c(c-2), v_h=a/(c-2)=beta/r_h,
    M_j=1+S_j, d_j=a c^j, u_j=d_j/M_j.

The symbol r_h is a hierarchy constant, not a backward vector r_j.
With x=tau/m and gamma=4+2sqrt(2), expansion gives
r_h=1+gamma x+lambda^2 x^2>1>beta>0. Hence0<v_h<1. From the word
recurrence M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j and M_0=1, induction gives

    M_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,
    v_h c^j<=M_j<=c^j,
    a<=u_j<=c-2.                                     (42)

The simple constants needed uniformly on the assigned domain are

    c<121/50, 1/4<a<=u_j<21/50.                      (43)

Indeed sqrt(2)<283/200 by squaring, so lambda<483/200. Also
c<=lambda(1+1/6144)<484/200=121/50 because483<6144.
Moreover beta>=1-1/6144>3/4 and c<3, proving a>1/4.

Choose ANY occurrence of the sole checkpoint, at level j. Its value
is b=1+d_j. Its two immediate complete children are H_j, and neither
contains a selected checkpoint because this is the only selection.
Thus the ACTUAL prefix sum s and suffix sum t satisfy

    X=1+s>=M_j, Y=1+t>=M_j,
    S_m=s+b+t=X+Y+d_j-1.                             (44)

There may be arbitrarily much exterior mass on either side. No
assumption of root, first-occurrence, or record selection is used.

For the main realization, (28) and(44) give

    E_env/H_base(H_m)
      <=2d_j^2(2X+2Y+2d_j-1)/[X^2(2Y-1)].            (45)

For fixed Y,d_j this decreases with X>0: the derivative of
(2X+K)/X^2, where K=2Y+2d_j-1>0, is -2/X^2-2K/X^3.
For fixed X,d_j it decreases with Y>1/2 because the numerator
equals (2Y-1)+2X+2d_j. Hence(45) is at most

    2u_j^2(4M_j+2d_j-1)/(2M_j-1)
      =2u_j^2[2+u_j+(1+u_j)/(2M_j-1)].               (46)

Main eligibility implies b>=1+epsilon^(-2)>=5, so d_j>=4.
By(43), M_j=d_j/u_j>200/21>19/2, hence2M_j-1>18.
Using(43), the bracket in(46) is strictly less than

    121/50+(71/50)/18=2249/900<5/2.

Thus

    E_env/H_base(H_m)<5(21/50)^2=441/500.             (47)

All independent kappa,epsilon values, including kappa>1 with the
full N_sp threshold, have been included: their only use here was
actual eligibility and the universally valid displacement bound.

For the source realization, (41) gives

    E_source/H_base(H_m)
       <=(1+d_j)^2(2X+2Y+2d_j-1)/[Y(X+4)^2].         (48)

Source eligibility gives b>=2(1+epsilon^(-2))>=10, so d_j>=9.
For fixed X,d_j the ratio decreases with Y, since its varying part
is 2+(2X+2d_j-1)/Y. For fixed Y,d_j, differentiation of
(2X+K)/(X+4)^2 gives (8-2X-2K)/(X+4)^3<0, where
K=2Y+2d_j-1>=19. Thus(48) is at most

    [(1+d_j)/(M_j+4)]^2[4+2u_j-1/M_j].              (49)

Since u_j>1/4, we have (1+d_j)/(M_j+4)<=d_j/M_j=u_j.
Equations(43),(49) now imply

    E_source/H_base(H_m)<2u_j^2(2+u_j)
                 <5(21/50)^2=441/500.               (50)

Equations(47),(50) prove(3) for EVERY eligible checkpoint position
and EVERY parameter pair. No trajectory was changed during these
comparisons; no minimum-level deletion was applied to block outputs.

At k=0 the first function is the hinge envelope
Hub_(1/(1+2S_m)) from initial1, with zero set(-infinity,0]. Its
terminal payoff is 1/(1+2S_m). The second is
Hub_(1/(1+S_m))/2, with the same closest distance1 and payoff
1/[2(1+S_m)]<H_base(H_m). This proves(4).

## 8. Exact boundary tests and the failure of the multi-stage shortcut

First, an all-zero ordinary block has every r_j=(epsilon,-1),
every v_j=(epsilon,-1), and P=Q=0. The two one-checkpoint formulas
give respectively (b-1)^2/[(1+epsilon^2)(1+2t)] and
b^2/[8(1+t)(epsilon+1/epsilon)^2]. The radial bounds(15),(41)
remain valid. Such a prefix is not an eligible one-checkpoint
selection in H_(m,tau), whose complete child has positive mass at
every eligible level; the hierarchy proof does not assert otherwise.

For a single ordinary step alpha, whenever
alpha>=1+(1/kappa-epsilon)/C, condition(7) selects the cap, so

    P=C alpha, Q=0,
    ||r_0||^2=(epsilon+C alpha)^2+1.                  (51)

This is an actual arbitrarily huge-step test of(15), not a limiting
gradient-flow substitution. An equality threshold gives the same
projected point; zeros before a cap leave it capped.

Here is exact order dependence at a fixed total mass. Set
epsilon=kappa=1/2, theta_*=arctan(3/4), and

    R_*=C exp(-theta_*/2), p_*=(4/5)R_*, q_*=(3/5)R_*,
    A_*=1+2(1+q_*)/C.                                (52)

For a single last ordinary step1, the cap test fails, since1>1/4.
Equation(8) reduces to 2sin theta-(3/2)cos theta=0, whose root
is theta_*. Equivalently n(theta_*)=(1/2,-1), verifying the
support relation exactly. For chronological block(A_*,1), the
last step first gives r=(1/2+p_*,-1-q_*). The preceding A_* caps
because its right side in(7) is
1+q_*+1/4+p_*/2>1+q_*. Its ACTUAL outputs are therefore

    P=A_* C+p_*, Q=q_*>0.                            (53)

For chronological block(1,A_*), the last A_* already caps, since
A_*>1+2/C>1+3/(2C), and the preceding unit step stays capped.
Its outputs are instead

    P=(A_*+1)C, Q=0.                                 (54)

Both blocks have sum A_*+1, while p_*<C and q_*>0. Thus actual
P,Q are not functions of their sums. This test invalidates that
shortcut, not any formula for the unchanged ordered construction.

Finally test extension of the exact one-source-checkpoint radius(39).
Use TWO source checkpoints b_0=b_1=10, all three ordinary blocks
empty (or made of zeros), epsilon=kappa=1/2. Both checkpoints are
eligible exactly at the threshold. Then P_i=Q_i=0,

    sigma=1/(A_sp+5/2), 0<sigma<1/5,
    D'_1=5sigma, D'_2=25sigma^2, ell'_1=ell'_2=0.      (55)

The strict sigma bound follows from H_sp>0 and A_sp>5/2. The local
finite planes are exactly 0, (C,0), and (1/2,-1) about center
(1-sigma/2,sigma). The point

    x_*=(1-sigma/2,sigma,0)                           (56)

is at the first center. The second local input is(1/5,0). It is
inside its zero wedge: its horizontal constraint has positive slack,
and its endpoint plane evaluates, before the positive scale,

    (1/2)[1/5-(1-sigma/2)]+sigma
                    =-2/5+(5/4)sigma<-3/20<0.

The terminal hinge is zero. Thus(56) belongs to the full(33), with
every old/future plane retained. Its normal from e_0 is
sigma(1/2,-1,0), an active first endpoint normal. Projection follows,
and hence

    rho_source^2=(5/4)sigma^2.                        (57)

By contrast the proposed extrapolation of(39) would give
(25/4)sigma^2, five times the actual squared distance. The tail cap
x_1<=sigma Q_0 used in(36) is no longer a constraint; the genuine
tail cap is x_2<=0. This is the exact failed interface.

For additional trajectory evidence, the actual gradients along the
two selected steps of(30) are

    g_0=(sigma/4,-sigma/2,0),
    x_1=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma,0),
    g_1=(0,5sigma^2/4,-5sigma^2/2),
    x_2=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma-25sigma^2/2,25sigma^2).

At x_2 the local terms are zero and the tail value is
(D'_2)^2/4, so the actual closest payoff is250sigma^2, while
using the incorrect squared radius would give50sigma^2. Smoothness
and all interpolation rows follow directly from the static function
and(31). This is a boundary test outside H_(m,tau); no small-tau
escape or benchmark comparison is inferred from it.

## 9. Original benchmark and the finite small-tau deficit

Use ONLY accepted fact_id04219554e41d9750 for the following inputs.
For nonnegative u,v define

    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=i<n)Omega(C_i,C_(n-i-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=i<n)G(C_i,B_(n-i-1)).                  (58)

These are the original ALL-split denominators and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.
The accepted ray enclosure is

    lambda^(-j)B_(2^j-1)<=Z_1
           <=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(2^j))+3/100)^2,       (59)

where Z_1 is their positive finite limit. The same accepted fact's
Section8 certifies every entry of its fixed public interval table
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA2562a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
Only its header, row1024, and hash were read in this round; no
generator, recurrence, checker, or mathematical evaluation was run.
With D80=1208925819614629174706176, that row gives

    D80 B_1024<=19229705732238461978760749948
                  <15910 D80
                   =19234009790068750169575260160.    (60)

Since19600<19602, sqrt(2)>140/99. The binomial identity
lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2) implies lambda^10>6725, because
(3363*99+2378*140)-6725*99=82>0. Thus(59) at j=10, (60), and
sqrt(15910)<127 give

    Z_1<(15910+381/50+9/10000)/6725
         <15918/6725<237/100.                        (61)

The last integer comparison is1591800<1593825. All these are written
scalar deductions from the existing accepted endpoint, not a new
benchmark evaluation and not a claim that the old draft is a fact.

For the hierarchy, (42) gives

    (1+2S_m)/lambda^m
      =2v_h(1+tau/m)^m+2(1-v_h)(2/lambda)^m-lambda^(-m). (62)

Also r_h-beta=(5+2sqrt(2))tau/m+lambda^2 tau^2/m^2
<8tau/m+6tau^2/m^2<9/(4m) for the stated m,tau. Since r_h>1,

    v_h=beta/r_h>1-9/(4m)>255/256,
    lambda^(-m)<1/256.                               (63)

For m>=2, binomial truncation gives
(1+tau/m)^m>=1+tau+tau^2/4. At tau=1/4 this is81/64, so(62),(63)
imply (1+2S_m)/lambda^m>20623/8192>5/2. Uniformly for
1/5<=tau<=1/4 the lower bound121/100 instead gives
(1+2S_m)/lambda^m>6161/2560>12/5. Combining(59),(61) with(4)
proves precisely(5), for EVERY m>=1536. No choice of benchmark
optimizer or alternate recurrence has been substituted.

For fixed tau>0, v_h tends1, (1+tau/m)^m tends exp(tau), and
(2/lambda)^m tends0. Hence(62) tends2exp(tau). The accepted ray
limit(59) proves the stated limit for L_actual,<=1. It is a limit
for this delimited actual family, never an upper bound on W_n.

## 10. Remaining interface, scope, and record

The endpoint projection mechanism succeeds for all actual one-checkpoint
realizations, with a uniform strict factor(3). It fully pays the true
initial distance, both half-step and full-step trajectories, every
affine plane, the terminal value, and every directed interpolation row.
It uses no backward-state replacement and does not collapse ordered
blocks to sums; sums enter only through inequalities proved from each
actual projected slope.

The same mechanism has NOT proved an all-count family obstruction.
For the main realization the exact remaining expression is(27), with
each M_i computed from its actual ordered block. For the source
realization it is (D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)rho_source^2] with(33)--(35).
The first-wedge lower bound(34) is valid but weaker than the special
one-stage formula(39); the explicit test(55)--(57) refutes copying
that stronger formula when a further stage replaces the first tail.
The exact equal-sum test(52)--(54) also bars replacing actual P_i,Q_i
after deleting a checkpoint without a new comparison proof.

Thus the unresolved task is to control the product of ACTUAL transfer
denominators together with the first-block or chained source distance
when k>=2 on the same H_(m,tau) family. There is no claimed counterexample
to its all-count comparison, no normalized escape, no conclusion about
the unrestricted W_n, and no original-target solution.

Matlas was queried for the minimizer/projection interface and the
endpoint-displacement bound; its results were inapplicable. Web search
provided generic Moreau-envelope orientation. No external theorem is
imported, and no new technical literature branch is claimed: the
explicit finite-plane and Euclidean arguments above are self-contained.
The skills used were query-memory, direct-proving, search-math-results,
and construct-counterexamples. The assignment closes the formal gate,
so this complete joint scoped proof remains UNVERIFIED. This is the
end of the ONE allocated analytical mechanism/round, without renewal.

Glossary: h,n,x_t,g_t,f,F,W_n,E(f,h),Q_ab are defined in Sections1,4,5;
lambda,tau,m,beta,c,a,H_j,H_(m,tau),n_m,S_j,H_base,L_actual,<=1 in1;
epsilon,kappa,theta_sp,C,c_sp,R,e,t,v,n,K,r_j,theta_j,t_j,P,Q,s in2;
T_sp,H_sp,A_sp,N_sp,B_sp,qmax in2; sigma,z_circ,c_1,c_2,U,Y,V,phi,
Z_phi,env_1 and the proximal points in3; b_i,s_i,M_i,sigma_i,D_i,
ell_i,Phi_i,delta,J,C_env,zeta_i,rho_env,E_env in4; all primed
objects,beta_i,G_i,Hub_d,F,C_source,zeta'_i,rho_source,A_rad,t_rad,
A_S,r_S,K_S,lambda_S in5; E_0,E_source in6; r_h,v_h,M_j,d_j,u_j,X,Y
in7; theta_*,R_*,p_*,q_*,A_* in8; Omega,G,C_n,B_n,U_n^ZJ,Z_1,D80
in9. Section-local symbols are explicitly scoped; universal notation
is unchanged. In particular capital M_i for actual transfer is distinct
from hierarchy M_j=1+S_j, and lowercase r_j for a backward vector is
distinct from hierarchy r_h. Subscripts and section context distinguish
the temporary ordinary index from the hierarchy level.

# Full-domain two-channel spiral guarantees collapse to Huber

This document defines a NEW scalar functional L_wide. It preserves the
old name L_sp and its restricted domain unchanged. All new algebra needed
below, including the reused hierarchy and mass-deletion ingredients, is
proved jointly here. The sole accepted mathematical input is
fact04219554e41d9750, for the original benchmark ray and saved enclosure.
The unverified geometric construction and independent analytical audit
are provenance, never correctness premises in this scalar proof.

The conclusion is an upper bound for two explicit lower-guarantee
formulas, not for the actual worst-case error W. It does not bound the
stronger exact closest-radius formula(6) of the geometric source, nor
the source-format realization after a closest-minimizer improvement.

## 1. Exact enlarged scalar definition

The immutable geometric source is finding c8fcf4ced5a34046 at
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/c8fcf4ced5a34046.md,
SHA2564d1e99db853eba3751a3f4761fc2c400c68eacdc9c7912b17c0b0c82d6975d2e.
Its formulas(1),(2),(7),(8) define the following numbers, without needing
any assertion about a function realizing them.

Take independently

    0<epsilon<=1/2, kappa>0.

For every such pair set

    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    C=c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp),
    d_sp=1-kappa^2,
    T_sp=(exp(d_sp*theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d_sp, 0<kappa<1,
    T_sp=theta_sp/kappa,                   kappa>=1,
    H_sp=C*[1+(1+kappa)*T_sp]-1,
    A=A_sp=epsilon+(H_sp+1)/epsilon,
    a_sp=2*A/C.                                           (1)

C and A are scoped abbreviations, not the accepted benchmark C_n or a
primitive-certificate capacity. The definition includes kappa=1 using
the second branch, with no division by d_sp there. Define exactly

    qmax_sp=1,                                       kappa<=epsilon,
    qmax_sp=sqrt((1+epsilon^2)/(1+kappa^2))
              *exp(kappa*[theta_sp-arctan(1/kappa)]), kappa>epsilon,
    N_sp=max{0,kappa*qmax_sp-epsilon},
    B_sp=1+max{epsilon^(-2),N_sp/epsilon}.                  (2)

No simplification of the general-kappa horizontal-normal price N_sp is
assumed. In particular, this definition does not silently substitute the
smaller old threshold when kappa>1.

Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be any finite nonnegative word at its original
physical length n. A nonempty subset of its original physical indices,
listed in increasing order, induces the actual decomposition

    h=(block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),

where k>=1 and s_i is the ordinary sum of block_i. Empty blocks and
zero physical entries are permitted. One parameter pair is fixed for
the entire selection. Define the two distinct scalar channels

    Phi_M=1/(1+2*s_k)*product_(i=0)^(k-1)
             [(b_i-1)/(C*s_i+max{epsilon*b_i,A})]^2,
             eligible when every b_i>=B_sp;                (3)

    Phi_S=1/[2*(1+s_k)]*product_(i=0)^(k-1)
             [b_i/(C*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b_i)]^2,
             eligible when every b_i>=2*(1+epsilon^(-2)).  (4)

Equation(4) uses C*(a_sp+s_i)=2*A+C*s_i exactly. Its numerator is b_i,
not b_i-1. Its terminal denominator is 2*(1+s_k), not 1+2*s_k.

Write H_base(h)=1/(1+2*sum h). Define

    L_wide(h)=max{H_base(h),
                 sup Phi_M over all pairs and eligible nonempty subsets,
                 sup Phi_S over all pairs and eligible nonempty subsets}. (5)

The supremum of an empty family in(5) means0. The supremum notation does
not presume parameter attainment. No product mixes factors from different
channels. The Huber term is included explicitly; hence the equality
proved below is attained by that term. All constants and denominators in
(1)--(5) are positive on their stated domains.

The source describes(4) as an exact payoff normalized by its specified
minimizer0 and initial distance1. We retain that convention. Interpreting
(3) or(4) as lower bounds for actual W remains conditional on establishing
the full geometry, which is not part of this scalar candidate.

## 2. Word family and the quantitative claims

Put lambda=1+sqrt(2). For a fixed real tau>0 and integer m>tau define

    beta=1-tau/m, c=lambda*(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    H_(m,tau)=H_m, n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j.                (6)

Every child keeps the SAME enclosing parameters(m,tau). A level is the
index j of a bridge 1+a*c^j, not the physical index of one occurrence.
Only tau=1 is the original diagnostic family; the theorem uses the
different words specified by small tau in(6).

The joint claims to be proved are as follows. For EVERY real
0<tau<=1/4, EVERY integer m>=1536, EVERY parameter pair in(1), and EVERY
nonempty subset of the physical word H_(m,tau), one has

    Phi_M<=1/(1+2*S_m),
    Phi_S<=5/[16*(1+S_m)].                               (7)

In(7) the formulas can even be evaluated on ineligible subsets; the
proof bounds that larger comparison class. Therefore(7) applies, in
particular, to each channel's own actual threshold, including equality.
Consequently

    L_wide(H_(m,tau))=1/(1+2*S_m).                       (8)

Retain the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark, defined for integers n>=0 by

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4*x*y))/2,
    G(x,y)=2*x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8*x*y)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=i<n) Omega(C_i,C_(n-i-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=i<n) G(C_i,B_(n-i-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                      (9)

Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 gives the positive finite limit

    B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1.                         (10)

For every fixed0<tau<=1/4 the further claim is

    lim_(m->infinity) B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))
                         =Z_1/(2*exp(tau)).            (11)

In fact, the strict benchmark deficits already hold at EVERY m>=1536:

    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,1/4))<237/250<1,
    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))<79/80 for1/5<=tau<=1/4.  (11a)

The corresponding limits are strictly below the same constants. These
claims use only the accepted benchmark enclosure, rederived from its
saved endpoint in Section8. No new benchmark calculation is performed.

## 3. Joint hierarchy identities and the selection mechanism

For fixed(m,tau), put

    r=c*(c-2), v=a/(c-2)=beta/r,
    M_j=1+S_j, d_j=a*c^j, b_j=1+d_j,
    u_j=d_j/M_j, M_max=M_(m-1).                         (12)

Writing x=tau/m and gamma=4+2*sqrt(2), direct expansion gives

    r=1+gamma*x+lambda^2*x^2>1>beta>0.                 (13)

Thus0<v<1 and c>lambda>2. The sum recurrence is
M_(j+1)=2*M_j+a*c^j with M_0=1. Substitution, using a=v*(c-2), proves
by induction that

    M_j=v*c^j+(1-v)*2^j, 0<=j<=m.                     (14)

It follows that

    1<=M_j<=c^j,
    0<u_j<=c-2,
    M_j<=M_max for0<=j<m.                              (15)

For the first two inequalities use c>2 and M_j>=v*c^j. For the last,
the sum recurrence has positive added terms and M_j>0.

Choose ANY currently selected checkpoint having minimum selected level
j. Its immediate complete H_j children contain only levels<j, so neither
child contains a selected checkpoint. If s and t are the actual ordinary
sums immediately before and after this checkpoint, then

    s>=S_j, t>=S_j.                                   (16)

This includes a first or last selected checkpoint: its adjacent ordinary
block is then the true initial or terminal block. Tied minimum levels
cause no problem because each child has strictly smaller levels.

Deleting a checkpoint below means deleting it from the selected subset,
not from the physical word. Its full physical step b is reclassified as
ordinary, so the two ordinary blocks merge to the sum s+b+t. All other
physical indices, step values, and the one parameter pair are retained.
Eligibility of any remaining checkpoint is unchanged.

We prove one minimum-level deletion scheme for both channels under the
same whole-word alternatives

    (I)  C>=r=c*(c-2),
    (II) A>=2*C*M_max,                                (17)

assuming C>=1, A>=2*C and2<c<=5/2. Section7 proves these assumptions
and the dichotomy for the ENTIRE enlarged parameter domain. The two
channel comparisons below are separate; neither is inferred from a
pointwise ordering of(3) and(4).

## 4. Mass channel: exact interior and terminal comparisons

Let b=b_j=1+d be a selected minimum-level checkpoint, with
M=M_j, S=M-1, u=d/M. Then0<u<=1/2 and s,t>=S.
For this channel write Q_M(b)=max{epsilon*b,A}.

If a next selected checkpoint b' exists, put R=Q_M(b'). Cancelling its
positive numerator b'-1 and all unchanged factors shows that deletion
does not lower Phi_M exactly when

    d*[C*(s+b+t)+R] <= (C*s+Q_M(b))*(C*t+R).           (18)

The right side minus the left is increasing in Q_M(b). Its derivatives
with respect to R,s,t are, respectively,

    C*s+Q_M(b)-d,
    C*(C*t+R-d),
    C*(C*s+Q_M(b)-d).

They are positive throughout s,t>=M-1 and Q_M(b),R>=A: indeed
C*(M-1)+A>=C*M>=M>=2*d. Thus its minimum is at
s=t=M-1 and Q_M(b)=R=A. Put z_M=(A-C)/M>=0. Dividing that minimum by
M^2 gives the exact expression

    E_M=C^2-C*u*(2+u)+z_M*(2*C-u)+z_M^2.              (19)

Under(I), u*(2+u)<=c*(c-2)<=C, so every grouped term in(19) is
nonnegative. Under(II),

    z_M=(A-C)/M>=C*(2*M_max-1)/M>=C,

because M<=M_max and M_max>=1. Therefore

    E_M>=C^2-(5/4)*C+C*(2*C-1/2)+C^2
        =4*C^2-(7/4)*C>0.                            (20)

Here u*(2+u)<=5/4 and2*C-u>0 justify each replacement. This proves
the interior deletion comparison in either branch of(17).

If b is the LAST selected checkpoint, the exact comparison is instead

    d^2*[1+2*(s+b+t)]
      <=(C*s+Q_M(b))^2*(1+2*t).                      (21)

Since C*s+Q_M(b)>=s+1, it suffices to bound

    d^2*[1+2*(s+b+t)]/[(s+1)^2*(1+2*t)].              (22)

For fixed s this decreases in t, because the fraction without d^2 is
[1+2*(s+b)/(1+2*t)]/(s+1)^2. Its s derivative after suppressing the
positive constant1+2*t is -2*(s+2*b+2*t)/(s+1)^3<0. Hence(22) is
largest at s=t=M-1. There it equals

    u^2*[(4+2*u)*M-1]/(2*M-1)
      <=u^2*(3+2*u)<=1.                              (23)

For the first inequality, the difference of the cross-multiplied
numerators is2*(1+u)*(M-1)>=0. For the second, u^2*(3+2*u) increases
on[0,1/2] and equals1 at1/2. This terminal comparison needs no
additional choice between(I) and(II).

Repeatedly deleting a currently selected minimum level strictly reduces
the number of selections. The comparisons keep Phi_M nondecreasing
until the empty-selection value1/(1+2*S_m) is reached. This proves
the first inequality in(7) under(17). It is a proof for an arbitrary
finite subset, not an executed recurrence or enumeration.

## 5. Source-format channel: the numerator shift is paid explicitly

Keep b=1+d, M=M_j, u=d/M, and s,t>=M-1 from Section4. Now define

    Q_S(b)=2*A+epsilon*b.

Its numerator is b itself. If a next selected checkpoint b' exists,
write R=Q_S(b'). The exact interior deletion comparison, after cancelling
the unchanged positive b', is

    b*[C*(s+b+t)+R] <= (C*s+Q_S(b))*(C*t+R).           (24)

The difference is increasing in Q_S(b), and its R,s,t derivatives are

    C*s+Q_S(b)-b,
    C*(C*t+R-b),
    C*(C*s+Q_S(b)-b),

respectively. All are positive on the larger comparison region
s,t>=M-1, Q_S(b),R>=2*A. In fact,

    C*(M-1)+2*A-b
      >=C*M+3*C-(M/2+1)>0,                           (25)

using A>=2*C, C>=1, M>=1 and d<=M/2. Therefore the smallest difference
in(24) occurs at s=t=M-1 and Q_S(b)=R=2*A. Define

    q=2*A-C>=3*C.

The minimum is EXACTLY

    F_S=(C*M+q)^2-(d+1)*[C*(2*M+d)+q]
       =M^2*[C^2-C*u*(2+u)]
         +M*[q*(2*C-u)-C*(2+u)]+q^2-q.               (26)

For the first equality, note that the merged physical sum is
2*(M-1)+(d+1)=2*M+d-1, so adding R=2*A yields
C*(2*M+d)+q. The extra1 in b=d+1 is retained in every term of(26).

Under(I), the M^2 coefficient in(26) is nonnegative. Also

    q*(2*C-u)-C*(2+u)
      >=3*C*(2*C-u)-C*(2+u)
       =C*(6*C-4*u-2)>=2*C>0,                       (27)

and q^2-q>=0 because q>=3*C>=3. Thus(24) holds in branch(I).

Under(II), use the first expression in(26). Put z_S=q/M. Since

    q=2*A-C>=C*(4*M_max-1)>=3*C*M,

one has z_S>=3*C. Moreover(d+1)/M<=3/2 and(2*M+d)/M<=5/2. Consequently

    F_S/M^2
      >=(C+z_S)^2-(3/2)*[(5/2)*C+z_S]
       =C^2-(15/4)*C+z_S*(2*C-3/2)+z_S^2
      >=16*C^2-(33/4)*C>0.                          (28)

The last step uses C>=1, so2*C-3/2>0 and substitution z_S>=3*C has
the claimed direction. Its final expression is positive for C>=1.
This proves the interior source-format comparison for both branches.

For a LAST selected checkpoint, deletion replaces the terminal factor
1/[2*(1+t)] by1/[2*(1+s+b+t)]. The exact ratio of the selected
local factor to the deleted local factor is

    R_tail=b^2*(1+s+b+t)/[(C*s+Q_S(b))^2*(1+t)].       (29)

Because Q_S(b)>=2*A>=4*C and C>=1, its denominator satisfies
C*s+Q_S(b)>=s+4. The resulting upper bound for(29) is

    b^2*(1+s+b+t)/[(s+4)^2*(1+t)].                   (30)

For fixed s, the fraction without b^2 equals
[1+(s+b)/(1+t)]/(s+4)^2 and decreases with t. Its derivative in s,
with the positive constant1+t suppressed, is

    (2-s-2*b-2*t)/(s+4)^3<0,

since b=1+d>1. Thus(30) is largest at s=t=M-1, where

    R_tail
      <=(d+1)^2*(2*M+d)/[(M+3)^2*M]
      <=(5/2)*[(M/2+1)/(M+3)]^2
      <=5/8.                                       (31)

For the last two inequalities use d<=M/2 and
M/2+1<=(M+3)/2. This is a uniform quantitative terminal contraction.
It has not been replaced by the mass-channel terminal comparison.

## 6. Finite pruning and the two different empty-selection values

For the source channel only, define the empty-product bookkeeping value
to be1/[2*(1+S_m)]. This is the value of its own displayed formula after
all checkpoints are removed from the subset. It is a comparison sink;
the functional(5) retains its actual Huber baseline1/(1+2*S_m).

Start with any nonempty selected subset, and repeatedly choose a minimum
currently selected level. At an interior deletion use(24); at a terminal
deletion use(31). Every remaining subset still has the complete children
property(16). At least one deletion is terminal, since the last remaining
checkpoint is terminal. All factors outside the local comparison are
unchanged and positive. Multiplying the comparisons therefore gives

    Phi_S<=(5/8)*1/[2*(1+S_m)]
         =5/[16*(1+S_m)].                            (32)

If additional deletions are terminal, each contributes another factor
at most5/8; only one such factor was needed in(32). The mass pruning in
Section4 gives its own bound1/(1+2*S_m). Finally

    5/[16*(1+S_m)]<1/(1+2*S_m),                      (33)

because cross multiplication reduces to5*(1+2*S_m)<16*(1+S_m).
Thus under the hypotheses and dichotomy(17), both channels are bounded
by the Huber baseline, and that baseline attains L_wide.

No pointwise comparison between Phi_S and Phi_M was used. The extra1
in the source numerator is absorbed in the q-dependent terms(26)--(28),
and the terminal factor is handled by(29)--(31). In particular, merely
copying the old b-1 proof would not prove the source-channel assertion.

The proof also covers level0, whose children are empty and M_0=1,
and arbitrary ties between selected levels. The bounds were proved on
the larger class of all nonempty subsets of this positive word, so there
is no lost threshold case, including B_sp at kappa>1 or equality at
either actual eligibility threshold. No ordinary block is replaced by
an envelope, alternative ordering, or child with a new parameter m.

## 7. The deletion dichotomy holds on the entire enlarged domain

Assume0<tau<=1/4 and m>=1536. First2<c<5/2. To give rational bounds,
sqrt(2)<10/7 implies lambda<17/7; for m>=9,

    c<=lambda*(1+1/(4*m))
      <(17/7)*(37/36)=629/252<5/2.                   (34)

Also gamma=4+2*sqrt(2)<7 and lambda^2=3+2*sqrt(2)<6, so m>=2 and(13)
give

    r-1<7/(4*m)+3/(8*m^2)
       <=31/(16*m)<2/m.                              (35)

For EVERY pair0<epsilon<=1/2,kappa>0, one has C>1. Both branches of
T_sp in(1) are positive. Therefore, without any restriction kappa<1,

    A=epsilon+(C/epsilon)*[1+(1+kappa)*T_sp]
      >=C/epsilon>=2*C.                              (36)

This includes kappa=1 and every kappa>1. The source's piecewise T_sp
and threshold price remain unchanged.

If C>=r, alternative(I) applies immediately. Consider ANY remaining
pair with C<r. Since C>=sqrt(1+epsilon^2), equations(35) and m>=2 imply

    epsilon^2<=C^2-1<r^2-1
      <4/m+4/m^2<=6/m<=1/256.                       (37)

Thus epsilon<1/16. Using pi>3 and arctan(epsilon)<epsilon gives

    theta_sp=pi/2-arctan(epsilon)>23/16>1.            (38)

For completeness pi>3 follows by comparing the unit circle circumference
with its inscribed regular hexagon, and arctan(epsilon)<epsilon follows
by integrating1/(1+t^2)<1 for positive t. Neither uses a decimal value.

The SAME assumption C<r yields, directly from the formula for C,

    kappa*theta_sp<=log C<log r<r-1<2/m.              (39)

Since theta_sp>1 and m>=1536, this implies

    kappa<2/m<=1/768<1/16.

In particular kappa<1 has now been proved in precisely the branch
where the exponential expression for T_sp will be used. Put

    X=(1-kappa^2)*theta_sp/kappa.

From(38)--(39),

    X>((1-kappa^2)*theta_sp^2/2)*m
      >(255/256)*(529/256)*(m/2)
       =(134895/131072)*m>m.                         (40)

For0<kappa<1, the formula in(1) simplifies exactly to

    1+(1+kappa)*T_sp=(exp(X)-kappa)/(1-kappa)
                    >=exp(X).                       (41)

Indeed subtracting exp(X) gives
kappa*(exp(X)-1)/(1-kappa)>=0. Combining(37),(40),(41) yields

    A>=C*exp(X)/epsilon>16*C*exp(m).                  (42)

The positive exponential series gives exp(1)>1+1+1/2=5/2>c.
Together with(15),

    M_max<=c^(m-1)<exp(m),
    A>16*C*exp(m)>2*C*M_max.                         (43)

This is alternative(II). Hence every parameter pair is covered by
(I) or(II), and Sections4--6 prove(7)--(8).

This is a finite uniform argument: no limits or optimized constants
K=m*kappa and E=m*epsilon were assumed. It includes every horizon-
dependent scaling, oscillatory choices, fixed positive curvature, both
sides of kappa=epsilon, both sides of kappa=1, and epsilon=1/2. Within
the stated small-tau family there is no remaining parameter region.

The precise changes from the old-domain proof are now visible. Its
small-C branch used kappa<=epsilon<1/16 to bound1-kappa^2. Here(39)
forces kappa<1/16 directly. It also used its exponential formula for A
throughout the old domain. Here the universal bound(36) replaces that
use outside the small-C branch, and(39) independently justifies the
exponential branch wherever(41) is used. No ordering of kappa and epsilon
enters any deletion comparison. The new source-channel comparisons in
Section5 additionally pay its different numerator and terminal factor.

## 8. Original benchmark: saved endpoint, finite deficit, and exact limit

Only the following part of accepted fact04219554e41d9750 is used:
the original all-split definitions(9), the positive ray limit(10), and
the unconditional enclosure

    lambda^(-j)*B_(2^j-1)<=Z_1
      <=lambda^(-j)*(sqrt(B_(2^j))+3/100)^2, j>=0.     (44)

Its conditional four-step seed construction is not a premise. The
relevant statement and Sections7--8 of that accepted fact were read.
Its Section8 fixes the saved table
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA2562a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
The hash and saved rows1023,1024 were read back; neither the generator
nor any benchmark or exact-check program was executed.

With D80=1208925819614629174706176, its row1024 gives

    D80*B_1024<=H1024=19229705732238461978760749948
                    <15910*D80
                     =19234009790068750169575260160. (45)

The integer comparison is displayed to expose the exact saved input,
not as a newly computed benchmark. Since19600<19602, sqrt(2)>140/99.
The binomial identity gives

    lambda^10=3363+2378*sqrt(2)>6725,                 (46)

where(3363*99+2378*140)-6725*99=82>0. Apply(44) at j=10. Using(45),
(46), sqrt(15910)<127 and the written rational comparisons

    15910+381/50+9/10000<15918,
    15918*100=1591800<1593825=6725*237,

one obtains

    Z_1<(sqrt(15910)+3/100)^2/6725
        <15918/6725<237/100.                         (47)

This rederives the already available rational enclosure directly from
the accepted fact and its fixed endpoint. No unverified sibling's
derived value of Z_1 is used as an input.

Here is an explicit finite comparison, strengthening a limit-only
statement. For EVERY0<tau<=1/4 and m>=1536, equations(12),(35) imply

    v=beta/r>(1-1/(4*m))/(1+2/m)
             =1-[9/(4*m)]/(1+2/m)
             >1-9/(4*m)>255/256.                    (48)

For the last inequality,9/(4*m)<=3/2048<8/2048=1/256. Also lambda>2
gives lambda^(-m)<1/256. From(14), the exact normalized physical mass is

    (1+2*S_m)/lambda^m
       =2*v*(1+tau/m)^m+2*(1-v)*(2/lambda)^m
           -lambda^(-m).                            (49)

All terms in the second summand are nonnegative. At tau=1/4, the first
three nonnegative binomial terms and m>=2 give

    (1+1/(4*m))^m
      >=1+1/4+(m-1)/(32*m)>=81/64.

Therefore(48)--(49) imply

    (1+2*S_m)/lambda^m
      >2*(255/256)*(81/64)-1/256
       =20623/8192>5/2.                              (50)

The final comparison is20623>20480. Since(44) also gives
B_(n_m)/lambda^m<=Z_1, combining(8),(47),(50) proves

    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,1/4))<237/250
                      for EVERY integer m>=1536.    (51)

For the entire interval1/5<=tau<=1/4, the same binomial argument yields

    (1+tau/m)^m
      >=1+tau+[(m-1)/(2*m)]*tau^2
      >=1+1/5+1/100=121/100.

Hence, uniformly over that tau interval,

    (1+2*S_m)/lambda^m
      >2*(255/256)*(121/100)-1/256
       =6161/2560>12/5,                              (52)

since6161>6144. Equations(8),(44),(47),(52) give the second finite bound

    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))<79/80
       for EVERY1/5<=tau<=1/4 and integer m>=1536.    (53)

No new benchmark horizon or numerical tail estimate is needed for these
finite claims. The physical index remains exactly n_m=2^m-1.

For completeness, for a fixed0<tau<=1/4, beta and r both tend1 as
m increases, so v tends1. The binomial limit
(1+tau/m)^m->exp(tau) can be read directly from

    tau/(1+tau/m)<=m*log(1+tau/m)<=tau,

which follows by integrating1/(1+t) between0 and tau/m. In(49), the
second summand tends0 since0<1-v<1 and2/lambda<1; the last term also
tends0. Thus

    (1+2*S_m)/lambda^m ->2*exp(tau).                  (54)

Combine(8),(10),(54) to get(11). Strict limiting constants follow from
(47) and exp(1/4)>5/4, or exp(tau)>=exp(1/5)>6/5 for tau>=1/5.
These strict inequalities follow from positive higher terms of the
exponential series. Thus both the finite and limiting comparisons have
the stated strict margin.

## 9. Review, retrieval, and exact scope

The following stress tests were checked symbolically in this ONE joint
candidate, without mathematical computation or a separate verifier:

* Every original nonempty subset is addressed by its own minimum selected
  level; no record-chain optimality is assumed.
* A checkpoint can be first, last, sole, tied in level, or at level0.
  Initial and terminal blocks still contain the stated complete children.
* Removing a selection merges s+b+t and keeps the full physical b=1+d.
  Interior next-checkpoint costs remain their ACTUAL values until the
  proved monotonicity lowers them to the comparison threshold.
* Both interior differences were expanded with their own numerators.
  The source-format extra1 gives the negative terms visible in(26);
  it is not discarded as a negligible large-level error.
* The two terminal denominators remain separate. The source comparison
  has the quantitative factor5/8, while the mass comparison is(21)--(23).
* All costs are kept positive at empty children and empty ordinary blocks.
  Threshold equalities and the general-kappa normal price are retained.
* No interchange of a parameter supremum and a limit occurs. Finite
  inequalities(37)--(43) cover the entire enlarged parameter domain.
* The parameters of every child remain the enclosing(m,tau). All benchmark
  comparisons use the original physical length and all-split recurrence.

## 10. Final theorem of the joint candidate

THEOREM (UNVERIFIED analytical candidate). Let L_wide be exactly(1)--(5),
including the piecewise T_sp, the mass threshold B_sp with its full N_sp
price, and the separate source threshold2*(1+epsilon^(-2)). Let H_(m,tau)
be exactly(6), with unchanged enclosing(m,tau) in every child. For every
real0<tau<=1/4 and integer m>=1536, simultaneously for all kappa>0,
0<epsilon<=1/2 and all respective eligible nonempty chronological
checkpoint subsets,

    Phi_M<=1/(1+2*S_m),
    Phi_S<=5/[16*(1+S_m)],
    L_wide(H_(m,tau))=1/(1+2*S_m).

For the original all-split benchmark(9) at the actual physical length
n_m=2^m-1, every such m satisfies

    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,1/4))<237/250<1.

Uniformly for all1/5<=tau<=1/4 and the same m>=1536 one also has

    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))<79/80<1.

For each fixed0<tau<=1/4 the exact limit is

    lim_(m->infinity) B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))
                      =Z_1/(2*exp(tau)),

where Z_1 is the accepted positive benchmark ray limit from
fact04219554e41d9750. These are statements only about the defined scalar
guarantees. They do not upper-bound W, the geometric source's exact-radius
formula(6), or its second realization after closest-radius normalization.

PROOF. The same minimum-level selection deletion mechanism in
Sections3--6 establishes both bounds under(17), paying each channel's
own numerator and terminal factor. Section7 proves(17) for every allowed
parameter pair at every stated depth. The baseline attains the equality.
Section8 proves the finite benchmark deficits from the accepted endpoint
and the exact limiting identity. All new intermediate steps are contained
in this single joint unverified proof; no earlier draft is a premise.

## Glossary

L_wide: the new supremum(5), distinct from the unchanged old L_sp.
H_base: the explicit baseline1/(1+2*sum h).

Omega,G,C_n,B_n,U_n^ZJ,Z_1: the unchanged benchmark and accepted ray(9)--(10).
D80,H1024: the accepted table denominator and saved upper numerator(45).
W_n(h): the original normalized worst-case GD error, namely the supremum
of2*(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2) over finite-dimensional convex
differentiable L-smooth f with a minimizer x_star and x_0!=x_star, under
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)*grad f(x_i). It is used only to delimit scope here.
